307 — Tensor Compiler + Runtime

Goal: Implement, from scratch in Rust, a TVM-style deep-learning tensor compiler and its execution runtime — a high-level Graph IR with shape/type inference, graph optimization passes (const fold, CSE, DCE, algebraic simplify, layout), dominator-based operator fusion, a low-level tensor/loop IR (TIR), a Halide-style schedule space (tile, reorder, vectorize, unroll, parallel, compute_at, inline), a CPU codegen backend (C source + portable SIMD) producing a compiled Module, and a runtime — strided zero-copy NDArrays, a liveness-based memory planner, a graph executor for static graphs and a bytecode VM for dynamic control flow — with an auto-tuner. Numerically validated against a reference oracle within tolerance. It is a compiler + runtime for tensor programs, not a training/autograd framework.

Project Number: 307

Project Name: Tensor Compiler + Runtime

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

Build a from-scratch, TVM-style deep-learning tensor compiler + execution runtime in Rust: import a model → Graph IR → graph passes → operator fusion → tensor/loop IR (TIR) → schedule → CPU kernel codegen → Module → runtime (GraphExecutor) / bytecode VM (Interpreter), with every compiled result numerically validated against a NumPy-style reference oracle.

1.1 Precise engineering definition

A two-IR ahead-of-time compiler and its runtime for whole-tensor programs. Pipeline (LOCKED stages):

model (ONNX-subset / textual graph)
  -> tc-graph  : Graph IR        (functional dataflow: GraphNode/GraphOp, TensorType/Shape/DType)
  -> tc-pass   : shape infer -> const fold -> CSE -> DCE -> simplify -> layout
  -> tc-fuse   : FusionGroups     (dominator fusion -> fused subgraphs)
  -> tc-ir     : PrimFunc/Stmt/Expr/Buffer/IterVar   (imperative loop-nest TIR)
  -> tc-schedule: split/reorder/tile/vectorize/unroll/parallel/compute_at/inline -> Schedule
  -> tc-codegen: CBackend / SimdBackend -> Kernel -> Module{ kernels, graph, MemoryPlan }
  -> tc-runtime: GraphExecutor (static)  |  tc-vm: Interpreter (dynamic)
  -> output tensors  (validated vs reference oracle, f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6)

Core ideas (LOCKED, kept consistent across all phases):

Principle Statement
Two-level IR (TVM) Graph IR = whole-tensor dataflow; TIR = loop-nests over Buffers. Lowering is per fused group.
Compute/schedule split (Halide) TensorExpr = what; Schedule = how (loop order, tiling, SIMD, threads). Schedule changes alter performance only, never numerics.
Shape inference TensorType propagated to fixed point; Dim::Dynamic symbolic, resolved at runtime by the VM.
Fusion = dominant CPU win Dominator-tree analysis groups injective/elementwise chains + a single reduce into one kernel; eliminates intermediate materialization.
Memory planning MemoryPlan = buffer liveness over topo order → storage reuse (register-allocation-on-tensors).
Codegen contract One C function per Kernel with stable KernelSig (ptrs + shapes + strides); vectorize→SIMD lanes, parallel→thread-pool tiles.
Static vs dynamic GraphExecutor = static shapes; tc-vm Interpreter = control flow + dynamic shapes, dispatching the same compiled Kernels.
Oracle Compiled output must match reference within documented tolerance; schedule rewrites must not exceed it.

1.2 System boundaries

In scope: model import (ONNX-subset + textual graph), graph-level optimization, fusion, lowering, a usable schedule space, scalar + portable-SIMD CPU codegen, a static graph executor, a dynamic bytecode VM, memory planning, an analytical+measured auto-tuner, and numerical conformance.

Out of scope (NON-goals — state where relevant):

Non-goal Owner / disposition
Autograd / backprop project #112 — this is forward inference only; no gradient construction.
Training loops / optimizers project #114 — no SGD/Adam, no parameter updates.
GPU / CUDA codegen projects #248 / #180; CPU-only here. Scope::Shared, IterKind::Thread, Device::Gpu exist as stubs gated PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §1.2").
Distributed execution project #351 — single-process, single-host.
Full ONNX op set a documented subset only (see Phase 1); unknown ops error explicitly.
Python frontend replaced by a textual graph format + an ONNX-subset importer; no pybind/CPython embedding.

1.3 Deployment model

  • tcc CLI binary (crate tc-cli): subcommands compile (model → Module), run (execute on inputs), dump (graph/tir/scheduled/c/module), tune (schedule search), check (vs oracle), bench.
  • Embeddable crate set: the nine tc-* crates link into a host Rust application; feed a model + input NDArrays, get a compiled Module + output NDArrays. no_std + alloc for the IR core crates (tc-graph, tc-ir); std for runtime/codegen/CLI.
  • Artifact: Module { kernels: Vec<Kernel>, graph: Graph, plan: MemoryPlan } — serializable, loadable by GraphExecutor or tc-vm.

1.4 Target users

ML-systems and compiler-infrastructure engineers building/extending tensor compilers; readers expected to reason about IR design, fusion, scheduling, and the codegen↔runtime ABI — not framework end-users.

1.5 Phase-0 deliverable — the nine tc-* crates

  1. tc-graph — Graph IR: Graph, GraphNode, GraphOp, TensorType, Shape, DType, op registry, importer entry points (no_std + alloc).
  2. tc-ir — tensor/loop IR (TIR): PrimFunc, Stmt, Expr, Buffer, IterVar, TensorExpr, IR builder (no_std + alloc).
  3. tc-pass — PassManager, Pass; analysis + transform passes (shape inference, const fold, CSE, DCE, algebraic simplify, layout). Dep: tc-graph, tc-ir.
  4. tc-fuse — FusionPass, FusionGroup, fusion rules + dominator analysis → fused subgraphs lowered to PrimFunc. Dep: tc-graph, tc-ir.
  5. tc-schedule — Schedule, Stage, primitives (split/reorder/fuse/tile/vectorize/unroll/parallel/compute_at/inline). Dep: tc-ir.
  6. tc-codegen — Target, CodeGen, CBackend, SimdBackend, Kernel, Module. Dep: tc-ir, tc-schedule.
  7. tc-runtime — NDArray, Storage, Device, Allocator, MemoryPlan, GraphExecutor, op dispatch, async scheduling. Dep: tc-codegen.
  8. tc-vm — VmInstruction, VmFunc, Interpreter, Closure, register/object model. Dep: tc-runtime.
  9. tc-cli — the tcc binary (subcommands above). Dep: all.

Phase-0 smoke (vertical slice): build a 2-node add(a,b) Graph, lower a hand-written PrimFunc, emit a C Kernel, compile+run via tc-runtime, validate vs an in-Rust reference add. Proves the full crate spine end-to-end before any optimization.

1.6 What later phases unlock

Phases Unlock
1 (§13) Real models in: typed Graph IR, op registry, ONNX-subset + textual importers.
2–3 (§14–15) Correct + minimized graphs: shape/type inference + verifier; const fold, CSE, DCE, algebraic simplify.
4–5 (§16–17) Fewer/cheaper kernels: dominator fusion; layout transform + canonicalization.
6–8 (§18–20) Tunable performance: TIR lowering; schedule primitives part 1 (split/reorder/fuse/tile/compute_at/inline) + part 2 (vectorize/unroll/parallel).
9 (§21) Native speed: C + portable-SIMD codegen, cc-compiled Kernels in a Module.
10–11 (§22–23) Execution: GraphExecutor + liveness MemoryPlan; bytecode VM with control flow + dynamic shapes + async dispatch.
12 (§24) Automatic schedules: search space + cost model (analytical + measured) + per-kernel tuner.
13 (§25) Trust: oracle conformance, differential testing, fuzzing, GFLOP/s benchmarks vs naive baseline.

1.7 Deferred features (gated PhaseDeferred)

Deferred Gate Notes
GPU/CUDA backend PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §1.2") Device::Gpu, Scope::Shared/Local, IterKind::Thread are typed stubs; no codegen.
Full ONNX op set PhaseDeferred (Phase 1) beyond the documented subset; importer errors on unknown ops.
Distributed execution PhaseDeferred (→ #351) single-host only.
Auto-tuner depth PhaseDeferred (Phase 12) Ansor-style learned cost models / evolutionary search beyond the baseline analytical+measured tuner.
LLVM codegen path feature-gated default = C source + portable SIMD; LLVM emission behind a cargo feature.

1.8 Timeline

Milestone Estimate
Phase 0 (workspace + vertical slice) 6–14 weeks
Full pipeline (Phases 1–13) several months

Single principal-level owner; phases are largely sequential (each depends on the prior IR layer) but 12 (tuning) and 13 (conformance) overlap once codegen + runtime land.

1.9 Recommended language — Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78)

Driver Rationale
Multi-IR correctness sum types + exhaustive match over GraphOp/Stmt/Expr make IR node handling and pass safety compiler-checked.
Arena IR Arena<GraphNode> + typed ids (NodeId, BufferId, VarId) avoid the lifetime tangle of a mutable graph compiler.
Runtime control zero-copy strided NDArray views, explicit Storage/Allocator/MemoryPlan, no GC perturbing the hot path.
Reference fidelity TVM's Relay/TIR split (C++) maps cleanly to tc-graph/tc-ir.

Rejected: C/C++ (memory-unsafe over a large mutable IR + buffer model); Python (too slow for the runtime hot path; fit only as a thin frontend, which is a non-goal); Go (weaker sum types, GC perturbs runtime timing).

Codegen target: C source compiled via the system cc + portable SIMD by default; an LLVM emission path behind a feature flag.

1.10 Prerequisites

Compiler IR design (multi-level dialects, pass managers — cf. MLIR); the Halide compute/schedule separation; the TVM Relay/TIR two-IR model + te schedule + graph executor / Relay VM; loop optimization (tiling, reorder, split/fuse, compute_at, inline); vectorization + SIMD intrinsics; thread-pool parallelism; memory planning / liveness-based buffer reuse; dominator-tree analysis; numerical tolerance reasoning (rtol/atol, fp accumulation order). References cited consistently: Apache TVM (Relay, TIR, te, graph executor + Relay VM, AutoTVM/Ansor), XLA (HLO fusion/layout), Halide (compute/schedule), MLIR (dialect + pass manager), ONNX (import format).

2. Why Build This

A tensor compiler is the single densest concentration of "systems engineering that matters for ML" in one codebase: a multi-IR optimizing compiler, a code generator, and a memory-managed runtime, all gated by a numerical-correctness oracle. Building one from scratch forces mastery of the exact mechanisms (the two-level IR, compute/schedule separation, fusion, tiling, liveness planning) that XLA/TVM/Halide/MLIR rest on — the layer between a model and the silicon that decides whether inference is fast or unusable.

2.1 Unique systems knowledge earned

Concept What it forces you to internalize Where (phase / crate)
Two-level IR (TVM model) functional dataflow graph (whole-tensor ops) vs imperative loop-nest IR (Buffers) — different invariants per level; lowering bridges them per fused group tc-graph / tc-ir; ## 13, ## 18
Compute/schedule separation (Halide) TensorExpr = WHAT; Schedule = HOW; transforms must preserve numerics, change only perf tc-schedule; ## 19–20
Operator fusion = dominant CPU win eliminating intermediate materialization (memory traffic), not raw FLOP reduction, is the lever; fused elementwise+reduce kernels stay in cache tc-fuse; ## 16
Dominator-based fusion analysis a graph problem (group an injective chain + single reduce under a dominator) cleanly maps to compiler dominator-tree machinery tc-fuse; ## 16
Loop tiling + vectorize + parallel cache-blocking for the L1/L2 hierarchy, SIMD lane mapping, thread-pool over tiles — the three knobs that move % of peak tc-schedule + tc-codegen; ## 20–21
Liveness-based memory planning "register allocation on tensors": buffer liveness over topological order, storage reuse for non-overlapping tensors MemoryPlan; ## 22
GraphExecutor vs bytecode VM static-shape topological dispatch vs dynamic control flow / variable shapes via a register VM dispatching the same compiled Kernels tc-runtime / tc-vm; ## 22–23
Numerical-correctness oracle every transform validated vs a NumPy-style reference within documented tolerance (f32 rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6); schedule changes must not exceed it ## 12, ## 25
Strided zero-copy NDArray shape/strides/offset view model: reshape/slice/transpose without copying; aliasing discipline tc-runtime; ## 22
Auto-tuning / schedule search a search space over schedule primitives + cost model (analytical + measured) → the idea (AutoTVM/Ansor) that reframed perf engineering as search ## 24

2.2 Why tensor compilers matter — historically + architecturally

  • The deployment backbone. Modern ML inference does not run interpreted op-by-op; it runs compiled. TVM (Relay/TIR/te-schedule, graph executor + Relay VM), XLA (HLO + fusion/layout), Halide (compute/schedule), and MLIR (dialects + pass manager) are the production layer between framework and hardware. This project rebuilds that layer in miniature.
  • The schedule-search inflection. Halide's compute/schedule split → AutoTVM/Ansor's automatic schedule search reframed kernel performance engineering from hand-written assembly to search over a transformation space. Implementing the space + cost model (## 24) makes that shift concrete rather than folklore.
  • Fusion is the architecture, not an optimization. XLA and TVM both treat fusion as a first-class structural decision: it sets kernel boundaries, hence memory traffic, hence achievable throughput on memory-bound DL workloads. Understanding why fusion dominates on CPU (bandwidth, not FLOPs) is the central systems insight.
  • IR design as the durable skill. The Relay/TIR split, the MLIR pass-manager pattern, and ONNX as an import boundary are reusable templates: any future "lower a high-level spec to fast machine work" problem (query planners, DSP graphs, shader pipelines) is the same shape.

2.3 Engineering muscles exercised

  • Multi-pass compiler design — PassManager/Pass, analysis vs transform passes, fixed-point shape inference, a verifier asserting every node is typed (## 14–15).
  • IR lowering — Graph IR → fused PrimFunc → scheduled TIR; maintaining a stable contract across three representations (## 16, ## 18, ## 19).
  • Code generation — emitting a C function per Kernel with a stable KernelSig (pointers + shapes + strides), a portable-SIMD path, compiling via the system cc into a Module (## 21).
  • Runtime memory management — Storage/Allocator, liveness-based reuse, zero-copy strided views, async op dispatch without a GC (## 22–23).
  • Graph algorithms — dominator trees (fusion), topological scheduling (executor), CSE on a dataflow DAG (## 15–16).
  • Numerical rigor — tolerance discipline, differential testing, fuzzing graph + schedule generators that must never crash or drift numerically (## 25).

2.4 Cross-links to the portfolio

Project Relationship
249 Tensor Compiler sibling / lineage; this is the canonical full Graph-IR→TIR→codegen→runtime build
248 CUDA Fusion GPU codegen + fusion is the deferred (PhaseDeferred) GPU continuation of tc-fuse + tc-codegen
112 Autograd NON-GOAL here; supplies the backward graph that this compiler would then optimize
30 Compiler shared core: lexing→IR→passes→codegen; here specialized to tensor/loop IR
143 SSA Optimizer the analysis/transform-pass discipline (DCE, CSE, simplify) reused on a dataflow IR
147 Vectorizer the vectorize primitive (## 20) + SimdBackend lane mapping draw directly on it
351 Distributed Tensor Runtime NON-GOAL here; consumes this single-node runtime as its execution unit
349 BLAS the perf baseline/oracle for MatMul/Conv kernels; % of BLAS is a stated target

2.5 Calibration value

  • Forces the full stack in one artifact. Few projects make you hold a frontend importer, a 3-level IR, a pass manager, an optimizing fusion engine, a code generator, and a memory-managed runtime + VM coherent under one correctness contract. Completing it calibrates against principal-level ML-systems work.
  • Quantified bar. Targets are concrete and verifiable: numerical match within f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6 on an op + model corpus; measurable GFLOP/s and % of peak / % of BLAS vs a naive baseline; fusion-driven reduction in intermediate buffer bytes; bounded compile time per fused kernel.
  • Transferable, not throwaway. The skills (IR lowering, dominator analysis, liveness allocation, codegen, schedule search) are the same ones used in any production compiler-infrastructure or ML-runtime role — this is a portfolio centerpiece, not a toy.

What Mastery Looks Like

  • Phase 1 (tc-graph): Can construct a Graph IR from an ONNX-subset or textual description by hand; can trace exactly why a node's GraphOp variant maps to the shape rule it does; can explain the arena+typed-id design choice versus Rc<RefCell<>> under adversarial questioning.
  • Phase 2–3 (passes): Can trace a shape-inference fixed-point convergence failure to the exact per-op type rule that stalled it; can identify whether DCE/CSE/const-fold are transform passes or analysis passes and explain the ordering constraint between them and the verifier.
  • Phase 4 (tc-fuse): Can draw the dominator tree for a given dataflow DAG and annotate which nodes FusionPass groups together; can state the precise condition under which a reduce node can be added to a fusion group without forming an invalid schedule.
  • Phases 7–8 (tc-schedule): Given a 3-nested loop, can specify the split/tile/reorder sequence that tiles for L1, vectorizes the innermost axis, and parallelizes the outermost — and predict the resulting loop-nest ordering in TIR.
  • Phase 9 (tc-codegen): Can explain the KernelSig ABI contract (pointer + shape + stride convention), state why the C backend deliberately does not rely on compiler autovectorization for correctness, and describe what SimdBackend emits differently.
  • Phase 10 (MemoryPlan + GraphExecutor): Can manually compute the liveness intervals for a 5-node topological graph and identify which buffers can alias storage; can explain what would break if MemoryPlan is recomputed at runtime instead of ahead-of-time.
  • Phase 11 (tc-vm): Can explain why the bytecode interpreter dispatches the same compiled Kernels as GraphExecutor yet handles dynamic shapes — what the register file does at runtime that the static executor cannot.
  • Phase 12 (auto-tuner): Can articulate why a purely analytical cost model fails for novel hardware; can describe the search space for a 2-D tiling problem and estimate the number of configs before measurement-driven pruning.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 System Diagram + Trust / Concurrency / Persistence Boundaries

                 ┌──────────────────────────────────────── TRUST BOUNDARY ────────────────────────────┐
                 │  UNTRUSTED: model bytes (ONNX-subset / textual graph) + UNTRUSTED: input tensors    │
                 └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    model (ONNX-subset / textual graph)        [untrusted; size-bounded, op-allowlisted, shape-validated]
            |  import  (tc-graph::import)
            v
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ tc-graph : Graph IR                                    │   GraphNode/GraphOp, TensorType/Shape/DType
    │   functional dataflow graph of whole-tensor ops        │   Arena<GraphNode>, NodeId, op registry
    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
            |  tc-pass: shape infer -> type check -> const fold -> CSE -> DCE -> simplify -> layout
            v   (PassManager drives ordered Pass list to fixed point; verifier: every node typed)
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ tc-fuse : FusionGroups                                 │   FusionPass, dominator analysis
    │   dominator-based grouping -> fused subgraphs          │   FusionGroup -> 1 PrimFunc each
    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
            |  lower each group  (tc-fuse -> tc-ir builder)
            v
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ tc-ir : PrimFunc / Stmt / Expr / Buffer / IterVar      │   imperative loop-nest IR over Buffers
    │   TensorExpr (WHAT) + naive nested-loop body           │   no_std + alloc
    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
            |  tc-schedule: split/reorder/fuse/tile/vectorize/unroll/parallel/compute_at/inline
            v   (HOW; numeric-result-preserving by construction)
    ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Schedule (scheduled TIR)  { func: PrimFunc, stages }   │
    └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
            |  tc-codegen: CBackend (C source) / SimdBackend (portable SIMD) -> Kernel
            v
    ┌─────────────────────── PERSISTENCE BOUNDARY ──────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ Module { kernels: Vec<Kernel>, graph: Graph, plan: MemoryPlan }                    │
    │   = the serialized compiled artifact; weights/params persist embedded with it      │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
            |  load
            v
    ┌─────────────────────── CONCURRENCY BOUNDARY ──────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  compile path: SINGLE-THREADED over the mutable IR (no shared-IR data races)       │
    │  ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────  │
    │  run path: tc-runtime GraphExecutor (static)  |  tc-vm Interpreter (dynamic)       │
    │    PARALLEL kernels over a thread pool (parallel axis -> tiles); async dispatch;    │
    │    NDArray Storage is REF-COUNTED (shared zero-copy views, atomic refcount)         │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
            |
            v
        output tensors  --->  validated vs the NumPy-style reference oracle (rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6 f32)

Boundary summary:

Boundary Edge Rule
Trust model bytes + input tensors Untrusted. Importer enforces op-allowlist (ONNX-subset), size caps, no unbounded recursion; shape inference rejects ill-typed graphs before any codegen. cc-invoked C source is generated by us, never from the model.
Concurrency compile vs run Compilation single-threaded over Arena-backed IR (typed ids, no aliasing). Runtime parallelizes kernel execution (thread pool, parallel axis → tiles) + async op dispatch; Storage refcount is atomic so zero-copy NDArray views cross threads safely.
Persistence Module The compiled Module (kernels + graph + MemoryPlan) is the on-disk artifact; constant weights (GraphOp::Const(TensorData)) persist embedded; loading rehydrates without recompilation.

3.2 Per-Box Responsibilities

tc-graph — high-level Graph IR (Relay-like)

Responsibility: the functional dataflow graph of whole-tensor operators and the import surface. Owns Graph { nodes: Arena<GraphNode>, outputs, inputs }, GraphNode { op, args: Vec<NodeId>, ty }, the GraphOp enum (Input/Const/Add/Mul/MatMul/Conv2d/Relu/Reshape/Transpose/Reduce/Call), TensorType { shape, dtype }, Shape(Vec<Dim>) with Dim::Static|Dynamic, DType, the extensible op registry, and the two importer entry points (textual graph format + ONNX-subset). no_std + alloc. Input: untrusted model bytes. Output: a typed-once-inferred Graph. Key invariant: nodes are SSA-like dataflow values referenced by NodeId into the arena — no cycles, single-assignment, args reference only earlier-defined nodes; ty is None until shape inference fills it. Later phases: Phase 1 builds the IR + importers; every downstream crate consumes Graph; the final Module carries the post-pass Graph for the executor.

tc-pass — pass infrastructure + graph passes

Responsibility: PassManager + Pass trait driving an ordered sequence of analysis and transform passes. Owns shape inference, type checking, constant folding, CSE, DCE, algebraic simplification (canonicalization), layout transform, and the verifier. Depends on tc-graph + tc-ir. Input: a Graph (possibly untyped). Output: a typed, optimized, canonical Graph. Key invariant: shape inference runs to a FIXED POINT propagating TensorType forward; the verifier guarantees every reachable node is typed before fusion; transform passes preserve graph semantics (numerically equivalent dataflow). Pass ordering is fixed and documented (Phase 2 establishes infra + inference; Phase 3 the optimization set; Phase 5 layout). Later phases: tc-fuse and tc-codegen assume a fully typed, simplified graph; dynamic dims (Dim::Dynamic) survive as symbolic, resolved at runtime by tc-vm.

tc-fuse — operator fusion

Responsibility: FusionPass + FusionGroup + the dominator-based fusion analysis. Groups an injective/elementwise chain plus at most one reduce into a single kernel, eliminating intermediate tensor materialization (the dominant CPU win). Depends on tc-graph + tc-ir. Input: typed optimized Graph. Output: a set of FusionGroups, each lowered to one PrimFunc. Key invariant: a fusion group has a single dominator boundary — every internal value is consumed only within the group except the group output; fusion never crosses a non-injective multi-consumer boundary that would force recompute beyond the cost threshold. Later phases: Phase 4 implements it; Phase 6 lowers each group; the executor dispatches one kernel per group.

tc-ir — low-level tensor/loop IR (TIR-like)

Responsibility: the imperative loop-nest IR. Owns PrimFunc { params: Vec<Buffer>, body: Stmt }, Stmt (For/Store/Block/IfThenElse/Allocate), Expr (Load/Var/Const/Bin/Call), Buffer { id, shape: Vec<Expr>, dtype, scope }, IterVar { var, kind } (Data/Reduce/Thread-deferred), Scope (Global/Local/Shared-deferred), TensorExpr, and the IR builder. no_std + alloc. Input: a FusionGroup. Output: a default naive nested-loop PrimFunc realizing the group's TensorExpr. Key invariant: the TensorExpr defines WHAT is computed and is the immutable semantic anchor; the loop nest is a faithful realization; buffer indices are affine in IterVars. Later phases: Phase 6 builds it; tc-schedule transforms the loop structure over it without touching the TensorExpr; tc-codegen emits from it.

tc-schedule — the schedule space

Responsibility: Schedule { func: PrimFunc, stages: Vec<Stage> }, Stage, and the primitives split/reorder/fuse/tile/vectorize/unroll/parallel/compute_at/inline (signatures over AxisId/StageId). Depends on tc-ir. Input: a PrimFunc. Output: a Schedule (scheduled TIR) with transformed loop nests. Key invariant (compute/schedule separation, Halide model): applying ANY schedule primitive MUST NOT change the numerical result — only loop order, tiling, lane assignment, and parallelism. split(ax,factor) -> (outer,inner), fuse(a,b) -> axis, tile = split×2 + reorder. Later phases: Phase 7 (split/reorder/fuse/tile/compute_at/inline), Phase 8 (vectorize/unroll/parallel + buffer-scope interaction), Phase 12 (auto-tuner searches this space).

tc-codegen — lowering scheduled TIR to target code

Responsibility: Target { arch, features }, the CodeGen trait (fn emit(&self, sched, target) -> Kernel), the CBackend (portable C source emission), the SimdBackend (portable SIMD path), Kernel { name, source, signature: KernelSig }, and Module { kernels, graph, plan }. Depends on tc-ir + tc-schedule. Input: a Schedule + Target. Output: a Kernel per fused group, packaged into a Module. Key invariant: each Kernel exposes a stable KernelSig (data pointers + shapes + strides) so the runtime binds NDArrays positionally; vectorize maps an axis to SIMD lanes, parallel maps an axis to thread-pool tiles. Emitted C is an OUTPUT artifact compiled via the system cc. Example emitted signature:

void k_fused_add_relu(const float* a, const float* b, float* out,
                      int64_t n0, int64_t s_a0, int64_t s_b0, int64_t s_out0);

Later phases: Phase 9 builds both backends + cc integration; tc-runtime loads the Module; Phase 13 validates emitted kernels vs the oracle.

tc-runtime — the runtime (static path)

Responsibility: NDArray { storage, shape, strides, dtype, offset } (strided, zero-copy slices/reshape), Storage { ptr, bytes, device } (ref-counted), Device (Cpu/Gpu-deferred), Allocator trait (alloc/free), MemoryPlan (liveness-based buffer reuse), and GraphExecutor (topological op dispatch over a Module + NDArray bindings) plus async dispatch. Depends on tc-codegen. Input: a Module + input NDArrays (untrusted, shape-validated against the graph). Output: result NDArrays. Key invariants: NDArray views never copy unless materialization is required; Storage refcount governs lifetime; MemoryPlan reuses one buffer for two tensors only when their liveness intervals are disjoint over the executor's topological order (register-allocation-on-tensors). GraphExecutor requires STATIC shapes. Later phases: Phase 10 builds it; Phase 12 measures kernels through it; Phase 13 benchmarks GFLOP/s.

tc-vm — the bytecode VM (dynamic path)

Responsibility: VmInstruction (LoadConst/AllocTensor/InvokeKernel/If/Goto/Call/Ret), VmFunc { instrs, num_regs }, the Interpreter (register file of NDArray/objects + call stack), Closure, and the register/object model. Depends on tc-runtime. Input: a Module whose graph has control flow / dynamic dims + runtime inputs. Output: result NDArrays. Key invariant: the VM dispatches the SAME compiled Kernels as the GraphExecutor — it differs only by allocating at runtime (AllocTensor resolves Dim::Dynamic) and branching (If/Goto/Call). Numerical results must match the static path where both apply. Later phases: Phase 11 builds it; handles models the GraphExecutor's static assumption rejects.

tc-cli — the tcc binary

Responsibility: the user-facing driver. Subcommands: compile (model → Module), run (execute on inputs), dump (graph/tir/scheduled/c/module), tune (schedule search), check (vs oracle), bench. Depends on all crates. Input: CLI args + model + input files. Output: Module files, result tensors, dump text, tuning logs, pass/fail + benchmark reports. Key invariant: each subcommand is a thin orchestration over the library crates — no compiler logic lives in the binary; dump exposes every IR stage for inspection/conformance. Later phases: present from Phase 0 (smoke) and extended each phase as new capabilities land; check/bench drive Phase 13 conformance + performance gates.

4. System Constraints

Hard, testable limits the compiler + runtime must satisfy. Every numeric target is a CI gate or a documented assumption; violations are bugs. Constraints below bind all nine tc-* crates and the tcc binary unless scoped otherwise.

4.1 Correctness & Numerical Tolerance (the oracle contract)

  • Oracle = a NumPy-style reference (Phase 13) computing each GraphOp in f64 then casting. Compiled Module output MUST match within tolerance per DType.
  • Schedule invariance (LOCKED): changing a Schedule (split/reorder/tile/vectorize/unroll/parallel/compute_at/inline) MUST NOT change results beyond the float-reassociation band below. Compute defines WHAT; schedule defines HOW.
  • Reduction reassociation (tiling a reduce axis, parallel-sum) is the ONLY sanctioned source of result drift; bounded by the reassociation band, never exceeding op tolerance.
Aspect DType rtol atol Notes
Elementwise (Add/Mul/Relu) F32 1e-5 1e-6 bit-exact for Relu/Reshape/Transpose (no FP op)
Elementwise F64 1e-12 1e-14
MatMul / Reduce F32 1e-4 1e-5 looser: accumulation order, FMA contraction
Conv2d F32 1e-4 1e-5 im2col/direct must agree within band
Integer ops (I8/I32/I64) int 0 0 bit-exact, overflow = documented wrap
Bool ops Bool — — bit-exact
Schedule-reassoc band (max ulp drift any schedule vs naive) F32 2e-5 2e-6 sub-tolerance; CI compares schedule pairs
  • Comparison rule: pass ⟺ |c − o| ≤ atol + rtol·|o| elementwise. NaN matches NaN; ±Inf must match sign. Mismatch → tcc check exits nonzero with first-diff index + values.

4.2 Compile-Time Budgets

Stage Target Measured by
Cold full compile, small model (≤50 nodes, e.g. 3-layer MLP) < 2 s wall tcc compile end-to-end incl. cc
Graph passes (shape→fold→CSE→DCE→simplify→layout) < 50 ms / 100 nodes tc-pass timer
Fusion (tc-fuse dominator analysis) < 20 ms / 100 nodes
Lowering one FusionGroup → PrimFunc < 5 ms tc-ir builder
Per-kernel codegen (CBackend/SimdBackend → Kernel.source) < 10 ms tc-codegen
Per-kernel cc compile (-O2 -march=native) < 400 ms each external cc
Warm recompile (codegen cache hit on unchanged group) < 100 ms total hash(PrimFunc+Schedule+Target) cache
tune per fused kernel (Phase 12, default trial cap) ≤ 200 trials / ≤ 30 s bounded, interruptible
  • cc invocation is the dominant cost; codegen MUST emit one C TU per Kernel to parallelize compilation across cores (compile time scales ≈ kernels / nproc).

4.3 Kernel Performance Targets (dev machine: x86_64, AVX2, 8 cores, ~50 GB/s DRAM BW)

Kernel class Target Rationale
Fused elementwise (vectorized) ≥ 80% of measured DRAM bandwidth memory-bandwidth-bound; FLOPs trivial
Fused elementwise vs unfused ≥ N× fewer DRAM passes (N = chain length) fusion kills intermediate materialization
MatMul, naive triple-loop (Phase 6 baseline) reference 1× the documented baseline
MatMul, tiled + vectorized + parallel (Phase 8/9) ≥ 10× naive baseline; ≥ 30% of OpenBLAS sgemm (where compared) tiling for L1/L2 + AVX2 FMA + threads
Single-thread vectorized f32 GEMM microkernel ≥ 8 GFLOP/s ~half AVX2 FMA peak per core
Conv2d (fused, scheduled) ≥ 5× naive direct conv
  • Vectorize maps a vectorize-marked axis to SIMD lanes (AVX2 = 8×f32); requires that axis stride = 1 in the innermost Buffer (Scope::Global/Local). parallel maps an outer axis to a thread pool over tiles; speedup target ≥ 0.7·nproc on compute-bound kernels.
  • BLAS comparison is informational only (no BLAS dependency shipped); printed by tcc bench.

4.4 Memory Constraints

Constraint Target
MemoryPlan peak-buffer reduction (liveness reuse) vs no-reuse ≥ 40% on the MLP/conv corpus; ≥ 2× on deep chains
NDArray slice / reshape / transpose zero-copy strided view (no alloc, no memcpy)
Storage overhead per buffer one ref-counted header; payload bytes exact, aligned to 64 B (SIMD)
Allocator fragmentation (arena/pool) < 10% slack vs sum of live bytes
Compiler peak RSS, small model < 256 MB
Runtime peak RSS ≈ planned peak buffers + weights + O(1) executor state
  • MemoryPlan = buffer liveness over GraphExecutor topological order; non-overlapping tensors share Storage (tensor register allocation). Aliased buffers MUST NOT overlap in live ranges — verified by a liveness assertion, else hard error.
  • NDArray invariant: offset + Σ (shape[i]−1)·strides[i] bytes ≤ storage.bytes; any view escaping this → reject at construction.

4.5 Dispatch / Runtime Overhead

Path Target
GraphExecutor per-op dispatch overhead (excl. kernel work) < 1 µs/op (lookup + bind args + call)
tc-vm Interpreter per-instruction overhead < 2 µs/instr (incl. dynamic-shape AllocTensor)
Module load (kernels already compiled, dlopen/link) < 50 ms
Cold runtime startup (process → first op) < 100 ms
Async dispatch queue enqueue O(1), lock-free fast path
  • Static graphs use GraphExecutor; dynamic control flow / Dim::Dynamic shapes use the tc-vm Interpreter, which allocates at runtime and dispatches the same compiled Kernels. No kernel duplication between executor and VM.

4.6 Scale Assumptions

Dimension Assumed max Behavior past max
Graph nodes 10⁴ passes stay sub-quadratic; > 10⁴ = warn, still correct
FusionGroup size ≤ 256 ops larger groups split (codegen TU bloat / register pressure)
Loop-nest depth per PrimFunc ≤ 8 deeper rejected by tc-ir verifier
Tensor rank ≤ 8 dims matches NDArray strided-view limit
Schedule axes per Stage ≤ 32 tuner search space bound
Kernels per Module ≤ 4096

4.7 Determinism

  • Definition: same model + same inputs + same Schedule + same Target ⇒ bit-identical outputs across runs and machines of the same arch, modulo documented float reassociation (§4.1) which is itself deterministic for a fixed schedule.
  • No nondeterministic threading effects: parallel reductions use a fixed tree-reduction order (not first-come accumulation), so multi-thread results are reproducible.
  • No RNG, no clock, no address-dependent behavior in kernels. tune (Phase 12) uses a seeded PRNG; the seed is recorded in the Module so a tuned compile is reproducible.

4.8 Recovery & Robustness (no-crash contract)

  • A malformed model (bad ONNX-subset op, rank mismatch, cyclic dataflow, unknown GraphOp, shape that cannot unify) is rejected at import / shape-inference with a diagnostic (node id + reason); the process MUST NOT panic, segfault, or produce a partial Module.
  • Fuzzing gate (Phase 13): random graph + random schedule generators run ≥ 10⁶ iterations with zero crashes/UB (ASan/Miri clean) and zero numerical-tolerance violations.
  • Every unsafe block (NDArray/Storage pointer math, FFI to compiled kernels) carries a documented invariant; bounds are checked at view-construction time so kernel hot loops stay unchecked but provably in-bounds.
  • Codegen failure (cc error) surfaces the emitted Kernel.source path + compiler stderr; never silently degrades.

4.9 Platform & Build Constraints

  • tc-graph and tc-ir are no_std + alloc (embeddable IR core); higher crates use std.
  • Rust edition 2021, MSRV 1.78. No GPU code paths active (Device::Gpu, Scope::Shared, IterKind::Thread are PhaseDeferred stubs — must compile, must not execute).
  • External toolchain: a C compiler (cc/clang) on PATH for the CBackend default path; optional LLVM path behind a feature flag (not default). Build fails fast with a clear message if cc is absent.
  • Non-goals enforced by absence: no autograd, no training loop, no full ONNX op set, no Python frontend, no distributed runtime.

Curated, non-negotiable. Each entry: what to read, why for THIS project, what to extract. Read before the phase that depends on it; re-read TVM/Halide every IR/schedule phase. Every entry names the resolving §/phase where its idea is applied (below).
