Illustrative emitted-kernel ABI (output artifact, not source to copy) — the stable KernelSig shape:

/* kernel for fused: relu(add(a,b)) ; KernelSig = (out, a, b, shape[], strides[]) */
void tc_kernel_fused_0(float* restrict out, const float* restrict a,
                       const float* restrict b,
                       const int64_t* shape, const int64_t* strides);

Cross-reference: S1/S2 gate §13 importer conformance; S3 gates §21 codegen + §22 runtime load; S4 gates §25 oracle pass/fail. Any importer or codegen behavior not pinned by a spec above MUST be documented as a project-local decision in the ADR table (§9).

6. Design Principles

Non-negotiable invariants for the compiler + runtime. Numbered P1–P15. Later phases (## 12–## 25) reference these by name (e.g. "per P1"). Violating any one is a release blocker.

6.1 P1 — The reference oracle is truth

  • Statement. Every compiled kernel/graph output MUST match the NumPy-style reference oracle within documented tolerance (f32: rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6; f64: rtol 1e-12, atol 1e-14; i*/bool: exact). The oracle, not the compiler, defines correctness.
  • Tradeoff. Pins us to a portable, slow but auditable reference; forbids "clever" rewrites that drift numerically even if faster.
  • Operational. tcc check runs every op + model in the corpus vs oracle on each CI run; any out-of-tolerance diff fails the build with {node, max_abs_err, max_rel_err, idx}.
  • Violation. A reassociated MatMul accumulation passes 1024×1024 but drifts to 2e-4 rel err at 8192 reductions → silent wrong inference, no test fails because corpus lacked the large shape. Mitigation: corpus must span reduction depths.

6.2 P2 — Compute/schedule separation (schedule never changes numerics)

  • Statement. TensorExpr defines WHAT (the math); Schedule defines HOW (loop order, tiling, vectorize, parallel). Applying ANY Schedule primitive (split/reorder/fuse/tile/vectorize/unroll/parallel/compute_at/inline) MUST preserve bit-comparable-within-tolerance results vs the unscheduled naive PrimFunc.
  • Tradeoff. Float non-associativity means reorder/parallel over a reduction CAN change results — so reduction-axis reordering/splitting is governed by P9, not free.
  • Operational. Differential test (## 25): for each PrimFunc, run naive vs N random valid schedules; assert equality within tolerance. Schedule search (## 24) only explores numerics-preserving moves.
  • Violation. parallel applied to a Reduce axis without a deterministic tree-reduce → run-to-run nondeterminism + tolerance failures under thread scheduling jitter.

6.3 P3 — Two-level IR, lower late

  • Statement. Graph-level reasoning (shape/type, fusion, layout, const-fold, CSE, DCE, simplify) happens on tc-graph Graph IR over whole-tensor ops. Loop-level reasoning happens on tc-ir TIR (PrimFunc/Stmt/Expr/Buffer/IterVar). Lower Graph→TIR ONLY per fused group, AFTER all graph passes.
  • Tradeoff. Two IRs = more code + a lowering boundary to maintain; but each IR stays small and each pass operates at its natural altitude.
  • Operational. No pass mutates both IRs. tc-pass graph passes run before tc-fuse; lowering (## 18) is the single Graph→TIR crossing.
  • Violation. Doing CSE on raw loop nests (TIR) instead of the dataflow graph → must re-derive value-equality across Load/Store aliasing; misses cross-op redundancy fusion would have exposed.

6.4 P4 — Passes are verifiable + composable

  • Statement. Every Pass is a total function IR -> IR that PRESERVES the IR verifier invariants (every GraphNode.ty is Some and consistent after shape inference; every BufferId/VarId/NodeId resolves; no dangling args; loop extents non-negative). Passes compose in any verifier-valid order the PassManager schedules.
  • Tradeoff. The verifier runs between passes in debug/CI (cost) but is gated off in release hot compile.
  • Operational. PassManager invokes verify() after each pass under --verify (CI default). A pass that breaks an invariant is rejected at its boundary, naming the offending node.
  • Violation. A buggy simplify rewrites x*1 -> x but drops the result node's ty → downstream codegen reads ty == None, panics or emits a kernel with the wrong dtype.

6.5 P5 — Fusion eliminates materialization

  • Statement. tc-fuse MUST fuse injective/elementwise chains (+ at most one reduce, per the dominator rule) into a single PrimFunc so intermediate tensors are NEVER allocated to global storage — they live in registers/Scope::Local.
  • Tradeoff. Larger fused kernels = bigger compile units + more register pressure; over-fusion can spill. Fusion rules cap group size + reduce count.
  • Operational. Dominator-based grouping (## 16); the MemoryPlan (## 22) sees fewer live buffers. Fusion is the dominant CPU win — target ≥2–4× over unfused on elementwise-heavy graphs.
  • Violation. Failing to fuse Conv2d -> Add -> Relu materializes two full activation tensors → memory bandwidth bound, 2–3× slower, and MemoryPlan peak balloons.

6.6 P6 — Shape inference reaches a fixed point or fails loudly

  • Statement. Shape/type inference propagates TensorType through the Graph to a fixed point. It MUST terminate: either every node is typed, or it raises a precise error ({node, op, reason, inferred_partial}) — never leaves a node ty == None silently and never loops forever.
  • Tradeoff. Dim::Dynamic symbolic dims cannot be fully resolved at compile time; they are deferred to the VM, narrowing static optimization.
  • Operational. Monotone lattice (unknown → partial → full); bounded iteration count = graph node count; on no-progress with remaining unknowns → hard error. Verifier (P4) enforces "all typed" post-pass for static subgraphs.
  • Violation. A Reshape with an under-constrained -1 dim left as Dynamic flows into the GraphExecutor (static path) → allocator asked for an unknown byte count → panic at runtime instead of a clear compile error.

6.7 P7 — Memory planning never aliases live tensors

  • Statement. MemoryPlan computes buffer liveness over the executor's topological order and reuses Storage ONLY for tensors with disjoint live ranges. Two simultaneously-live tensors MUST NOT share bytes.
  • Tradeoff. Aggressive reuse minimizes peak memory but constrains async/out-of-order dispatch (a reused buffer serializes its producers/consumers). Conservative reuse wastes memory.
  • Operational. Liveness = classic register-allocation-on-tensors; tcc dump module shows the assignment + peak bytes. Async dispatch (## 23) must respect reuse-induced dependencies.
  • Violation. A WAR/WAW hazard from reusing a still-live buffer under async dispatch → one op overwrites another's input → corrupted output, intermittent and order-dependent.

6.8 P8 — Codegen emits a stable kernel ABI

  • Statement. Every Kernel exposes a stable KernelSig: C function taking (data ptr, shape[], strides[], offset) per buffer, fixed argument order, no global state. Runtime + VM dispatch ANY kernel uniformly through this ABI.
  • Tradeoff. Passing shapes/strides at call time (vs baking constants) costs a few loads; we accept it so one compiled kernel serves multiple shapes and the VM's dynamic shapes work without recompile.
  • Operational. CBackend/SimdBackend both emit the same signature shape; GraphExecutor + Interpreter (InvokeKernel) marshal NDArray → ABI identically. tcc dump c shows the signature.
  • Violation. A backend bakes a static stride into the kernel body → the VM reuses it for a different stride at runtime → reads wrong elements, no crash, wrong numbers.

6.9 P9 — Determinism with documented float reassociation

  • Statement. For a fixed Module + inputs + thread count, output is bit-identical run-to-run. Any reassociation that changes float results (reduction tree shape, SIMD horizontal-sum order, parallel partial sums) MUST be a DOCUMENTED, fixed-by-schedule choice — never thread-timing dependent.
  • Tradeoff. Deterministic parallel reductions (fixed tree + fixed partition) forgo some load-balancing flexibility.
  • Operational. parallel/vectorize over reductions use a fixed partition derived from split factors, not runtime work-stealing order. Documented in each kernel's metadata; tcc check runs each case twice and asserts bit-equality.
  • Violation. Thread-pool work-stealing accumulates partials in arrival order → two runs differ in the last ULPs → flaky check, non-reproducible bug reports.

6.10 P10 — Static graph → GraphExecutor; dynamic → VM

  • Statement. Fully static shapes (no Dim::Dynamic, no control flow) execute on the GraphExecutor (precomputed topological dispatch + precomputed MemoryPlan). Dynamic shapes or control flow (If/Goto/Call) execute on the tc-vm Interpreter, allocating at runtime. Both dispatch the SAME compiled Kernels.
  • Tradeoff. Two executors to maintain; the VM's per-instruction overhead is higher but it's the only path that handles Dim::Dynamic.
  • Operational. tcc compile classifies the model; tcc run selects executor. A static subgraph inside a dynamic model still compiles to shared Kernels invoked via InvokeKernel.
  • Violation. Routing a dynamic-shape model through GraphExecutor → MemoryPlan built for one shape, fed another → undersized Storage, OOB write.

6.11 P11 — Bounded compile + search budgets

  • Statement. Compilation and auto-tuning are time/resource bounded. tcc tune takes an explicit budget (trial count or wall-clock); the cost model prunes the schedule space; default tcc compile uses a fixed heuristic schedule and MUST finish (target: a ResNet-class graph compiles < 30 s without tuning).
  • Tradeoff. Bounded search may miss the global-optimum schedule; we accept a "good enough within budget" schedule and let tuning opt in to more.
  • Operational. Tuner (## 24): analytical cost model first, then ≤N measured trials; best-so-far always valid (P2). Budget exhaustion returns the best schedule found, never hangs.
  • Violation. Unbounded exhaustive tiling-factor search on a large MatMul → compile never terminates in CI → pipeline timeout.

6.12 P12 — Observable IR at every stage

  • Statement. Every intermediate artifact is dumpable: tcc dump graph | tir | scheduled | c | module. Each stage's output is human-readable and round-trippable for inspection. No opaque steps.
  • Tradeoff. Maintaining printers/parsers for 5 forms is upkeep; worth it for debuggability + golden-file tests.
  • Operational. Golden-file tests snapshot each dump for the corpus (## 25); a diff flags unintended IR drift. Bug reports attach the failing stage's dump.
  • Violation. A fusion regression with no dump graph → engineer can't see groups, debugs by guessing → days lost; with the dump, the bad FusionGroup is visible in one line.

6.13 P13 — No GC in the runtime hot path

  • Statement. The execution hot path (kernel dispatch, NDArray views, Storage alloc/free) does NO garbage collection and NO unbounded allocation per op. Storage is ref-counted; MemoryPlan pre-plans static-graph buffers so steady-state inference allocates zero new global buffers.
  • Tradeoff. Manual ref-counting + pre-planning is more error-prone than a GC; justifies the Rust choice (no GC, explicit ownership).
  • Operational. Allocator reuses freed Storage via a free list / arena; NDArray slices/reshapes are zero-copy strided views (no data copy). Steady-state allocation count asserted == plan size in bench (## 25).
  • Violation. Allocating a fresh Storage per op per inference → allocator pressure + cache thrash → latency variance + 2× slowdown vs planned reuse.

6.14 P14 — Zero-copy NDArray semantics are explicit

  • Statement. NDArray is a strided {storage, shape, strides, dtype, offset} view. reshape/transpose/slice produce NEW views sharing Storage when layout permits; a copy happens ONLY when explicitly required (non-contiguous → contiguous for a kernel that demands it). Whether an op copies is documented per op.
  • Tradeoff. Aliased views mean a write through one view is visible through another — caller must know; the layout pass (## 17) inserts copies only when cost-justified.
  • Operational. Codegen kernels consume arbitrary strides via the ABI (P8), so most reshapes need no copy. tcc dump module annotates which buffers alias.
  • Violation. Treating a strided transpose as contiguous in a kernel that ignores strides → reads row-major over column-major data → garbage output (couples to P8).

6.15 P15 — Non-goals stay out of the architecture

  • Statement. No autograd/backprop (proj 112), no training loops (114), no GPU/CUDA codegen (248/180; Device::Gpu, Scope::Shared, IterKind::Thread are PhaseDeferred stubs only), no distributed execution (351), no full ONNX op set (documented subset), no Python frontend (textual graph + ONNX-subset importer). These MUST NOT leak forward-shaped assumptions into the IR or runtime.
  • Tradeoff. Narrower scope; but the IR stays clean and the deferred GPU hooks (Scope, Device, IterKind) are present without being implemented.
  • Operational. Deferred enum variants compile but error if exercised (unimplemented: GPU deferred); the op registry is extensible but the conformance corpus covers only the documented subset.
  • Violation. Adding a backward-pass field to GraphNode "for later" → bloats the IR, complicates every pass + verifier, with no in-scope consumer.

7. System Invariants

System-wide hard rules that hold across the compile→run pipeline. Each is a checkable property; violation is a defect, not a degradation. Format per invariant: Statement / Why / Enforced / Detection / Telemetry / Recovery / Debugging. [CRIT] = correctness; violation produces wrong numbers or UB. [STRUCT] = structural; violation produces a crash or invalid IR. [OP] = operational; violation degrades but does not corrupt.

7.1 Invariant taxonomy

# Name Class Boundary enforced
INV-1 Pass semantic preservation [CRIT] every tc-pass Pass
INV-2 Schedule numerical equivalence (master perf invariant) [CRIT] every tc-schedule primitive
INV-3 Shape inference soundness + fixed point [CRIT] tc-pass shape infer
INV-4 IR verifier holds post-pass [STRUCT] tc-pass PassManager
INV-5 MemoryPlan storage disjointness [CRIT] tc-runtime MemoryPlan
INV-6 Fusion dataflow preservation [CRIT] tc-fuse FusionPass
INV-7 Kernel ↔ KernelSig contract [STRUCT] tc-codegen CBackend/SimdBackend
INV-8 NDArray strided-view bounds [CRIT] tc-runtime NDArray
INV-9 GraphExecutor topological dispatch [STRUCT] tc-runtime GraphExecutor
INV-10 VM runtime type/shape safety (trap, not UB) [CRIT] tc-vm Interpreter
INV-11 Buffer scope legality (Local/Shared) [STRUCT] tc-ir + tc-codegen
INV-12 Allocator alloc/free pairing (no leak, no double-free) [STRUCT] tc-runtime Allocator

Global enforcement mode: --invariants={off,debug,paranoid}. debug (default in test/CI): verifier + INV-1/3/4/9 checks. paranoid: adds numerical re-check of INV-2/6 against the oracle per transform; adds INV-5/8 bounds on every access. off: release hot path only.

7.2 INV-1 — Pass semantic preservation [CRIT]

  • Statement. For any Pass p: Graph→Graph, executing the graph before and after p on identical inputs yields outputs within tolerance (f32 rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6; f64 rtol 1e-12). Applies to const fold, CSE, DCE, algebraic simplify, layout transform.
  • Why. Optimization that changes results is a miscompile — the worst class of bug; silently corrupts every downstream model.
  • Enforced. PassManager::run in tc-pass; each Pass::transform is paired with a Pass::name() and registered tolerance. paranoid mode snapshots the pre-pass graph, interprets both on seeded random inputs, diffs vs oracle.
  • Detection. Differential harness (Phase 13): tcc check --per-pass runs the corpus through the pass pipeline incrementally, asserting equivalence after each pass.
  • Telemetry. Trace record {pass, node_count_pre/post, max_abs_err, max_rel_err, argmax_node} per pass; emitted to the pass log under --trace=pass.
  • Recovery. Offending pass auto-disabled in safe pipeline preset; recompile without it.
  • Debugging. Bisect pass list (--passes=a,b,c); the argmax_node localizes the first divergent node; dump pre/post subgraph via tcc dump --graph --around=<NodeId>.

7.3 INV-2 — Schedule numerical equivalence (master perf invariant) [CRIT]

  • Statement. No Schedule primitive — split, reorder, fuse, tile, vectorize, unroll, parallel, compute_at, inline — may change the numerical result of the PrimFunc. Only loop structure / performance changes. This is the compute/schedule separation guarantee (Halide model).
  • Why. The entire auto-tuner (Phase 12) explores thousands of schedules; if any could alter results, every tuned kernel would need full re-validation and the search is unsound.
  • Subtleties (documented, must hold):
    • Reduce reorder + float non-associativity: reordering a IterKind::Reduce axis or parallelizing a reduction changes float summation order → result drifts. Allowed only within the INV-2 tolerance; tuner records the reduction tree and bounds error growth (atol scaled by extent for large reductions). Cross-thread reduction uses a fixed deterministic tree, not racy accumulation.
    • vectorize must not cross a reduction-carried dependence; legality checked first.
    • compute_at / inline must preserve producer-before-consumer order.
  • Enforced. Schedule::* primitives run a legality_check before mutating stages; illegal transforms (e.g. reorder violating a dependence, parallel over a non-parallel reduce axis without the deterministic-tree flag) return Err(ScheduleError) and do not apply.
  • Detection. Phase 13 differential: each scheduled kernel diffed vs the naive (Phase 6 default-loop) PrimFunc on random inputs; tuner rejects any candidate exceeding tolerance.
  • Telemetry. {kernel, schedule_hash, max_rel_err_vs_naive, reduction_axes_reordered}.
  • Recovery. Candidate dropped from the search space; falls back to last-known-good schedule (default = naive) for that fused kernel.
  • Debugging. tcc dump --scheduled --kernel=<name> shows transformed loop nest; replay a single primitive via --schedule-step=N to find the divergence-introducing transform.

7.4 INV-3 — Shape inference soundness + fixed point [CRIT]

  • Statement. After shape inference, every GraphNode.ty is Some(TensorType) consistent with op + args per the op registry's shape rule, and the fixpoint iteration terminates (monotone lattice over Shape/DType). Static dims unify exactly; Dim::Dynamic(name) unifies symbolically (same name ⇒ same value at runtime).
  • Why. Codegen, MemoryPlan, and the executor all assume types are present + correct; an unsound type silently produces a mis-sized buffer.
  • Enforced. tc-pass shape-inference pass propagates TensorType to fixed point; the verifier (INV-4) rejects any ty == None or rule-violating node. Lattice height bounded by graph rank → guaranteed termination; iteration cap = 2 × node_count, exceeding it is a bug (non-monotone rule).
  • Detection. Per-op shape oracle in conformance corpus; symbolic dynamic dims validated by the VM at runtime against actual NDArray shapes (links to INV-10).
  • Telemetry. {iterations_to_fixpoint, nodes_untyped, unify_failures}; if iterations_to_fixpoint > node_count warn (slow-converging rule).
  • Recovery. Untyped/inconsistent node → hard compile error with the node + offending op rule; no partial codegen.
  • Debugging. tcc dump --graph --types prints inferred TensorType per node; --shape-trace logs each unification step.

7.5 INV-4 — IR verifier holds after every pass [STRUCT]

  • Statement. After every Pass (graph or TIR), the verifier passes:
    • Graph IR: no dangling NodeId (every arg ∈ nodes), no ty-shape mismatch, no cycles in the dataflow DAG, inputs/outputs reference live nodes.
    • TIR: no use-before-def of any VarId/BufferId, every Load/Store targets an Allocated-or-param Buffer, Expr types well-formed, loop extent ≥ 0.
  • Why. A pass that corrupts the IR cascades into a crash deep in codegen/runtime where the root cause is unrecoverable.
  • Enforced. PassManager::run calls verify() after each Pass in debug/paranoid; verify() returns Result<(), VerifyError> with the first offending id + reason.
VerifyError ::= DanglingNodeId(NodeId, in: NodeId)
              | TypeShapeMismatch(NodeId, expected: TensorType, got: TensorType)
              | Cycle(Vec<NodeId>)
              | UseBeforeDef(VarId | BufferId, at: StmtSpan)
              | UnallocatedBuffer(BufferId, at: StmtSpan)
              | NegativeExtent(VarId, Expr)
  • Detection. Always-on in CI; the pass that first fails verify() is named.
  • Telemetry. {pass, verify_ok, first_error}.
  • Recovery. Abort pipeline before codegen; never compile an unverified IR.
  • Debugging. Verifier names the producing pass + id; tcc dump --tir --verify annotates.

7.6 INV-5 — MemoryPlan storage disjointness [CRIT]

  • Statement. The MemoryPlan never assigns the same Storage (overlapping byte range) to two tensors whose liveness intervals overlap in the executor's topological order. Reuse is permitted only for non-overlapping intervals (register-allocation-on-tensors).
  • Why. Aliasing live tensors silently overwrites data → wrong results that look like random numerical corruption.
  • Enforced. MemoryPlan builder computes liveness [def, last_use] per intermediate tensor over the topo order, then a greedy/interval coloring assigns storage; a post-build assert_disjoint() re-scans all (tensor, storage-range, interval) triples.
  • Detection. paranoid: poison freed storage (0xDEAD) and bounds-tag each NDArray; reads of poisoned bytes flagged. Conformance: identical results with planning on vs off.
  • Telemetry. {plan_bytes, peak_live_bytes, reuse_count, overlap_violations}; overlap_violations must be 0.
  • Recovery. On any detected overlap, disable reuse (each tensor gets fresh storage) and recompute — correct but higher peak memory.
  • Debugging. tcc dump --module --memplan prints the liveness/coloring table (tensor → storage slot, interval); diff peak vs no-reuse to confirm coloring.

7.7 INV-6 — Fusion dataflow preservation [CRIT]

  • Statement. A FusionGroup lowered to one PrimFunc computes exactly the values (within tolerance) the unfused subgraph would, with identical externally-visible outputs; only intermediates are elided (not materialized).
  • Why. Fusion is the dominant CPU win but it rewrites the boundary between ops; a wrong fusion rule miscomputes whole kernels.
  • Enforced. tc-fuse dominator-based grouping admits only legal patterns: an injective/elementwise chain plus at most one reduce/inject root; the group's external inputs/outputs must equal the subgraph's boundary edges (checked against the dominator frontier). No op with an external consumer of an intermediate is fused.
  • Detection. Differential: execute graph with FusionPass on vs off; outputs must match within INV-1 tolerance. Each fusion rule has corpus fixtures.
  • Telemetry. {group_id, op_count, intermediates_elided, boundary_inputs/outputs}.
  • Recovery. Suspect rule disabled; group falls back to unfused (each op its own kernel).
  • Debugging. tcc dump --graph --fusion colors nodes by FusionGroup; compare boundary edges pre/post.

7.8 INV-7 — Kernel ↔ KernelSig contract [STRUCT]

  • Statement. An emitted Kernel.source exactly implements its KernelSig: parameter count/order = (buffer pointers, then shape extents, then strides), dtypes match the Buffer dtypes, and the executor/VM call passes arguments matching that signature.
  • Why. A sig mismatch is an ABI bug — type-punned pointers / wrong strides → UB at the C boundary.
  • Enforced. CodeGen::emit produces Kernel.signature and Kernel.source from one source of truth (the PrimFunc params); a post-emit check parses the emitted prototype and asserts it equals KernelSig. The executor builds the call from KernelSig only.
// emitted-kernel prototype (OUTPUT artifact), e.g. fused add+relu:
void k_add_relu(float* out, const float* a, const float* b,
                int64_t n, int64_t s_out, int64_t s_a, int64_t s_b);
  • Detection. Round-trip test: KernelSig → emitted prototype → re-parsed KernelSig must be identical; cc compile failure ⇒ contract bug.
  • Telemetry. {kernel, n_ptr_args, n_shape_args, n_stride_args, cc_status}.
  • Recovery. Compile fails fast; no Module is produced with a mismatched kernel.
  • Debugging. tcc dump --c --kernel=<name> prints source + sig side by side.

7.9 INV-8 — NDArray strided-view bounds [CRIT]

  • Statement. Every NDArray access at multi-index i resolves to a byte offset within [0, storage.bytes): offset + Σ i[d]·strides[d] (in elements ·dtype.size()) is in range; zero-copy slices/reshape produce views that stay within the parent storage.
  • Why. Out-of-bounds strided access is silent memory corruption / UB.
  • Enforced. NDArray constructors (slice, reshape, transpose) validate that the max reachable offset < storage.bytes and (for reshape) element count is preserved; paranoid adds a per-access bound check in the runtime dispatch path.
  • Detection. Conformance fuzzing of view ops; ASAN under cargo test for the C-FFI kernel boundary; debug_assert on every stride computation.
  • Telemetry. {op, max_offset, storage_bytes, in_bounds}.
  • Recovery. Invalid view → Err(ShapeError), no NDArray produced; trap not UB.
  • Debugging. tcc run --check-views logs computed bounds per access.

7.10 INV-9 — GraphExecutor topological dispatch [STRUCT]

  • Statement. GraphExecutor dispatches kernels in a valid topological order of the Module graph: every input NDArray of a node is produced (or bound) before the node runs; async dispatch only relaxes order between independent nodes (respecting the dependence DAG).
  • Why. Running a consumer before its producer reads uninitialized/stale storage.
  • Enforced. Executor computes a topo order at load; async scheduler issues a node only when all in-edge producers have completed (dependency counters / readiness queue).
  • Detection. Cycle check at load (shares INV-4 cycle detection); paranoid tags each storage as written and asserts every read follows a write.
  • Telemetry. {nodes_dispatched, ready_queue_depth, out_of_order_violations=0}.
  • Recovery. On a detected ordering violation, fall back to serial topo execution.
  • Debugging. tcc run --trace=dispatch logs dispatch order + per-node readiness time.

7.11 INV-10 — VM runtime type/shape safety (trap, not UB) [CRIT]

  • Statement. In tc-vm, any runtime type/shape mismatch — InvokeKernel with NDArrays whose shapes/dtypes violate the kernel's KernelSig, an If on a non-scalar-bool reg, a Call arity mismatch — is a deterministic trap (VmTrap), never UB. Dynamic Dim::Dynamic shapes are resolved + checked at AllocTensor/InvokeKernel time.
  • Why. The VM handles dynamic control flow + variable shapes where static inference cannot prove safety; it must fail safe.
  • Enforced. Interpreter validates each VmInstruction against register contents before executing: dtype/shape vs KernelSig, register occupancy, label validity.
VmTrap ::= ShapeMismatch(KernelId, expected: KernelSig, got: Vec<Shape>)
         | DTypeMismatch(KernelId, RegId)
         | BadCondition(RegId)         // If on non-bool/non-scalar
         | ArityMismatch(FuncId, got: usize)
         | UninitRegister(RegId)
  • Detection. VM conformance corpus with dynamic shapes + control flow; fuzzer emits random VmFuncs — must trap cleanly, never crash/UB.
  • Telemetry. {func, pc, instr, trap_kind} on trap; counters per trap kind.
  • Recovery. Trap unwinds the call stack, frees the register file, returns Err(VmTrap) to the caller; no partial corruption.
  • Debugging. tcc run --vm --trace=instr dumps the instruction stream + register shapes at the trapping pc.

7.12 INV-11 — Buffer scope legality [STRUCT]

  • Statement. Only Scope::Global/Local are codegen-legal on the CPU path; Scope::Shared (and IterKind::Thread) are GPU-deferred and must be rejected by the CPU backend, not silently miscompiled. Local buffers must not escape the loop nest that allocates them.
  • Why. Emitting CPU C for a GPU-only scope produces nonsense; escape produces dangling stack references.
  • Enforced. CBackend/SimdBackend::emit matches on Buffer.scope; Shared/Thread ⇒ Err(CodeGenError::UnsupportedScope). Escape analysis in TIR verifier.
  • Detection. Negative fixtures with Shared scope must fail compilation cleanly.
  • Telemetry. {kernel, scopes_seen, unsupported_scope}.
  • Recovery. Compile error; no Module emitted.
  • Debugging. tcc dump --tir --scopes annotates each Buffer's scope.

7.13 INV-12 — Allocator alloc/free pairing [STRUCT]

  • Statement. Every Allocator::alloc is matched by exactly one free over a run; no leak (all freed at executor/VM teardown), no double-free, no use-after-free. Storage is ref-counted; bytes returned to the allocator only at refcount 0.
  • Why. Leaks degrade long-running serving; double-free is UB.
  • Enforced. Storage Drop decrements refcount → Allocator::free; the executor's MemoryPlan owns the allocation lifecycle. paranoid poisons freed bytes.
  • Detection. Leak check at teardown (alloc_count == free_count); ASAN/Miri in CI.
  • Telemetry. {alloc_count, free_count, peak_bytes, live_at_teardown=0}.
  • Recovery. Teardown force-frees any live storage with a warning (leak logged).
  • Debugging. tcc run --trace=alloc logs each alloc/free with size + caller.

7.14 Invariant ↔ phase ↔ test matrix

Invariant Introduced Primary gate (phase) CI check
INV-1 ## 14 ## 25 differential per-pass check --per-pass
INV-2 ## 17–## 20 ## 25 schedule diff + ## 24 tuner check --schedule
INV-3 ## 14 ## 14 verifier verify, shape oracle
INV-4 ## 12 every pass verify always-on
INV-5 ## 22 ## 22 plan + ## 25 --memplan, poison
INV-6 ## 16 ## 25 fusion-on/off diff check --fusion
INV-7 ## 21 ## 21 round-trip sig round-trip, cc
INV-8 ## 22 ## 25 view fuzz ASAN, view bounds
INV-9 ## 22 ## 22 dispatch trace cycle + write-before-read
INV-10 ## 23 ## 23 + ## 25 VM fuzz trap-not-crash fuzz
INV-11 ## 18/## 21 ## 21 negative fixtures scope reject
INV-12 ## 22 ## 22 teardown Miri/ASAN, leak count

Governing rule. INV-1, INV-2, INV-6 are the three correctness pillars; together they say the only thing the whole compiler is allowed to change is performance. Any commit that weakens a [CRIT] invariant requires an ADR and a corpus expansion, never a tolerance loosening without justification.

8. Domain Model — Graph IR, TIR, and the Schedule

This is the semantic core: two IRs (tc-graph, tc-ir), the compute/schedule separation (Halide model), fusion (TVM dominator model), shape inference, memory planning. Every transform below preserves numerical result; only performance moves. Invariant NUM-INVARIANT: for any schedule S, eval(apply(S, primfunc)) == eval(primfunc) within tolerance (f32 rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6) — see §13 oracle.

8.1 Graph IR (tc-graph) — the functional dataflow layer

Whole-tensor dataflow DAG. Nodes are values (SSA-like), edges are args. No mutation, no aliasing at this layer — that appears only after lowering to TIR.

Type Role Key fields
Graph the DAG nodes: Arena<GraphNode>, inputs: Vec<NodeId>, outputs: Vec<NodeId>
GraphNode one value op: GraphOp, args: Vec<NodeId>, ty: Option<TensorType>
GraphOp operator tag Input/Const/Add/Mul/MatMul/Conv2d/Relu/Reshape/Transpose/Reduce(ReduceKind)/Call(String)
TensorType value type shape: Shape, dtype: DType
Shape dims Vec<Dim>; Dim::Static(i64) | Dim::Dynamic(String)
DType scalar F32/F64/I32/I64/I8/Bool
  • NodeId = arena index (typed u32 newtype); Arena is generational, no dangling, O(1) deref. DAG is acyclic by construction — add_node only references already-inserted ids → topological order = insertion order.
  • ty starts None; shape-inference (§8.6) fills it to a fixed point.
  • Const(TensorData) carries an owned host buffer (folded constants live here).
  • Call(String) = named subfunction / external op; resolved by op registry.

8.1.1 Op-registry contract

Each GraphOp variant (and each Call name) registers an OpDef:

OpDef {
  name:        &'static str,
  n_inputs:    Arity,                 // Fixed(n) | Variadic
  infer_type:  fn(&[TensorType], &Attrs) -> Result<TensorType, TypeError>,
  lower:       fn(&[TensorExpr], &Attrs) -> TensorExpr,   // -> TIR compute
  fuse_pattern: FusePattern,          // Injective | Elementwise | Reduce | Opaque
}

Registry invariants:

  • R1 every GraphOp constructible in a Graph has an OpDef (verifier checks).
  • R2 infer_type is pure + monotone (never narrows a resolved Static to Dynamic).
  • R3 lower emits exactly one TensorExpr output; multi-output ops are modeled as a tuple-producing op + projections (deferred ops note this).
  • R4 fuse_pattern drives §8.5; Opaque = a hard fusion boundary.

FusePattern taxonomy: Elementwise (Add/Mul/Relu, shape-preserving, 1:1), Injective (Reshape/Transpose, index-remap, no reduce), Reduce (Reduce/MatMul/Conv2d, contracts an axis — a fusion group has ≤1 reduce), Opaque (Call to unknown extern).

8.1.2 Textual graph format (importer + tcc dump graph)

A .tcg round-trippable textual form (the non-ONNX frontend). EBNF:

graph     ::= "graph" ident "{" sig stmt* "}"
sig       ::= "in"  "(" binding ("," binding)* ")"
              "out" "(" ident   ("," ident)*   ")"
binding   ::= ident ":" tensortype
stmt      ::= "%" ident "=" op "(" arglist? ")" (":" tensortype)?
op        ::= ident attrs?
attrs     ::= "[" attr ("," attr)* "]"
attr      ::= ident "=" literal
arglist   ::= ("%" ident) ("," "%" ident)*
tensortype::= dtype "[" dim ("," dim)* "]"
dim       ::= int | "?" ident          (* "?n" = Dim::Dynamic("n") *)
dtype     ::= "f32"|"f64"|"i32"|"i64"|"i8"|"bool"

Example (a fused-conv corpus fixture):

graph net {
  in(x: f32[1,3,224,224], w: f32[64,3,7,7], b: f32[64])
  out(y)
  %c = Conv2d[stride=2, pad=3](x, w) : f32[1,64,112,112]
  %d = Add(%c, b)                     : f32[1,64,112,112]
  %y = Relu(%d)                       : f32[1,64,112,112]
}

Types after : are optional in source; the importer leaves them None and shape-inference fills them. On re-dump they are always printed (canonical form).

8.2 TIR (tc-ir) — the imperative loop-nest layer

After fusion, each FusionGroup lowers to one PrimFunc: an imperative loop nest over Buffers. This is where loop order, indexing, and storage become explicit and schedulable.

8.2.1 TIR grammar (EBNF)

primfunc  ::= "primfn" "(" buffer ("," buffer)* ")" "{" stmt "}"
stmt      ::= for | store | block | ifte | alloc
for       ::= "for" itervar "in" "[" expr "," expr ")" "{" stmt "}"   (* min, extent *)
store     ::= buffer_id "[" expr "]" "=" expr ";"
block     ::= "{" stmt* "}"
ifte      ::= "if" "(" expr ")" stmt ("else" stmt)?
alloc     ::= "alloc" buffer_id ";"
expr      ::= load | var | const | bin | call
load      ::= buffer_id "[" expr "]"
var       ::= var_id
const     ::= float
bin       ::= expr binop expr
binop     ::= "+"|"-"|"*"|"/"|"%"|"<"|"=="|"max"|"min"
call      ::= intrin "(" (expr ("," expr)*)? ")"        (* exp, sqrt, fma, ... *)
buffer    ::= buffer_id ":" dtype "<" expr ("," expr)* ">" scope
itervar   ::= var_id ":" iterkind                       (* Data | Reduce *)
scope     ::= "global"|"local"|"shared"                 (* shared = GPU-deferred *)

Maps 1:1 to the LOCKED Rust enums: Stmt::{For,Store,Block,IfThenElse,Allocate}, Expr::{Load,Var,Const,Bin,Call}, Buffer{id,shape,dtype,scope}, IterVar{var,kind} (IterKind::{Data,Reduce}; Thread deferred).

TIR invariants:

  • T1 every Buffer referenced by Load/Store is a param or appears in an enclosing Allocate.
  • T2 a Store index expression is affine in enclosing IterVars (required for schedule legality + bounds proofs); non-affine → Opaque, not scheduled.
  • T3 a Reduce IterVar may only index Loads on the RHS, never the store axis of its own output (else the reduction is ill-formed).
  • T4 Scope::{Shared} and IterKind::Thread are reserved (GPU-deferred); the CPU codegen rejects them.

8.2.2 TensorExpr and default-PrimFunc lowering

TensorExpr = the declarative compute (Halide "what"): an output shape + a pure lambda over output indices. Lowering = wrap each output axis in a For (Data kind) and each contracted axis in a For (Reduce kind), innermost, emitting the body as a Store.

TensorExpr {
  name:   String,
  shape:  Vec<Expr>,                 // output extents
  axes:   Vec<IterVar>,              // Data axes (output) + Reduce axes
  body:   fn(indices) -> Expr,       // pure; reads input Buffers via Load
}

lower_default(te) -> PrimFunc rule: nest Data axes outer→inner in declared order, then Reduce axes, init the store cell before the reduce loop, accumulate inside. Result is the naive nested-loop baseline; all performance comes from the schedule (§8.3).

8.3 Compute/schedule separation — worked matmul

Compute (C[i,j] = Σ_k A[i,k]*B[k,j], M=N=K=1024, f32) as TensorExpr:

te_matmul = TensorExpr {
  name: "matmul",
  shape: [M, N],
  axes:  [i:Data, j:Data, k:Reduce],
  body:  |i,j| reduce(k, A[i,k] * B[k,j], init=0.0),
}

Naive PrimFunc from lower_default — matmul(A: f32<M,K> global, B: f32<K,N> global, C: f32<M,N> global): a 3-deep loop nest over i ∈ [0,M), j ∈ [0,N), k ∈ [0,K); each output C[i,j] is zero-initialized then accumulated as the reduction Σ_k A[i,k]·B[k,j]. Row-major index algebra: C[i*N+j], A[i*K+k], B[k*N+j]. No tiling, vectorization, or parallelism yet.

Schedule — identical numerics, optimized loops; built from Schedule::new(primfn):

# Directive Effect
1 split i, 64 → (i_o, i_i) tile rows
2 split j, 64 → (j_o, j_i) tile cols
3 split k, 256 → (k_o, k_i) tile contraction
4 reorder [i_o, j_o, k_o, i_i, k_i, j_i] cache-blocked iteration order
5 vectorize j_i innermost → 8× f32 AVX2 SIMD lanes
6 unroll k_i unroll the reduce tail
7 parallel i_o thread-pool over row tiles

Transformed loop nest (schedule applied → scheduled TIR): a parallel outer loop over i_o ∈ [0,M/64), nested j_o ∈ [0,N/64) and k_o ∈ [0,K/256) tiles, then i_i ∈ [0,64), an unrolled k_i ∈ [0,256), and a vectorized j_i ∈ [0,64) mapped to 8-lane SIMD FMA/store. The accumulation C[(i_o·64+i_i)·N + j_o·64+j_i] += A[(i_o·64+i_i)·K + k_o·256+k_i] · B[(k_o·256+k_i)·N + j_o·64+j_i] reuses the naive nest's index algebra.

Index algebra unchanged → NUM-INVARIANT holds; only iteration order + vector/parallel mapping differ. Targets: naive ~1–2 GFLOP/s; scheduled ≥30 GFLOP/s single-thread avx2, ≥60% of OpenBLAS sgemm multi-thread (§13 bench).

Schedule legality (checked by tc-schedule before apply):

  • L1 split(ax,f) requires extent(ax) % f == 0 OR emits a guarded tail loop (IfThenElse); never silently truncates.
  • L2 reorder must keep every Reduce axis inside the Data axes that index its output cell (else partial sums leak across cells).
  • L3 vectorize(ax) requires ax is the innermost axis and the Store index is unit-stride in ax; otherwise Err(IllegalSchedule::NotInnermost).
  • L4 compute_at(stage,ax)/inline must not move a producer below a consumer that reads it (dependency-preserving).

8.4 (Schedule primitives summary)

Primitive Signature Effect Legality
split split(ax,f)->(o,i) one loop → two L1
reorder reorder(&[ax]) permute loop order L2
fuse fuse(a,b)->ax two loops → one adjacency, same body
tile tile(ax,ay,fx,fy) split+split+reorder L1×2
vectorize vectorize(ax) axis → SIMD lanes L3
unroll unroll(ax) replicate body static extent
parallel parallel(ax) axis → thread pool no loop-carried dep
compute_at compute_at(st,ax) move producer inward L4
inline inline(st) fold producer into consumer expr producer used once-affine

8.5 Operator fusion via dominator analysis

Goal: eliminate intermediate materialization. A FusionGroup is a connected subgraph fusable into one PrimFunc. Rule: an Elementwise/Injective chain plus at most one Reduce at the group head can fuse iff each member is dominated by the group's exit in the reverse dataflow (every path to outputs passes through it → safe to inline, no external reader of the intermediate).

Algorithm (FusionPass):

  1. build dominator tree over the dataflow DAG (Lengauer-Tarjan, O(E·α)).
  2. seed groups at Reduce/Opaque boundaries.
  3. greedily absorb a node n into its dominated Elementwise/Injective successors while fuse_pattern compatible and group has ≤1 reduce.
  4. emit each FusionGroup → composed TensorExpr → lower_default → PrimFunc.

Worked subgraph — Conv2d -> Add(bias) -> Relu:

BEFORE (3 GraphNodes, 2 intermediate tensors materialized):
  %c = Conv2d(x,w)   -> f32[1,64,112,112]   (~3.2 MB)
  %d = Add(%c,b)     -> f32[1,64,112,112]   (~3.2 MB)   <- materialized
  %y = Relu(%d)      -> f32[1,64,112,112]               <- materialized

dominators: %y dom-by %d dom-by %c; %d,%c have no external reader
-> FusionGroup{ reduce: Conv2d, elemwise: [Add, Relu] }

AFTER fusion (1 PrimFunc, 0 intermediate tensors): a single loop nest over output coordinates (oc, oh, ow) with an inner reduction over (ic, kh, kw) accumulating acc += x[..]·w[..] (the conv reduce); the bias add (acc + b[oc]) and ReLU (max(acc, 0)) are fused into the same nest, before the lone store to y[oc,oh,ow].

Win: 2 fewer 3.2 MB writes + reads + 2 fewer kernel launches; the bias/relu ride the conv's already-hot accumulator. Fusion is the dominant CPU speedup. Boundary: a second consumer of %c (a residual add) → %c is not dominated by %y → fusion stops, %c materializes.

8.6 Shape inference to a fixed point

infer_shapes (a tc-pass analysis) propagates TensorType forward in topological order, calling each OpDef::infer_type. Iterate until no ty changes (a fixed point); a pure-monotone lattice (R2) → terminates in one pass for a DAG, ≤2 for graphs with Reshape back-constraints.

Worked example (.tcg fixture above, conv branch):

iter 0:  x:f32[1,3,224,224]✓  w:f32[64,3,7,7]✓  b:f32[64]✓  c,d,y: None
iter 1:  c = Conv2d.infer(x,w; stride=2,pad=3)
            -> [1,64, (224+2*3-7)/2+1=112, 112] = f32[1,64,112,112] ✓
         d = Add.infer(c,b)  -> broadcast b[64]->[1,64,1,1] -> f32[1,64,112,112] ✓
         y = Relu.infer(d)   -> f32[1,64,112,112] ✓
iter 2:  no change -> FIXED POINT. verifier: every node.ty == Some(_).

Dynamic dims: Dim::Dynamic("n") stays symbolic; arithmetic keeps it (n*2, n+pad) and the node is marked dynamic → routed to the VM (§8.7g), not the static GraphExecutor.

8.7 MemoryPlan — liveness-based buffer reuse

After fusion + lowering, the GraphExecutor runs kernels in topological order. MemoryPlan computes per-tensor live ranges [first_def, last_use] and reuses storage for non-overlapping tensors — register allocation on tensors.

Worked example (5 intermediates, topo order t1..t5):

tensor  bytes   live range        assigned slot
t1      4 MB    [0, 2]            S0
t2      4 MB    [1, 3]            S1
t3      2 MB    [2, 2]            S2   (dies at 2)
t4      2 MB    [3, 4]            S2   (reuses S0? no: S0 live till 2; reuse S2: free at 2) -> S2
t5      4 MB    [4, 4]            S0   (S0 free after t1 dies at 2) -> S0
peak: max simultaneously-live = slots {S0,S1,S2} at step 2 = 4+4+2 = 10 MB
naive (no reuse) = 16 MB ; saving 37.5%

Algorithm: sort by first_def; greedy interval coloring with a free-list keyed by byte-size class; output MemoryPlan{ tensor->StorageSlot }. Aliased tensors (zero-copy Reshape/Transpose views, §10 NDArray) share a slot by definition and are excluded from coloring (their storage = producer's).

8.8 Malformed inputs and edge cases — modeled response

# Condition Where caught Modeled response
a Shape mismatch at import (Add(f32[4], f32[5]) non-broadcastable) importer / infer_type Err(ShapeError{node,lhs,rhs}); abort compile, no partial module
b Untypable node (Call("unknown"), no OpDef) shape-infer fixed point + verifier Err(TypeError::Unresolved(node)); verifier fails (R1)
c Cyclic graph (importer fed a back-edge) Graph build / topo sort rejected at construction (arena forbids fwd-ref) or Err(CyclicGraph) from topo
d Illegal schedule: vectorize non-innermost / non-unit-stride tc-schedule apply, L3 Err(IllegalSchedule::NotInnermost); schedule unchanged, NUM-INVARIANT safe
e Dynamic shape on static path (Dim::Dynamic) executor selection route to tc-vm Interpreter; GraphExecutor::compile returns Err(DynamicShape)
f Divide-by-zero in const fold (Div(c, Const 0.0)) const_fold pass f32 → emit IEEE inf/nan (no panic, matches oracle); int → Err(ConstFold::DivByZero), fold skipped, op kept
g split factor not dividing extent tc-schedule, L1 emit guarded tail loop (IfThenElse); never silent truncation
h Reduce axis indexing its own store cell TIR verifier, T3 Err(IllFormedReduce)

All errors are typed (thiserror), carry the offending NodeId/AxisId, and never produce a numerically-wrong module — fail closed.

8.9 End-to-end compile+run sequence

 tcc        tc-graph   tc-pass    tc-fuse    tc-ir     tc-schedule  tc-codegen  tc-runtime
  |  import     |          |          |         |           |           |           |
  |------------>| Graph    |          |         |           |           |           |
  |             |  infer/fold/cse/dce/simplify/layout       |           |           |
  |             |--------->|          |         |           |           |           |
  |             |          |-->groups |         |           |           |           |
  |             |          |          |-lower-->| PrimFunc  |           |           |
  |             |          |          |         |--schedule>| sched TIR |           |
  |             |          |          |         |           |--emit---->| Kernel    |
  |             |          |          |         |           |           |  cc->.so  |
  |             |          |          |         |           |           |--Module-->|
  |  run(inputs)|          |          |         |           |           |           | plan mem
  |-------------------------------------------------------------------------------->| dispatch
  |             |          |          |         |           |           |           |  kernels
  |<--------------------------------- output NDArray -------------------------------| (topo)
  |  check vs oracle (rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6)                                        |

Static shapes → GraphExecutor; any Dim::Dynamic → tc-vm Interpreter allocates at runtime and dispatches the same compiled Kernels. The terminal check gate enforces NUM-INVARIANT against the NumPy-style reference oracle.

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

One row per locked architectural decision. ADRs are immutable once accepted; supersession (not edit) on change. Status: all Accepted unless tagged Deferred.

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust, edition 2021, MSRV 1.78 C/C++; Python; Go Sum types + exhaustive match for IR nodes → pass safety; no GC for runtime hot path + zero-copy NDArray; ownership tames large mutable IR + buffer model. C/C++ memory-unsafe over IR; Python too slow for runtime (frontend-only role); Go weak sum types + GC perturbs latency. C wins when embedding the compiler in a tight bare-metal firmware loop with no allocator (no Rust std/alloc available, LLVM GC incompatible). Python wins for a pure research-notebook frontend that never touches the hot path.
ADR-02 Workspace decomposition Nine member crates tc-graph, tc-ir, tc-pass, tc-fuse, tc-schedule, tc-codegen, tc-runtime, tc-vm, tc-cli Single monolithic crate; two-crate (compiler/runtime) split Crate boundary = enforced dependency DAG (e.g. tc-pass cannot reach codegen); parallel cargo build; per-crate fuzz/bench targets; clean compiler↔runtime cut at tc-codegen::Module. Monolith loses boundary checks; coarse split hides layering. Monolithic crate wins when cross-crate link time dominates (e.g., incremental compile on a single-file project with <2 000 lines total) or when inlining across crate boundaries is critical for sub-microsecond dispatch.
ADR-03 IR layering Two-level: Graph IR (tc-graph) + tensor/loop IR (tc-ir), lowered per fused group Single unified IR; MLIR-style multi-dialect with custom infra TVM Relay/TIR split: whole-tensor dataflow vs imperative loop-nest are distinct optimization domains (fusion/layout vs tiling/vectorize). Single IR conflates both → unschedulable. MLIR dialect infra is a multi-year project off-scope; we reuse its pass-manager design, not its codebase. MLIR multi-dialect wins when the target has 3+ distinct abstraction levels needing progressive lowering (e.g., accelerator ISA with special DMA tiles, vector units, and scalar registers — as in Google TPU's XLA/MLIR stack). Single unified IR wins for a toy scalar interpreter with no optimization pass.
ADR-04 IR storage model Arena + typed ids NodeId/BufferId/VarId/AxisId/StageId Rc<RefCell<Node>> graphs; Box trees with parent ptrs Arena = O(1) alloc, contiguous cache layout, trivial clone/serialize, no cycles, ids are Copy. Rc graph compiler → lifetime tangle, ref-cycle leaks, poor locality. Typed ids prevent cross-arena confusion at compile time. Rc<RefCell<>> wins in an interpreter REPL where users mutate graph nodes interactively in a single-threaded Python-like environment (shared mutable references match the use model, and performance is dominated by user think-time).
ADR-05 Compute/schedule separation Halide model: TensorExpr = WHAT, Schedule = HOW; schedule never alters numerics Hand-written per-op kernels; fused-template metaprogramming Decouples correctness (one compute def) from performance (many schedules) → enables auto-tuning (Phase 12) + portability. Hand-written kernels: O(ops × targets × schedules) blowup, no search. Invariant: schedule change ⇒ identical result within tolerance. Hand-written kernels win when a vendor provides architecture-tuned intrinsic libraries (cuBLAS, oneDNN) that outperform any auto-scheduler on a proprietary accelerator; the compute/schedule abstraction overhead becomes unjustified.
ADR-06 Operator fusion strategy Dominator-tree analysis in FusionPass grouping injective/elementwise chain + ≤1 reduce Pattern-match-only (fixed peephole rules); no fusion Dominator analysis generalizes to arbitrary DAG shapes; fusion is the dominant CPU win (eliminates intermediate materialization + memory traffic). Pattern-only misses long chains / diamond shapes. No-fusion → memory-bound. Pattern-match-only wins when the fusion policy is safety-critical and must be auditable by regulators — a fixed, enumerated peephole table with formal proofs per pattern is easier to certify than a graph-theory analysis pass.
ADR-07 Default kernel backend C source (CBackend) compiled via system cc LLVM-only; direct assembly emission; Cranelift C backend is portable, debuggable (readable kernels = dump c), zero heavyweight build dep, leverages cc autovec/-O3. LLVM-only adds large build dep + version churn. Direct asm = per-arch rewrite, unsafe. LLVM wins in an AOT deployment where the target device has no system cc (e.g., mobile firmware image, Wasm sandbox) and the binary must ship pre-compiled kernels with vectorization guarantees. Direct assembly wins for a cycle-accurate inner loop on a novel ISA unsupported by any C compiler.
ADR-08 Optional LLVM path LLVM codegen behind a llvm feature flag (off by default) LLVM mandatory; never Power users get tighter codegen without forcing the dep on the default build / CI. Feature-gated keeps MSRV + minimal-deps invariant for the core path. LLVM-mandatory wins when the deployment fleet is homogeneous (single LLVM version locked to OS packages), GFLOP/s parity with BLAS is a hard gate, and the build-dep burden is already paid by the surrounding toolchain.
ADR-09 SIMD strategy Portable SIMD abstraction (SimdBackend) emitting width-parametric vector ops Target-specific intrinsics (AVX2/NEON inline) hardcoded; scalar-only One vectorize lowering retargets across arches via Target.features; matches Halide/portable-simd model. Intrinsics = N× duplicated backends, fragile. Scalar-only forfeits 4–8× throughput. Hand-coded intrinsics win when squeezing the last 10–20% from a single-target HPC cluster node where AVX-512 gather/scatter, FMA, and prefetch patterns cannot be expressed in portable abstractions (e.g., GEMM kernel competing with MKL on Xeon).
ADR-10 Memory management Liveness-based MemoryPlan (buffer reuse via interval analysis) Arena-per-op (alloc each intermediate); GC; static max-size slab Register-allocation-on-tensors: reuse storage for non-overlapping live ranges → minimal peak RSS. Arena-per-op wastes memory + cache. GC: latency jitter. Plan computed over executor topological order. GC wins in an interactive research environment (e.g., TensorFlow 1.x eager mode, PyTorch autograd) where dynamic graph changes between iterations make static liveness analysis stale after every recompile. Static max-size slab wins in a hard-real-time system with no dynamic allocation allowed (MISRA-C, AUTOSAR).
ADR-11 Static vs dynamic execution Split: GraphExecutor (static shapes) + tc-vm Interpreter (control flow / dynamic shapes) Single executor handling both; VM-only Static path: ahead-of-time MemoryPlan, no per-op branching → max throughput. VM: registers + If/Goto/Call, runtime alloc for Dim::Dynamic. Same compiled Kernels dispatched by both. Unified path pays dynamic cost on static graphs. VM-only wins when >50% of ops in the workload have data-dependent output shapes (NLP with dynamic sequence lengths, sparse graphs) and the static executor would require worst-case-shape over-allocation that exceeds device RAM.
ADR-12 Model frontend ONNX-subset importer + textual graph format Full ONNX op set; Python frontend / eager API Documented op subset (matches GraphOp registry) is tractable + testable; textual format = readable fixtures + golden tests. Full ONNX = hundreds of ops, off-scope. Python frontend = FFI + interpreter dep, deferred. Full ONNX wins when the goal is a production inference server accepting arbitrary customer models (ResNets, Transformers, detection heads) — a subset importer rejects valid models and becomes a business blocker. Python eager API wins for a research framework where users expect NumPy-like syntax.
ADR-13 Correctness validation External NumPy-style reference oracle, f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6 Self-checking (compiler validates itself); property-only Independent oracle catches compiler and spec bugs (self-check is circular). Numerical tolerance is the contract: every pass + schedule + backend must match oracle. Differential + fuzz testing layered on top (Phase 13). Self-checking wins when the oracle itself is unavailable on the target (embedded device with no Python/NumPy) and the only option is comparing two independent code paths generated by the same compiler at different optimization levels.
ADR-14 Auto-tuner approach Cost model = analytical + measured, guided search over schedule primitives Exhaustive grid search; pure-analytical; pure-measured (AutoTVM-style only) Analytical prunes obviously bad configs cheaply; measured ranks survivors on hardware → far fewer trials than exhaustive (combinatorial blowup). Pure-analytical inaccurate; pure-measured too slow. Pure-measured (AutoTVM-style) wins when a novel accelerator has no accurate analytical model and the hardware team cannot provide roofline numbers — measurement is the only ground truth. Exhaustive grid wins in a tiny fixed schedule space (e.g., 3 tile sizes × 2 vectorize widths) where the grid fits in a 30-second tuning budget.
ADR-15 Tensor layout model NDArray = strided view (shape + strides + offset over ref-counted Storage); zero-copy slice/reshape/transpose Contiguous-only tensors; copy-on-view Strides enable zero-copy reshape/transpose/slicing + layout transforms without allocation. Contiguous-only forces copies on every layout op. Ref-counted Storage shares buffers safely. Contiguous-only wins on a DMA-based accelerator that requires physically contiguous buffers for every op and where strided access triggers a hardware fault — copy-on-view is mandatory, not optional.
ADR-16 DType set F32, F64, I32, I64, I8, Bool Add F16/BF16/complex/quantized now; F32-only Covers inference math + indices + masks/bools with bounded codegen surface. F16/BF16/quant deferred (codegen + accumulation complexity). F32-only blocks integer indexing + bool predicates. F16/BF16 wins when targeting modern GPU or NPU hardware where the hardware's peak throughput is measured in BF16 TFLOPS and F32 throughput is 2–4× lower (NVIDIA A100, Apple Neural Engine).
ADR-17 Float reassociation policy Deterministic: reassociation only under explicit fast-math opt-in; default preserves evaluation order Always allow reassoc; never allow Determinism = bit-reproducible runs + stable oracle comparison; FP add/mul non-associative ⇒ uncontrolled reassoc breaks tolerance + reproducibility. Opt-in fast-math for performance when tolerance permits. Always-allow-reassoc wins when throughput is the only objective and bit-reproducibility is explicitly waived (e.g., HPC climate simulation ensemble runs where statistical error dwarfs FP rounding, and the 20% SIMD speedup from reassoc is load-bearing).
ADR-18 Core IR crate environment no_std + alloc for tc-graph and tc-ir Full std everywhere IR crates are pure data + transforms → portable to constrained/embedded compile hosts + faster compile; std confined to runtime/codegen/cli where I/O + threads + cc invocation live. Full std wins if the IR crates must use HashMap with a random-seed DoS-hardened hasher (requires std::collections::HashMap), spawn threads for parallel IR transforms, or link against platform APIs available only in std (e.g., mmap on POSIX via std::fs).
ADR-19 Benchmarking harness criterion for kernels/passes/runtime; GFLOP/s + % of naive baseline reported std::time ad-hoc; nightly #[bench] Statistically rigorous (outlier detection, regression tracking) on stable Rust. Targets: report GFLOP/s + speedup vs naive nested-loop baseline per Phase 13. std::time wins when integrating with an external performance regression database that expects raw timing JSON and criterion's HTML reports are discarded; or in a bare-metal test harness that cannot run cargo at all.
ADR-20 Fuzzing Generator-based: cargo-fuzz over graph + schedule generators (must not crash; must stay within tolerance) No fuzzing; manual edge cases only Random valid graphs + random valid schedules surface pass/codegen/VM crashes + numerical divergence. Schedule fuzzer directly stresses ADR-05 invariant (schedule ≠ numerics change). Manual edge-case testing wins in a regulated safety domain (IEC 61508, ISO 26262) where the fuzzer's random seed and coverage path must be fully auditable and reproducible — random fuzzing is treated as non-deterministic and cannot be cited in the safety case.
ADR-21 CI GitHub Actions: fmt + clippy (deny warnings) + test + MSRV check + fuzz smoke + bench-no-regress None; other CI Standard, free for the repo; matrix over default + llvm feature; MSRV job pins 1.78; gates merges on oracle conformance. Self-hosted CI wins when build machines must have pinned GPU hardware for kernel benchmark tests or when data residency requirements prohibit sending source code to GitHub's cloud runners.
ADR-22 MSRV policy Pin 1.78; bump only with changelog entry + CI MSRV-check enforcement Track latest stable; no policy Predictable for downstream + reproducible builds; CI fails on accidental newer-stdlib use. Track-latest wins in a rapid-prototype repo where compiler features (async generators, let-else, impl Trait in fn ptrs) are the primary driver and downstream consumers all control their own toolchain.
ADR-23 Dependency policy Minimal deps: only criterion (dev), cargo-fuzz (dev), feature-gated LLVM, system cc; no heavy runtime deps Pull BLAS/ndarray/serde-everywhere Self-contained learning + audit surface; NDArray/Arena/parser written in-tree. BLAS would mask our own kernel performance (the point is to generate competitive kernels). BLAS/MKL wins in a production inference server where performance SLAs are non-negotiable and the project goal is running models fast (not generating kernels as a learning exercise).
ADR-24 Pass infrastructure PassManager + Pass trait with declared analysis/transform + a verifier (every node typed post-pass) Hardcoded fixed pass pipeline; ad-hoc function calls Composable ordering, dependency-aware scheduling, MLIR-style. Verifier after each transform catches IR corruption early (untyped node ⇒ hard error). Hardcoded pipeline wins when the pass order is provably optimal and fixed (e.g., a minimal AOT compiler for a single-op ASIC with exactly 3 transforms), and the PassManager's dynamism and verifier overhead add measurable compile-time cost.
ADR-25 Compiled artifact boundary Module { kernels, graph, MemoryPlan } as the single load/run interface Pass loose kernel list around; in-memory-only Stable serialization + load boundary between compiler and both runtimes; MemoryPlan travels with kernels so executor/VM need no recomputation. In-memory-only wins in a JIT hot loop where serialization roundtrip latency is unacceptable (e.g., per-batch recompilation in a streaming pipeline where the compile→run turnaround must be sub-millisecond).
ADR-26 GPU / CUDA codegen Deferred (Scope::Shared, IterKind::Thread, Device::Gpu reserved but unimplemented) Implement now; remove the hooks CPU-only scope (GPU = projects 248/180); reserving enum variants keeps the type surface forward-compatible without dead code paths shipping. Implement-GPU-now wins if the primary use case is large transformer inference where GPU throughput is 10–50× CPU and CPU-only benchmarks are meaningless for the intended hardware tier.
ADR-27 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (dual) GPL; proprietary; single license Permissive ecosystem-standard dual license (matches Rust + TVM-adjacent norms); maximal downstream reuse. GPL wins when the project's sustainability model depends on copyleft reciprocity to prevent proprietary forks from extracting value without contributing back (e.g., a compiler foundation that competes with commercial vendors). Proprietary wins when the compiler is the core IP of a commercial product.

10. Repository Layout

Single Cargo workspace, nine tc-* member crates under crates/, governance + conformance + fuzz + docs at root. Directory layout mirrors the 14-phase plan: each crate file maps to a phase deliverable (Phase 1→tc-graph, Phase 6→tc-ir, Phases 7–8→tc-schedule, Phase 9→tc-codegen, Phase 10→tc-runtime, Phase 11→tc-vm). no_std + alloc crates: tc-graph, tc-ir (compiler core stays allocation-portable); std crates: tc-codegen (spawns cc), tc-runtime, tc-vm, tc-cli.

        • fuzz_graph.rsarbitrary well-typed Graph → passes must not panic
        • fuzz_schedule.rsrandom legal schedule → numerically equal to naive
        • fuzz_importer.rsmalformed textfmt/ONNX-subset bytes → no panic
        • fuzz_codegen.rsrandom scheduled TIR → emitted-C compiles + numeric-correct
        • fuzz_vm.rsrandom VmFunc bytecode → no panic, bounded, no OOB

10.3 Layout invariants

Invariant Rule
Dependency DAG crate deps follow the pipeline arrow; no cycles; tc-graph/tc-ir depend on nothing internal
no_std boundary tc-graph, tc-ir build --no-default-features (alloc only); CI gate enforces it
One concept per file each module file owns exactly the types named in §11 Core Schema and Core Types; no cross-file type defs
Phase ↔ file map every Phase 1–13 deliverable lands in the file annotated above; BUILD_PLAN.md is authoritative
Oracle isolation tools/oracle-gen (Python) is build-time only; never a runtime dep of any tc-* crate
Generated code emitted C lives in a temp build dir at compile time, never committed; templates/kernel.c.tmpl is the only checked-in C scaffold
Test data conformance fixtures + oracle-data/*.npz are committed (small) or regenerated via scripts/gen_oracle.sh

11. Core Schema and Core Types

Normative API surface the phases build against. Every type below is canonical (locked spine). Signatures are illustrative (1-2 line bodies max); full bodies live in the phase deliverables. Grouped by crate, in workspace dependency order. Cross-cutting contracts (compute/schedule separation, KernelSig ABI) are called out at the end.

11.1 Ownership / mutation / concurrency model (cross-crate)

Concern Rule
IR ownership Arena-allocated nodes + typed ids (NodeId, BufferId, VarId, AxisId, StageId). No Box/Rc graph spaghetti; ids are Copy, cycle-free, u32 indices into an Arena<T>.
IR mutation Passes take &Graph/&PrimFunc, produce new/modified arenas; never mutate in place across a pass boundary. Each transform pass is Graph -> Graph. Verifier gates output.
Compile-phase concurrency Single-threaded. Arenas are !Sync by intent; no locks in the compiler. Determinism is required (same input -> byte-identical Module).
Runtime concurrency Storage is ref-counted + Send; kernels run parallel over tiles via a thread pool (parallel axis). NDArray views share Storage; views are Send when storage is.
Persistence Module is THE serialized artifact (kernels + graph + MemoryPlan). Per-stage dump formats are text, for inspection/diff/test only, not the runtime contract.
Compatibility Op registry is extensible (GraphOp::Call(String) escape hatch); adding ops/passes is additive. KernelSig ABI is the stable boundary between codegen and runtime.

Typed-id newtypes (all crates):

pub struct NodeId(pub u32);    // index into Graph.nodes arena
pub struct BufferId(pub u32);  // index into a PrimFunc buffer table
pub struct VarId(pub u32);     // SSA-ish loop/scalar var
pub struct AxisId(pub u32);    // a loop axis within a Schedule
pub struct StageId(pub u32);   // a compute stage within a Schedule

11.2 tc-graph — high-level Graph IR (Relay-like)

no_std + alloc. A functional dataflow graph of whole-tensor ops. Nodes are immutable post-construction; passes rebuild.

/// The high-level dataflow graph. Arena of nodes + designated I/O.
pub struct Graph {
    pub nodes: Arena<GraphNode>,
    pub outputs: Vec<NodeId>,   // graph result tensors
    pub inputs: Vec<NodeId>,    // placeholders (GraphOp::Input)
}

/// One operator application. `ty` is None until shape inference fills it.
pub struct GraphNode {
    pub op: GraphOp,
    pub args: Vec<NodeId>,      // dataflow operands, DAG edges (acyclic)
    pub ty: Option<TensorType>, // inferred TensorType, verifier-gated
}

/// Operator set. Extensible: `Call(String)` dispatches the op registry.
pub enum GraphOp {
    Input(String),       // named placeholder
    Const(TensorData),   // baked literal tensor
    Add, Mul,            // elementwise (broadcasting)
    MatMul,              // 2D contraction
    Conv2d,              // NCHW default, attrs in registry
    Relu,                // elementwise max(0,x)
    Reshape, Transpose,  // layout/view ops
    Reduce(ReduceKind),  // Sum/Max/Min/Mean over axes
    Call(String),        // registry-resolved extension op
}

/// Full tensor type: static-or-symbolic shape + element dtype.
pub struct TensorType { pub shape: Shape, pub dtype: DType }

pub struct Shape(pub Vec<Dim>);
pub enum Dim {
    Static(i64),       // known extent
    Dynamic(String),   // symbolic dim, resolved at runtime by the VM
}

pub enum DType { F32, F64, I32, I64, I8, Bool }
  • Invariant: args form a DAG; no cycles, no dangling NodeId. Verified by tc-pass.
  • ty == None is legal only pre-shape-inference; the verifier requires every reachable node typed before fusion.
  • Op registry (separate table keyed by op name for Call): holds shape rule, type rule, lowering rule, attrs schema. Adding an op = registering these; no enum edit needed for Call ops.
  • TensorData (in Const): owned dense buffer + TensorType; the only owned data inside the graph.
  • Importers: textual graph format + ONNX-subset -> Graph. ONNX op coverage is a documented subset (non-goal: full op set).

11.3 tc-ir — low-level tensor/loop IR (TIR-like)

no_std + alloc. Imperative loop-nest IR over Buffers. One PrimFunc per fused group.

/// A lowered, schedulable kernel function: typed buffer params + a loop-nest body.
pub struct PrimFunc { pub params: Vec<Buffer>, pub body: Stmt }

/// Statement IR. `For` carries an IterVar; `Allocate` reserves a Buffer.
pub enum Stmt {
    For { var: IterVar, min: Expr, extent: Expr, body: Box<Stmt> },
    Store { buffer: BufferId, index: Expr, value: Expr },
    Block(Vec<Stmt>),
    IfThenElse { cond: Expr, then: Box<Stmt>, els: Option<Box<Stmt>> },
    Allocate { buffer: BufferId },
}

/// Scalar/index expression IR. Pure; no side effects except via Store.
pub enum Expr {
    Load { buffer: BufferId, index: Box<Expr> },
    Var(VarId),
    Const(f64),
    Bin { op: BinOp, lhs: Box<Expr>, rhs: Box<Expr> },
    Call(IntrinId, Vec<Expr>),   // intrinsics: max, exp, fma, etc.
}

/// A typed n-D memory region; shape is symbolic (Vec<Expr>) for dynamic dims.
pub struct Buffer { pub id: BufferId, pub shape: Vec<Expr>, pub dtype: DType, pub scope: Scope }

/// A loop iteration variable + its semantic role.
pub struct IterVar { pub var: VarId, pub kind: IterKind } // Data, Reduce, Thread(deferred)

/// Memory scope. Shared/Thread reserved for the deferred GPU path.
pub enum Scope { Global, Local, Shared }
  • TensorExpr (declarative compute spec, lowered from a GraphOp group): defines WHAT is computed (output buffer = f(input loads) over an iteration domain + reduce axes). The default lowering emits a naive nested-loop PrimFunc.
  • BinOp: Add/Sub/Mul/Div/Max/Min/Mod/CmpLt/.. ; IntrinId: exp/log/sqrt/max/fma/.. registered intrinsics codegen maps to C/SIMD.
  • IterKind: Data (spatial, parallelizable/vectorizable), Reduce (carries an accumulator, NOT freely reorderable across data axes), Thread (deferred GPU).
  • Mutation: schedule transforms operate on a Schedule wrapping the PrimFunc, not the PrimFunc directly; the PrimFunc is the unscheduled reference.
  • Builder: IrBuilder constructs Stmt/Expr trees + a buffer table; the only sanctioned construction path (keeps BufferId/VarId allocation consistent).

11.4 tc-pass — pass infrastructure

Depends on tc-graph + tc-ir. Hosts analysis + transform passes and the verifier.

/// A compiler pass over the Graph IR. Transform passes return a new Graph.
pub trait Pass {
    fn name(&self) -> &str;
    fn run(&self, g: Graph) -> Result<Graph, PassError>;
    fn invalidates(&self) -> &[AnalysisKind]; // which cached analyses this pass dirties
}

/// Ordered pipeline + analysis cache + verifier gate.
pub struct PassManager { /* Vec<Box<dyn Pass>> + analysis cache */ }
impl PassManager {
    pub fn add(&mut self, p: Box<dyn Pass>);
    pub fn run(&self, g: Graph) -> Result<Graph, PassError>; // verifier runs after each pass
}
  • Passes (Phases 2-5): ShapeInference, TypeCheck, ConstFold, Cse, Dce, AlgebraicSimplify, LayoutTransform.
  • Analysis vs transform: analysis passes populate the cache (shape/type, dominator tree); transform passes consume + rebuild + declare invalidates().
  • Verifier: after every transform pass, asserts well-formedness (DAG, all nodes typed, dtype consistency, no dangling ids). A failing verifier aborts compilation with a PassError (no silent miscompile).
  • Shape inference: propagates TensorType to a fixed point; Dim::Dynamic stays symbolic (resolved at runtime by the VM).

11.5 tc-fuse — operator fusion

Depends on tc-graph + tc-ir. Dominator-based grouping; the dominant CPU win.

/// The fusion pass: groups ops, then lowers each group to one PrimFunc.
pub struct FusionPass { /* fusion rules + dominator analysis */ }

/// A maximal fusible subgraph (one elementwise/injective chain + ≤1 reduce).
pub struct FusionGroup {
    pub nodes: Vec<NodeId>,  // member graph nodes, in topo order
    pub root: NodeId,        // group output node
}
  • Algorithm: build the dominator tree over the dataflow graph; merge injective/elementwise producers into their consumer when the consumer dominates all uses; absorb a single Reduce as the group epilogue. Eliminates intermediate materialization.
  • Output: each FusionGroup lowers (via tc-ir's TensorExpr lowering) to one PrimFunc -> one Kernel.
  • Fusion rules keyed by op pattern: elementwise+elementwise, elementwise+reduce, inject-into-conv-epilogue (e.g. Conv2d then Relu). Rules are data, extensible alongside the op registry.

11.6 tc-schedule — the schedule space

Depends on tc-ir. Wraps a PrimFunc; primitives transform loop structure only.

/// A PrimFunc + the ordered loop transforms applied to it.
pub struct Schedule { pub func: PrimFunc, pub stages: Vec<Stage> }

/// One compute stage's loop axes under transformation.
pub struct Stage { pub axes: Vec<IterVar> /* loop structure under transform */ }

impl Schedule {
    pub fn split(&mut self, ax: AxisId, factor: i64) -> (AxisId, AxisId); // 1 loop -> outer,inner
    pub fn reorder(&mut self, order: &[AxisId]);                          // permute loop nest
    pub fn fuse(&mut self, a: AxisId, b: AxisId) -> AxisId;               // 2 loops -> 1
    pub fn tile(&mut self, ax: AxisId, ay: AxisId, fx: i64, fy: i64);     // 2D blocking
    pub fn vectorize(&mut self, ax: AxisId);                              // axis -> SIMD lanes
    pub fn unroll(&mut self, ax: AxisId);                                 // fully unroll
    pub fn parallel(&mut self, ax: AxisId);                               // axis -> thread pool
    pub fn compute_at(&mut self, stage: StageId, ax: AxisId);            // nest producer under consumer axis
    pub fn inline(&mut self, stage: StageId);                            // fold producer into consumer
}
  • Compute/schedule separation contract (LOCKED, normative): a Schedule may ONLY change loop order, blocking, lane/thread mapping, and materialization. It MUST NOT change the numerical result of func. Any primitive that would alter results is a bug, gated by conformance (§25).
  • Reduce safety: vectorize/parallel on a Reduce axis is rejected unless the reduction is associative + a partial-accumulator split is emitted; reorder may not move a data axis inside its dependent reduce axis.
  • Validity: split/tile require divisibility or emit a tail/IfThenElse guard; compute_at/inline require a producer-consumer edge. Invalid primitive sequences error at schedule-apply time, before codegen.

11.7 tc-codegen — lowering scheduled TIR to target code

Depends on tc-ir + tc-schedule. Produces the Module artifact.

/// A compile target: arch + ISA features that gate SIMD codegen.
pub struct Target { pub arch: String, pub features: Vec<String> } // e.g. "x86_64", ["avx2"]

/// Scheduled-TIR -> source backend. CBackend (scalar C) + SimdBackend (portable SIMD).
pub trait CodeGen { fn emit(&self, sched: &Schedule, target: &Target) -> Kernel; }

/// One emitted kernel: name, source text, and its stable ABI signature.
pub struct Kernel { pub name: String, pub source: String, pub signature: KernelSig }

/// The compiled, serializable artifact loaded by the runtime.
pub struct Module { pub kernels: Vec<Kernel>, pub graph: Graph, pub plan: MemoryPlan }

KernelSig — the stable ABI boundary between codegen and runtime:

/// Stable C ABI per kernel. Buffers passed as raw pointers + shape/stride metadata.
pub struct KernelSig {
    pub params: Vec<ParamDesc>,  // ordered: inputs then outputs
}
pub struct ParamDesc {
    pub name: String,
    pub dtype: DType,
    pub rank: usize,
    pub role: ParamRole,         // Input | Output | Scalar
}
  • ABI: every emitted C kernel is void name(<dtype>* p0, .., int64_t* shapes, int64_t* strides); the runtime binds NDArray.storage.ptr + shape + strides positionally per KernelSig. The signature is the ONLY contract; backends may change codegen internals freely.
  • Backends: CBackend always available (compiled via system cc); SimdBackend emits portable SIMD intrinsics gated by Target.features; an LLVM path is feature-flagged, off by default.
  • Module serialization: kernels (source or compiled blobs) + the optimized Graph + MemoryPlan; this is the persisted artifact tcc compile writes and tcc run loads. Dump formats (graph/tir/scheduled/c/module) are derived views for inspection.

11.8 tc-runtime — NDArray, allocation, executor

Depends on tc-codegen. CPU-only (Device::Gpu deferred).

/// A strided, zero-copy tensor view over ref-counted Storage.
pub struct NDArray {
    pub storage: Storage,
    pub shape: Vec<i64>,
    pub strides: Vec<i64>,  // element strides; enables zero-copy slice/reshape/transpose
    pub dtype: DType,
    pub offset: i64,        // element offset into storage
}

/// A ref-counted device buffer. Send; the unit of memory ownership.
pub struct Storage { ptr: *mut u8, bytes: usize, device: Device }

pub enum Device { Cpu, Gpu /* deferred */ }

/// Pluggable allocation strategy (bump, pool, system).
pub trait Allocator {
    fn alloc(&mut self, bytes: usize) -> Storage;
    fn free(&mut self, s: Storage);
}

/// Liveness-based buffer reuse: which intermediate tensors share Storage.
pub struct MemoryPlan { /* tensor -> storage-slot assignment from liveness analysis */ }

/// Executes a Module's kernels in topological order over NDArray bindings.
pub struct GraphExecutor { /* Module + NDArray binding table + Allocator */ }
  • NDArray semantics: views share Storage (clone = ref-count bump, zero-copy); reshape/slice/transpose produce new views without copying when stride-compatible. Non-contiguous views materialize on demand for kernels that require contiguity.
  • Storage: ref-counted (clone bumps, drop decrements + frees at zero); Send so storage crosses thread boundaries for parallel kernels. Raw ptr is encapsulated; safe API only.
  • MemoryPlan: computed at compile time over the executor's topo order; reuses storage for tensors with non-overlapping liveness (register-allocation-on-tensors). Reduces peak memory; stored in the Module.
  • GraphExecutor: static shapes only; binds inputs, allocates per the MemoryPlan, dispatches each Kernel via its KernelSig, returns output NDArrays. Async dispatch overlaps independent kernels.

11.9 tc-vm — bytecode VM for dynamic graphs

Depends on tc-runtime. Handles control flow + dynamic shapes the GraphExecutor cannot.

/// Register-machine instruction set over NDArray/object registers.
pub enum VmInstruction {
    LoadConst(RegId, ConstId),
    AllocTensor(RegId, ShapeRef),          // runtime shape, resolves Dim::Dynamic
    InvokeKernel(KernelId, Vec<RegId>, RegId), // dispatch a compiled Kernel
    If(RegId, Label, Label),
    Goto(Label),
    Call(FuncId, Vec<RegId>, RegId),       // closures / sub-functions
    Ret(RegId),
}

/// A VM function body + its register frame size.
pub struct VmFunc { pub instrs: Vec<VmInstruction>, pub num_regs: usize }

/// The register-machine interpreter: register file + call stack.
pub struct Interpreter { /* Vec<NDArray|Object> registers, call stack of frames */ }
  • Same kernels: the VM dispatches the identical compiled Kernels as the GraphExecutor; the only difference is runtime allocation + control flow. Closure captures a VmFunc + bound registers.
  • Dynamic shapes: AllocTensor resolves Dim::Dynamic from runtime register values; InvokeKernel rebinds shapes/strides per call.
  • Object model: registers hold NDArray or boxed objects (tuples/closures); ref-counted, freed at frame exit.

11.10 tc-cli — tcc binary

Thin orchestration over all crates. Subcommands: compile (model -> Module), run (execute on inputs), dump (graph/tir/scheduled/c/module), tune (schedule search), check (vs the NumPy-style oracle), bench (GFLOP/s vs naive baseline). No new core types; composes the above.

11.11 Normative contracts summary

Contract Statement
Compute/schedule separation Schedule primitives change performance only; numerical output is invariant. Conformance enforces this.
KernelSig ABI The sole stable boundary between tc-codegen and tc-runtime/tc-vm; pointer + shape + stride positional binding.
Verifier gate Every transform pass output is re-verified (DAG, typed, dtype-consistent); failure aborts, never miscompiles.
Determinism Compilation is single-threaded + deterministic: same input -> byte-identical Module.
