Numerical oracle Compiled output matches reference within tolerance (f32 rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6); schedule changes must not exceed it.
Memory safety Raw ptr lives only inside Storage; ref-counting governs lifetime; Storage: Send enables parallel kernels.

12. Phase 0 — Workspace, end-to-end "add" smoke through the whole pipeline

Bootstrap the nine-crate workspace and drive ONE thin slice through every stage: add(a,b) Graph → hand-lowered PrimFunc → CBackend C kernel → cc compile → Module load → GraphExecutor run on two NDArrays → assert vs in-Rust reference within tolerance. All stubs todo!() except the slice path. No fusion/schedule/pass logic yet — those are vertical-deferred to later phases; here we prove the seams line up.

0.1 Objectives

  • Cargo workspace tensor-compiler/ + 9 member crates (tc-graph, tc-ir, tc-pass, tc-fuse, tc-schedule, tc-codegen, tc-runtime, tc-vm, tc-cli) wired with exact dep DAG; cargo build --workspace green.
  • Define type shells: Graph/GraphNode/GraphOp, PrimFunc/Stmt/Expr/Buffer, NDArray/Storage, stubbed PassManager / CodeGen / GraphExecutor.
  • CBackend emits a compilable C function for the elementwise-add PrimFunc; cc produces a .so/.dylib; dlopen into Module.
  • Reference oracle harness scaffold (oracle::add) + tolerance comparator (assert_close, f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6).
  • tcc binary skeleton with subcommands parsed (compile/run/dump/tune/check/bench); only the add smoke wired end-to-end.
  • CI (fmt → clippy -D warnings → build → test), Makefile, Dockerfile, all reproducible.

0.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

  • Workspace (ADR-02 nine-crate decomposition; ADR-01 Rust/no_std split): root Cargo.toml [workspace] members=[9 crates] resolver="2"; pin MSRV 1.78 via rust-toolchain.toml; [workspace.dependencies] for shared (bumpalo arena, libloading, anyhow, clap).
  • tc-graph (no_std+alloc): arena.rs (Arena<T> over Vec<T> + NodeId(u32) newtype); graph.rs Graph{nodes,inputs,outputs}, GraphNode{op,args,ty}, GraphOp enum (full variant list, only Input/Const/Add reachable now); ty.rs TensorType/Shape/Dim/DType. Builder helper Graph::add_node(op,args)->NodeId.
  • tc-ir (no_std+alloc): prim.rs PrimFunc{params:Vec<Buffer>,body:Stmt}; stmt.rs Stmt(For/Store/Block/IfThenElse/Allocate); expr.rs Expr(Load/Var/Const/Bin/Call), BinOp; buffer.rs Buffer{id,shape,dtype,scope}, BufferId/VarId/IntrinId newtypes, IterVar/IterKind/Scope.
  • tc-codegen: target.rs Target{arch,features} (default Target::host()); codegen.rs trait CodeGen{fn emit(&self,&Schedule,&Target)->Kernel}; cbackend.rs CBackend — walk Stmt/Expr → C string (For→for(i=0;i<N;i++), Store→buf[idx]=…, Bin(Add)→a+b); Kernel{name,source,signature}, KernelSig{params:Vec<(name,DType,ndim)>}; module.rs Module{kernels,graph,plan} + Module::compile_and_load(cc) -> dlopen handle.
  • tc-runtime: ndarray.rs NDArray{storage,shape,strides,dtype,offset} + NDArray::new_contiguous, as_ptr; storage.rs Storage{ptr,bytes,device} Rc-counted, BumpAllocator: Allocator; executor.rs GraphExecutor::run(&Module,&[NDArray])->Vec<NDArray> (Phase 0: single-kernel dlsym + call); oracle.rs reference ops + assert_close.
  • tc-pass/tc-fuse/tc-schedule/tc-vm: type shells + todo!() method bodies; tc-schedule::Schedule{func,stages} real enough that codegen accepts a trivial (identity-scheduled) PrimFunc.
  • tc-cli: clap derive, dispatch to dump(graph/tir/scheduled/c/module — Phase 0: graph + c only), others unimplemented!() with a clear message.
  • CodeGen→cc bridge: emit kernel_add(const float* a,const float* b,float* out,int64_t n); cc -O2 -shared -fPIC -o lib.so; libloading::Library + Symbol.

0.3 Deliverable

cargo test -p tc-runtime smoke_add builds the 2-node graph, emits C, compiles via cc, loads, runs on two length-N f32 NDArrays, and passes assert_close vs oracle::add. tcc dump --c add.graph prints the emitted kernel. make ci green in the Docker image.

0.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo build --workspace clean, 0 warnings
cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings passes
cargo fmt --check clean
smoke_add numerical max abs/rel err ≤ atol 1e-6 / rtol 1e-5, N=1024
End-to-end smoke wall-time < 3 s incl. cc invocation
cc emitted-kernel compile exit 0, no warnings under -Wall
tcc dump --c deterministic byte-identical C across runs
Docker make ci reproducible green from clean clone

0.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
fixtures/add_2node.graph textual: %a=input;%b=input;%c=add(%a,%b);out %c parses → 3-node Graph
fixtures/add.expected.c golden emitted kernel byte-match tcc dump --c
fixtures/add_in.npy{a,b} two f32[1024] out = a+b vs oracle
malformed/add_arity.graph add(%a) (1 arg) error add expects 2 args, got 1
malformed/dtype_mismatch.graph add(f32,i32) error dtype mismatch f32/i32
malformed/empty.graph no out error graph has no outputs

0.6 Pitfalls

  • C↔Rust ABI drift: KernelSig ndim/dtype must match emitted C signature exactly; mismatch = silent UB/segfault. Root cause: hand-kept-in-sync sig. Debug: assert KernelSig vs parsed C prototype in test; run under ASan.
  • cc not on PATH / wrong compiler: smoke hangs or errors opaquely. Resolve CC env → fallback cc; surface the full failing command + stderr.
  • dlopen symbol mangling: kernel must be extern "C" / no mangling; missing → dlsym null. Verify with nm lib.so.
  • Strided NDArray assumed contiguous: Phase-0 kernel indexes buf[i]; a non-contiguous input gives wrong results silently. Guard: debug_assert!(nd.is_contiguous()) at dispatch.
  • Arena NodeId stale after realloc: store ids, never &GraphNode; index through Arena.
  • f32 compare with ==: use assert_close; never exact equality post-cc -O2 (FMA reassociation).

0.7 Performance Concerns

  • Phase-0 perf is NOT a goal beyond the < 3 s smoke budget; cc invocation dominates (~0.5–1 s). Cache compiled .so keyed by source hash to keep iteration fast.
  • Avoid per-element FFI; one kernel_add call over the whole buffer. Establish the "one kernel call per fused group" contract early.
  • Set baseline: record add throughput (GB/s memory-bound) so later SIMD phases (8/9) measure against it.

0.8 Edge Cases

  • N=0 (empty tensor) → loop runs 0×, valid empty output.
  • N=1 (scalar-ish) → no vectorization assumptions break.
  • a aliases out (in-place) → Phase-0 forbids; document, assert distinct ptrs.
  • DType other than F32 in smoke → reject with explicit "Phase 0 supports F32 only".
  • Mixed shapes (broadcast) → out of scope; require identical shapes, error otherwise.

0.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
cc compile fail nonzero exit dump emitted C + stderr to target/tcc/last_fail.c
dlopen/dlsym fail libloading Err report .so path + symbol name
Numerical mismatch assert_close print first-K diverging indices + actual/expected/err
Parse error importer Result line/col + offending token
Panic in stub todo!() message names the unimplemented method + phase that fills it

0.10 Operational Notes

  • CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml): matrix linux/macos; steps fmt → clippy -D warnings → build --workspace → test --workspace; cache ~/.cargo + target.
  • Makefile: build, test, fmt, clippy, ci (= fmt+clippy+build+test), smoke, docker.
  • Dockerfile: rust:1.78-slim + build-essential (provides cc); make ci as build step → reproducible.
  • Determinism: emitted C must be byte-stable (sorted node iteration, no HashMap ordering) so goldens hold.
  • Layout: tensor-compiler/{Cargo.toml, rust-toolchain.toml, Makefile, Dockerfile, crates/tc-*/, fixtures/, oracle/}.
  • Versioning: workspace version 0.0.0; tag phase0 on green smoke.

13. Phase 1 — Graph IR, op registry, type/shape system, and importers

1.1 Objectives

  • Build tc-graph core: Graph/GraphNode/GraphOp, TensorType/Shape/Dim/DType, arena-backed NodeId.
  • Extensible op registry: each op declares arity, attrs, dtype constraints, shape-infer fn, reference-eval fn (used later as oracle hook).
  • Two importers: (a) textual graph format parser (round-trippable), (b) ONNX-subset importer mapping ONNX NodeProto → GraphOp.
  • Produce a typed-or-untyped Graph (types Option<TensorType>; full inference is Phase 2) that tcc dump --graph renders.
  • Invariant: import is total over the documented subset; unknown ops → typed error, never panic.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Modules under tc-graph/src/:

File Contents
graph.rs Graph{nodes:Arena<GraphNode>,inputs,outputs:Vec<NodeId>}, add_node, topo_order(), cycle check
node.rs GraphNode{op,args:Vec<NodeId>,ty:Option<TensorType>}, NodeId(u32)
op.rs GraphOp enum (Input/Const/Add/Mul/MatMul/Conv2d/Relu/Reshape/Transpose/Reduce(ReduceKind)/Call(String)); ReduceKind{Sum,Max,Mean}
ty.rs TensorType{shape:Shape,dtype:DType}, Shape(Vec<Dim>), Dim::{Static(i64),Dynamic(String)}, DType::{F32,F64,I32,I64,I8,Bool}; DType::bytes()
registry.rs OpRegistry, OpSpec{name,arity:Arity,attrs,infer:fn,dtype_rule}, register(), lookup()
arena.rs typed Arena<T> (Vec + generation-free Id); no_std + alloc
text/parse.rs text/print.rs textual format reader/writer (round-trip)
onnx/import.rs ONNX ModelProto decode (minimal protobuf reader) → Graph
  • Op registry (ADR-04 arena+typed ids; paper P6 Relay typed graph IR §5.2): static table built at init; register(OpSpec); importers + Phase-2 inference query it. Adding an op = one OpSpec, no enum-match edits elsewhere (except the GraphOp variant).
  • Const folding hook: Const(TensorData) carries raw bytes + TensorType; TensorData{dtype,shape,bytes:Vec<u8>}.
  • ONNX subset map (ADR-12 ONNX-subset importer; onnx/import.rs): Add→Add, Mul→Mul, MatMul/Gemm→MatMul, Conv→Conv2d, Relu→Relu, Reshape→Reshape, Transpose→Transpose, ReduceSum/Max/Mean→Reduce(_); initializers→Const; graph inputs→Input(name). Attrs (strides/pads/dilations/perm/axes) stored on node attr-map.
  • Cycle detection: topo_order() returns Err(GraphError::Cycle) (ONNX is a DAG; defensive).

1.3 Deliverable

tcc dump --graph <model> parses a textual .tcg file OR a .onnx (subset) into a Graph and prints canonical textual form. tc-graph builds no_std+alloc; importers gated behind std feature for file IO.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Round-trip: parse(print(g)) == g (structural eq) for all textual fixtures; byte-identical print after one normalize pass.
  • ONNX import of a ≥6-node model (matmul+add+relu) yields the expected node count, op kinds, edges, and Const initializer bytes.
  • Unknown ONNX op or arity mismatch → GraphError with op name + node index; zero panics under fuzzed inputs (Phase 13 ties in).
  • cargo build -p tc-graph --no-default-features succeeds (no_std).

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Expected
fixtures/text/add2.tcg 2 inputs + Add → 1 output; round-trips byte-identical
fixtures/text/mlp.tcg MatMul→Add→Relu; topo order stable
fixtures/onnx/gemm_relu.onnx imports to MatMul,Add,Relu; 1 Const (weights)
fixtures/onnx/conv_small.onnx Conv2d w/ strides=[1,1] pads=[0,0,0,0] attrs preserved
fixtures/bad/unknown_op.onnx Err(UnknownOp("LSTM"))
fixtures/bad/cyclic.tcg Err(Cycle); bad/arity.tcg → Err(Arity{op,got,want})

1.6 Pitfalls

  • ONNX initializer vs input overlap: a name in both initializer and input is a Const, not an Input — dedupe by name or you get a dangling free var. Debug: dump input/const name sets.
  • Endianness/dtype width decoding raw TensorProto.raw_data: assume little-endian, validate bytes.len() == shape_prod * dtype.bytes(); mismatch ⇒ silent garbage. Assert it at import.
  • Reshape with -1 / 0: ONNX allows inferred (-1) and copy (0) dims — store symbolically, do NOT resolve here (Phase 2). Resolving early double-resolves.
  • Gemm transA/transB/alpha/beta ≠ plain MatMul: emit Transpose/scale nodes or attrs; dropping them silently changes results.
  • Attr default vs absent (e.g. Conv dilations): missing attr ≠ zero; apply ONNX spec defaults explicitly or shape infer drifts.

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • Import is offline/one-shot; target < 50 ms for a 1k-node model. Arena Vec<GraphNode> keeps nodes contiguous; NodeId(u32) (4 B) edges, no Box per edge.
  • Avoid string-keyed maps in hot paths; intern op/attr names to small ids during import.
  • Minimal protobuf reader: streaming varint decode, no full reflection; allocate Const bytes once (no copy on round-trip).

1.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty graph (no nodes) → valid; prints # empty. Single-node Input with no consumer → valid (will hit DCE later).
  • Scalar tensor Shape(vec![]) (rank 0); fully-dynamic Shape([Dynamic("N"),Dynamic("C")]).
  • Multi-output node (e.g. future Split): model as op + index; subset here uses single-output ops only — reject multi-output ONNX with Unsupported.
  • Duplicate Input names, self-loop edge, output referencing nonexistent NodeId → typed errors.

1.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Truncated/invalid protobuf varint/length bounds check Err(MalformedOnnx{offset})
Unknown op / multi-output registry lookup miss Err(UnknownOp)/Unsupported, list supported subset
Cycle / dangling NodeId topo_order + bounds check Err(Cycle)/Err(BadRef) w/ node ids
dtype/shape byte mismatch len assert on Const Err(ConstSizeMismatch)
  • All errors are enum GraphError (thiserror-style), carry node index/op name; panics reserved for internal invariant bugs only.

1.10 Operational Notes

  • CLI: tcc dump --graph model.tcg|model.onnx [--json]; --json for tooling/tests, default canonical text.
  • Versioning: textual format carries #tcg v1 header; importer pins ONNX opset (document supported opset range, e.g. 11–17).
  • Logging: --verbose prints per-node import map (ONNX op → GraphOp) for debugging unsupported models.
  • The op registry is the extension point: new ops land via OpSpec + a GraphOp variant + fixtures; downstream phases (infer/fuse/lower) consume registry metadata, never re-hardcode op lists.

14. Phase 2 — Pass infrastructure, shape inference, and type checking

Foundational analysis layer in tc-pass. Build the PassManager/Pass machinery (ordered execution, analysis caching + invalidation), then the two passes every downstream phase assumes: shape inference (propagate TensorType to a fixed point) and type checking (dtype consistency, op arity/constraints), plus the verifier asserting every GraphNode.ty == Some(_). Output contract: a fully-typed Graph, or a precise PassError naming the offending NodeId and reason.

2.1 Objectives

  • PassManager: ordered pass pipeline; analysis-result cache keyed by AnalysisId; dependency-driven invalidation on graph mutation.
  • Pass trait: distinguish AnalysisPass (read-only, cacheable) vs TransformPass (mutates, declares invalidations).
  • ShapeInference: worklist fixed-point over dataflow; per-GraphOp type relations; Dim::Static arithmetic + Dim::Dynamic symbolic unification.
  • TypeCheck: arity, dtype-equality / promotion rules, op-specific constraints (e.g. MatMul inner-dim match, Conv2d rank=4).
  • Verifier: post-condition forall n: n.ty.is_some(); structural checks (acyclicity, arg-count, valid NodeId).
  • Determinism: pass order + iteration order fixed; identical input Graph -> identical typed Graph and identical errors.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files (under crates/tc-pass/src/):

File Contents
manager.rs PassManager, PassPipeline, AnalysisCache, run() driver
pass.rs Pass, AnalysisPass, TransformPass traits; PassError, PassResult
analysis/shape.rs ShapeInference, type-relation registry, worklist solver
analysis/typecheck.rs TypeCheck, dtype rules, op-constraint table
verify.rs Verifier, structural + typed-completeness checks
relations.rs TypeRel fn-per-op: (args: &[TensorType]) -> Result<TensorType, TypeErr>

Core interfaces (ADR-24 PassManager+Pass+verifier; theory: P5 MLIR pass-manager design):

pub trait Pass { fn name(&self) -> &str; fn invalidates(&self) -> &[AnalysisId]; }   // [] for pure analysis
pub trait AnalysisPass: Pass { type Out; fn analyze(&self, g: &Graph) -> Result<Self::Out, PassError>; }
pub trait TransformPass: Pass { fn run(&self, g: &mut Graph, c: &mut AnalysisCache) -> Result<bool, PassError>; }
pub struct PassManager { pipeline: Vec<Box<dyn TransformPass>>, cache: AnalysisCache }
impl PassManager { pub fn run(&mut self, g: &mut Graph) -> Result<(), PassError>; }
pub struct PassError { pub node: Option<NodeId>, pub pass: &'static str, pub kind: ErrKind }
pub enum ErrKind { ShapeMismatch{exp:Shape,got:Shape}, DTypeMismatch{a:DType,b:DType},
    Arity{op:&'static str,exp:usize,got:usize}, Unresolved(NodeId), Cyclic, RankErr{op:&'static str,exp:usize,got:usize}, Untyped(NodeId) }
  • Worklist shape solver (P6 §6.6 shape inference to fixed point; INV-3 §7.4): init queue = topo order; pop node, compute TypeRel from arg tys; if changed, set node.ty, push consumers. Reach fixed point. Dynamic dims unify by name; Static(a) vs Static(b) with a!=b -> ShapeMismatch. Bound iterations ≤ 2·|nodes| to detect non-convergence -> error.
  • TypeRel registry: HashMap<GraphOp-discriminant, fn>; e.g. Add/Mul: broadcast shapes, dtype must match -> elementwise; MatMul: [..,m,k]×[..,k,n] -> [..,m,n]; Conv2d: NCHW relation w/ stride/pad/dilation attrs; Reshape: product-preserving; Reduce: drop/keep reduced axes.
  • Broadcast rule: NumPy semantics — align trailing dims, each pair (1,n)|(n,1)|(n,n) ok else mismatch; one Dynamic side assumes compatible (resolved at runtime).
  • Caching: AnalysisCache: HashMap<AnalysisId, Box<dyn Any>>; TransformPass::invalidates() removes entries before/after run; shape + typecheck recomputed only on miss.
  • Verifier (P4 §6.4 passes verifiable+composable; INV-4 §7.5): runs last by default; rejects any Untyped node, arg-count vs op arity, dangling NodeId, cycle via DFS color marks.

2.3 Deliverable

tc-pass exporting PassManager with a default pipeline [ShapeInference, TypeCheck, Verifier]. tcc dump graph --typed model.tg prints every node with its inferred TensorType. A typed Graph round-trips; any ill-typed input yields a single PassError with NodeId + ErrKind. Library + fixtures compile under the workspace; no codegen/runtime dependency.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Every node in a valid corpus Graph has ty = Some(_) after PassManager::run; verifier passes.
  • Fixed-point reached in ≤ 2·|nodes| worklist iterations on all valid fixtures; non-convergence is an explicit error, never a hang.
  • Each malformed fixture produces exactly one PassError with the correct NodeId and ErrKind variant (golden-matched).
  • Analysis cache hit on a re-run with no mutation: 0 re-analysis (assert via instrumented counter).
  • Shape inference on a 200-node graph completes in < 5 ms; deterministic across 100 runs (identical output bytes).

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
infer_matmul.tg MatMul([8,16],[16,4]) out [8,4] f32
infer_broadcast.tg Add([4,1,8],[3,8]) out [4,3,8]
infer_dynamic.tg Add([N,8],[N,8]) w/ Dim::Dynamic("N") out [N,8], N unified
infer_conv2d.tg Conv2d([1,3,32,32],[16,3,3,3]) stride1 pad1 [1,16,32,32]
bad_matmul.tg MatMul([8,16],[8,4]) ShapeMismatch @ matmul node, k:16≠8
bad_dtype.tg Add(f32,i32) DTypeMismatch{F32,I32}
bad_arity.tg Relu(a,b) Arity{exp:1,got:2}
bad_rank.tg Conv2d rank-3 input RankErr{exp:4,got:3}
bad_cycle.tg node refs descendant Cyclic
bad_reshape.tg Reshape([8,16]->[5,5]) ShapeMismatch (128≠25)

Golden .expected files store stdout of tcc dump graph --typed; malformed cases store the PassError Display string.

2.6 Pitfalls

  • Non-monotone update -> oscillation: re-deriving a node's type to a different shape each pass loops forever. Root cause: missing fixpoint monotonicity (type must only refine, e.g. Dynamic->Static, never flip). Debug: log per-node type history; assert refinement lattice ordering.
  • Stale cache after mutation: a transform forgets invalidates(), downstream reads dead TensorType. Symptom: typecheck passes on wrong shapes. Debug: cache entries carry a Graph-revision counter; mismatch = bug.
  • Broadcast vs dynamic-dim conflation: treating Dim::Dynamic as broadcastable 1. Root cause: ambiguous lattice. Decision: Dynamic unifies only with equal-named Dynamic or any Static (assume-compatible), never silently broadcasts.
  • Topo order assumed but graph has multi-output / shared subexprs: visiting a node before all args typed -> spurious Unresolved. Fix: worklist re-enqueues; never single-pass.
  • Reshape with -1 inferred dim: forgetting product back-solve; emit ShapeMismatch only after attempting -1 resolution against known total.
  • Error masking: first error aborts, hiding others; but multiple errors are non-deterministic to order. Decision: report the topologically-first offending node only — deterministic, single error.

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • Worklist with VecDeque + visited-dirty set; avoid full re-scan per iteration. Target O(|edges|·passes), passes ≤ small constant in practice.
  • TypeRel dispatch via match on GraphOp discriminant (no dynamic alloc per node).
  • Shape arithmetic on Vec<Dim>: reuse buffers; broadcast aligns in-place. Inference < 5 ms / 200 nodes, < 50 ms / 5000 nodes.
  • AnalysisCache keyed by cheap AnalysisId enum; Box<dyn Any> downcast amortized.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-dim / scalar tensor (Shape(vec![])): valid; broadcasts against any.
  • Dim of size 0 (empty tensor): legal shape, propagates; product checks honor 0.
  • All-dynamic graph: every dim symbolic; inference succeeds, defers concrete check to VM.
  • Single-node graph (Input only): typed from declared TensorType; verifier trivially passes.
  • Diamond dataflow (shared arg feeding two consumers): typed once, both read cache.
  • Const node: type from embedded TensorData; no inference needed but must match consumers.
  • Mixed Static/Dynamic in same MatMul k-dim: Static(k) vs Dynamic -> bind dynamic to static.

2.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Response
Non-convergent fixpoint iter > `2· nodes
Untyped node post-run verifier scan Untyped(NodeId), abort pipeline
Cycle in dataflow DFS coloring Cyclic, name back-edge node
Cache corruption revision-counter mismatch panic (internal invariant), not PassError
Unknown GraphOp in TypeRel registry missing-key lookup PassError "no type relation for op"

2.10 Operational Notes

  • tcc dump graph --typed is the primary inspection tool; --analysis shape dumps the fixpoint trace for debugging.
  • Pipeline configurable via PassManager::with_pipeline(&[...]); default [ShapeInference, TypeCheck, Verifier] runs before every Phase ≥3 pass.
  • RUST_LOG=tc_pass=debug logs each worklist step + cache hit/miss counters.
  • Invariant enforced repo-wide: no Phase ≥3 transform may run on an untyped Graph (debug-assert Verifier precondition).
  • Errors are Display-stable (golden-tested); changing wording requires regenerating .expected fixtures.

15. Phase 3 — Graph optimization passes

Transform passes in tc-pass: constant folding (CF), common-subexpression elimination (CSE), dead-code elimination (DCE), algebraic simplification / canonicalization (Simplify). All run on Graph (post shape/type inference, Phase 2). Every pass: numerics-preserving, leaves graph re-verifiable, idempotent on its own output.

3.1 Objectives

  • CF: evaluate fully-const subgraphs at compile time → GraphOp::Const(TensorData); reuse the Phase-10 reference op kernels (CPU eval) so folded value == runtime value.
  • CSE: hash-cons structurally-identical GraphNodes (same op + same arg NodeIds + same TensorType); collapse duplicates to one canonical NodeId.
  • DCE: prune nodes not reachable from Graph.outputs; remove dangling const/intermediate nodes after CF/CSE.
  • Simplify: pattern-rewrite canonicalization — x+0, 0+x, x*1, x*0, mul/add of consts, Reshape(Reshape(x)), Transpose(Transpose(x))=id, Relu(Relu(x)), double-negation.
  • Invariant (LOCKED): output Graph numerically identical to input on the oracle (f32 rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6) for all sampled inputs.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files (under crates/tc-pass/src/, all depend on Phase-2 pass.rs / verify.rs):

file contents
transform/const_fold.rs pub struct ConstFold; impl Pass for ConstFold
transform/cse.rs pub struct Cse; impl Pass for Cse + NodeKey hash key
transform/dce.rs pub struct Dce; impl Pass for Dce
transform/simplify.rs pub struct Simplify; impl Pass for Simplify + RewriteRule table
transform/eval.rs fn eval_const(op:&GraphOp, args:&[&TensorData]) -> TensorData (compile-time interpreter)
  • Pass trait (Phase 2; P4 §6.4 passes verifiable+composable; INV-1 §7.2 pass semantic preservation): fn run(&self, g:&mut Graph) -> PassResult; fn name(&self)->&str;. Register CF→CSE→DCE→Simplify→(repeat to fixpoint, max 8 iters) in PassManager.
  • CF algorithm: topo-order DFS; a node is const iff all args are Const. For each such node call eval_const (delegates to ref kernels: Add/Mul broadcast, MatMul, Reshape, Transpose, Reduce, Relu). Replace node with Const(result); keep TensorType unchanged. Skip ops with no const evaluator (Conv2d optional behind flag, expensive).
  • CSE algorithm: single topo pass; NodeKey = (discriminant(op), op-payload-hash, args: Vec<NodeId>, TensorType). HashMap<NodeKey, NodeId>; on hit, rewire consumers via remap: HashMap<NodeId,NodeId> applied to later nodes' args. Inputs (Input(_)) never merged across names; Const merged by bit-equal TensorData (canonicalize -0.0/NaN payload).
  • DCE algorithm: mark-sweep — BFS reachability from outputs over reverse args; unmarked NodeIds dropped from Arena; renumber via compaction map; rewrite args/inputs/outputs.
  • Simplify: ordered RewriteRule list, each fn match_rewrite(&Graph, NodeId)->Option<NodeId>; greedy single bottom-up sweep; emit new node or alias existing; const-collapsing rules defer actual value to CF.

3.3 Deliverable

tc-pass exposes optimize(g:&mut Graph) running CF/CSE/DCE/Simplify to fixpoint. tcc dump graph --opt model.txt prints the optimized graph; tcc check confirms optimized vs unoptimized outputs match the oracle. Per-pass node-count delta logged.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • All four passes implemented, each impl Pass, registered in PassManager; output re-verifies (Phase-2 verifier: every node typed, args arity/dtype valid).
  • Numerics: optimized Graph matches unoptimized within rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6 on ≥50 random input draws across fixture corpus.
  • CF: a graph of Add(Const,Const)→single Const; CSE: N identical subtrees → node count drops to 1 copy + reuse; DCE: unreachable nodes count = 0 post-pass; Simplify: x+0 etc. collapsed (asserted by node match).
  • Fixpoint reached ≤8 iterations on corpus; each pass idempotent (run twice = run once, verified by node-count stability).

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

fixture input expected
cf_add_consts.txt add(const[2,3], const[2,3]) 1 Const node, value = elementwise sum
cse_dup_matmul.txt two identical matmul(a,b) feeding add 1 matmul, reuse
dce_orphan.txt output uses only relu(a); stray mul(b,c) present mul/b/c pruned
sim_xplus0.txt add(x, const_zeros) aliases x
sim_reshape2.txt reshape(reshape(x,s1),s2) single reshape(x,s2)
combo_pipeline.txt mixed fixpoint stable; oracle-equal
malformed: cf_shape_mismatch.txt add of consts w/ incompatible shapes rejected by verifier pre-CF, error EShapeMismatch
malformed: sim_bad_alias.txt rule would drop output node pass must refuse; EOutputElided

Each fixture has .expected (dumped optimized graph) + oracle tensor .npy-style ref.

3.6 Pitfalls

  • CF float reproducibility: compile-time eval must use the SAME f32 rounding/accumulation order as the runtime kernel, else folded const drifts > tol. Root cause: FMA/reassoc; fix: share eval_const math with Phase-10 ref kernels; debug via per-node oracle diff.
  • CSE merging non-equal consts: 0.0/-0.0 hash equal but NaN!=NaN; bit-canonicalize payload before hashing. Symptom: silent wrong-value reuse; debug by dumping NodeKey.
  • DCE eliding aliased outputs: if an output NodeId was remapped by CSE but outputs not updated, DCE prunes the live result. Fix: apply remap to outputs/inputs before sweep.
  • Simplify infinite loop: non-terminating rewrite (e.g. a+b↔b+a both firing). Root cause: no canonical ordering; fix: commutative ops canonicalize arg order by NodeId, rules only reduce a monotone cost metric.
  • Stale TensorType after rewrite: new const/reshape node missing inferred type → verifier fail. Fix: re-run shape inference (Phase 2) or carry type from replaced node.
  • Arena dangling ids: dropping nodes without compaction leaves stale NodeId in args. Fix: single compaction map applied atomically.

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • Compile-time CF of large consts (e.g. MatMul of [1024,1024]) can dominate compile latency; cap by --fold-budget (default 16 MiB result, 50 ms/op) — skip beyond.
  • CSE/DCE are O(N) topo passes; hashing dominates — use fast 64-bit hash (fxhash) over NodeKey, avoid re-hashing per fixpoint iter (cache).
  • Fixpoint loop: bound to 8 iters; track "changed" flag, early-exit when no pass mutates. Target: full opt of a 5k-node graph < 100 ms.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty/scalar tensors (shape [], [0]) in CF — broadcast + 0-size eval must not panic.
  • Dynamic dims (Dim::Dynamic): node not const → CF skips; CSE keys treat symbolic dims as opaque-equal only if same symbol name.
  • Const that IS an output: DCE must keep; Simplify x*0→zeros must preserve output shape/dtype.
  • Self-referential alias after Simplify (x+0→x) where x is also an output: keep both edges.
  • All-const graph: collapses to pure Const outputs, zero compute ops — runtime must still produce them.

3.9 Failure Analysis

failure detection action
numeric drift post-CF property test oracle diff quarantine op from CF allowlist; log
verifier fail post-pass PassManager runs verify after each pass abort pipeline, dump offending node
non-termination iter counter > 8 error EPassDiverged, emit pre-divergence graph
node-count grew per-pass delta check flag rule as non-monotone, disable
OOM during CF fold-budget guard skip fold, leave dynamic

3.10 Operational Notes

  • Flags: --opt-level {0,1,2} (0=DCE only; 1=+CF+CSE; 2=+Simplify+fixpoint), --fold-budget, --dump-pass <name> (before/after).
  • tcc dump graph --opt --opt-level N and --pass-stats (per-pass node delta, ms, iters).
  • Logging: tc_pass=debug traces each rewrite (rule name, old→new NodeId). Determinism: stable iteration over Arena order → reproducible output graph hash (regression-pinned in CI).
  • Verifier auto-runs after each pass in debug builds; release builds run it once at pipeline end (env TC_VERIFY_ALL=1 forces per-pass).

16. Phase 4 — Operator fusion

Crate: tc-fuse (depends tc-graph + tc-ir). Input: typed, optimized Graph (post Phase 3). Output: same Graph annotated with FusionGroup partition + each group lowered to a single PrimFunc. Thesis: fusion is the dominant CPU win — it kills intermediate materialization (DRAM round-trips), turning N memory-bound elementwise kernels into 1.

4.1 Objectives

  • Dominator-based fusion analysis → partition Graph nodes into FusionGroups; each group lowers to one PrimFunc.
  • Fusion rules by op pattern-kind: Opaque < Injective < Reduce < Complex (TVM OpPatternKind model) — encode legal merges.
  • Group injective/elementwise chains + at most one Reduce (epilogue/prologue) per group; eliminate materialized intermediates.
  • Produce a materialization-count metric (#intermediate Buffers before vs after) proving reduction.
  • Invariant: fusion changes performance only, never numerics (compute/schedule separation upstream).

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files under tc-fuse/src/:

File Owns
pattern.rs OpPatternKind + op_pattern(op: &GraphOp) -> OpPatternKind
dominator.rs post-dominator tree over the reverse dataflow graph
group.rs FusionGroup { root: NodeId, members: Vec<NodeId>, master: NodeId } + union-find
rules.rs can_fuse(parent_kind, child_kind, num_consumers) -> bool
pass.rs FusionPass: Pass; orchestrates analyze → group → lower
lower.rs lower_group(g: &Graph, grp: &FusionGroup) -> PrimFunc
metric.rs materialization_count(g, &[FusionGroup]) -> usize 
pub enum OpPatternKind { ElemWise, Broadcast, Injective, CommReduce, OutEWiseFusable, Opaque }
pub struct FusionGroup { pub master: NodeId, pub members: Vec<NodeId>, pub pattern: OpPatternKind }
impl FusionPass { pub fn run(&self, g: &mut Graph) -> Vec<FusionGroup>; }
  • Pattern map: Add/Mul/Relu → ElemWise; Reshape/Transpose → Injective; Reduce → CommReduce; Conv2d/MatMul → OutEWiseFusable (compute-heavy "master", fuses elementwise epilogue only); Call(unknown)/Const/Input → Opaque.
  • Algorithm (ADR-06 dominator-tree fusion strategy; P5 §6.5 fusion eliminates materialization; INV-6 §7.7 fusion dataflow preservation; paper P1 §5.2 TVM FuseOps): (1) build DAG post-dominator tree (immediate post-dominator ipdom per node over reverse edges). (2) For each node src with kind ≤ kCommReduce, walk path src → ipdom, checking every intermediate is Injective/ElemWise and can_fuse holds; union into one group. (3) OutEWiseFusable master fuses only ElemWise/Broadcast children downstream (epilogue fusion: conv→bias→relu). (4) CommReduce fuses injective inputs (prologue) but not consumers.
  • Single-consumer constraint: a producer fuses into a consumer only if the consumer is its sole use along the dominated path; multi-consumer node → boundary (or duplicate-compute decision deferred). Diamonds resolved via post-dominator (re-converge point).
  • Lowering: topo-order group members; bind each member's output to a TIR TensorExpr; inline non-master members as Expr (no Store/Allocate for intermediates); only the group's external outputs get Buffers + Store. Result: one PrimFunc with fused loop nest, intermediates as register-level Expr.

4.3 Deliverable

FusionPass integrated into PassManager; tcc dump --fused <model> prints the group partition + per-group PrimFunc; tcc dump --tir shows fused loop nests with no intermediate Allocate. conv→bias_add→relu fuses to a single FusionGroup/PrimFunc.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • conv→bias→relu chain → exactly 1 FusionGroup (master = Conv2d); --dump fused confirms 3 members.
  • Materialization count: a 5-op elementwise chain a+b*c-relu(d)/e → intermediates drop from 4 → 0 (only group output materialized).
  • Numerics: fused PrimFunc output matches unfused per-op execution within f32 rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6.
  • Idempotent: re-running FusionPass on a fused graph is a no-op (no group splitting/merging).
  • No Opaque op ever absorbed into a group; group count = #opaque-boundaries + #fused-regions.

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
fuse_ewise_chain.graph 1 group, members={Add,Mul,Sub,Relu,Div}, materialized=1
fuse_conv_bias_relu.graph 1 group, master=Conv2d, epilogue={Add,Relu}
fuse_reduce_softmax.graph reduce fuses injective prologue; exp/div elementwise epilogue separate-or-fused per rule
fuse_diamond.graph shared producer → boundary; 2 consumer groups, producer materialized once
nofuse_two_opaque.graph 2 groups, no merge (Opaque boundary)
malformed: cycle.graph reject — FuseErr::NotAcyclic (dominator build precondition)
malformed: untyped.graph reject — FuseErr::UntypedNode (Phase 2 shape-infer must precede)

4.6 Pitfalls

  • Multi-consumer over-fusion → fusing a node into one consumer that's read by another duplicates compute or breaks the other's input. Root: ignoring out-degree. Debug: assert out_degree==1 along fused path; dump per-node consumer set.
  • Reduce double-counting → fusing two reduces (or a reduce into another reduce's path) yields wrong loop nest (mismatched reduction axes). Root: rule allows ≤1 CommReduce/group. Debug: --dump tir inspect reduction IterVar count.
  • Broadcast in fused loop → eltwise with implicit broadcast needs index remapping; naive shared-index lowering reads OOB. Root: differing shapes in group. Debug: print per-member output Shape; verify broadcast-aware index in lower.rs.
  • Stale dominator tree → mutating the graph between analyze + lower invalidates ipdom; group members reference dead NodeId. Root: pass reorder. Debug: recompute + assert NodeId liveness pre-lower.
  • Layout mismatch silently fused → NCHW conv + NHWC eltwise fused without convert (Phase 5 not yet run). Root: phase ordering. Mitigation: gate epilogue fusion on identical layout attr; defer cross-layout to Phase 5.

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Win source: eliminated intermediate DRAM traffic — memory-bound eltwise chain goes from N HBM passes to 1. Target: ≥3x speedup on 5-op f32 chain (length-1M) vs unfused.
  • Analysis cost: dominator tree O(V·E) naive / near-linear w/ Lengauer-Tarjan; fusion pass must be <5% of total compile time on a 500-node graph.
  • Avoid fusing compute-bound masters with large epilogues into a single huge loop that spills registers — cap fused-group member count (heuristic, e.g. ≤16) revisited by Phase 12 cost model.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • Single-node graph → 1 group (itself); no merge.
  • All-Opaque graph → each node its own group; materialization unchanged.
  • Reduce as graph output (no epilogue) → group = reduce + injective prologue only.
  • Const-folded subgraph (post Phase 3) → Const is Opaque, not fused into consumers.
  • Dynamic-shape (Dim::Dynamic) intermediate → still fusable (symbolic extents in loop), but materialization metric uses symbolic size; flag for VM path.

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Cycle in dataflow dominator precondition check FuseErr::NotAcyclic, abort pass
Untyped node pre-pass verifier FuseErr::UntypedNode, require Phase 2
Numeric drift post-fuse conformance oracle (4.4) bisect: disable fusion per-group, find offending rule
Group lowers to empty PrimFunc post-lower assert body≠∅ FuseErr::EmptyGroup, log master NodeId
OOM building dominator on huge graph size guard fall back to no-fusion (each node = group), warn

4.10 Operational Notes

  • RUST_LOG=tc_fuse=debug dumps the post-dominator tree + each fusion decision (fuse(parent→child): kind ok / rejected:reason).
  • tcc dump --fused --format dot emits a Graphviz coloring of FusionGroups for visual inspection.
  • Feature flag --no-fusion (CLI) bypasses FusionPass for differential debugging (compare fused vs unfused numerics).
  • Metric materialization_count logged before/after; CI asserts monotone non-increase on the fixture corpus.
  • FusionPass ordering: after Phase 3 simplify/DCE, before Phase 5 layout — documented in PassManager default pipeline.

17. Phase 5 — Layout / data-format transform and canonicalization

Crates: tc-pass (primary), tc-graph (op layout traits + convert node). Depends on Phase 2 shape inference, Phase 3 simplify. Goal: minimize layout-convert insertions, canonicalize to backend-required contiguous formats, preserve numerics.

5.1 Objectives

  • Per-op data-format selection (NCHW ⟷ NHWC for conv/pool; row-major contiguous for matmul/elementwise) driven by op preference + cost.
  • Insert LayoutConvert nodes only at format boundaries (where producer-format ≠ consumer-required-format) — never per-op.
  • Cost-aware placement: minimize total convert bytes via a forward propagation + backward reconciliation, not greedy local insertion.
  • Canonicalize to contiguous/default layouts at graph outputs + at ops whose backend kernel mandates a fixed format.
  • Emit a convert-count + convert-byte metric; numerics invariant under layout (oracle re-check, rtol 1e-5).

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files (under tc-pass/src/passes/layout/):

File Contents
mod.rs LayoutPass: Pass; run(&Graph) -> Graph; orchestrates infer→insert→canon→simplify-fold
format.rs enum Layout { NCHW, NHWC, RowMajor(Rank), Opaque }; Layout::permutation(), is_contiguous()
op_layout.rs trait OpLayout { fn preferred_in(&self, ty:&[TensorType]) -> Vec<Layout>; fn out(&self, ins:&[Layout]) -> Layout; } per GraphOp
infer.rs forward layout propagation to fixed point over topo order
insert.rs boundary diff + LayoutConvert node materialization
cost.rs convert_cost(from,to,ty) = bytes(ty) if from≠to else 0; tie-break = downstream consumer count

Algorithm (P4 §6.4 passes verifiable+composable; paper Required-Reading-P7 §5.2 XLA layout-assignment-as-a-pass):

  1. Propagate: seed inputs = RowMajor; each op picks out() from incoming layouts + preferred_in. Conv2d anchors NCHW/NHWC choice; elementwise/Add/Mul/Relu are layout-agnostic (pass-through, no anchor).
  2. Anchor cost model: for each layout-sensitive op enumerate candidate formats; pick min total Σ convert_cost(producer_out, candidate_in) summed over args + expected output converts (greedy with one backward fixup pass over anchored ops).
  3. Insert: for edge u→v where layout(u) ≠ required_in(v), splice GraphOp::LayoutConvert{from,to} (new op, registered in tc-graph registry; carries permutation). Dedup: if u feeds N consumers all needing same target, insert one shared convert.
  4. Canonicalize: force graph outputs + matmul operands to RowMajor contiguous; mark non-contiguous Reshape/Transpose results needing materialization.
  5. Fold: cancel adjacent inverse converts (NCHW→NHWC→NCHW ⇒ identity); fold convert into preceding Const (apply permutation at compile time); re-run CSE on converts.

New IR: GraphOp::LayoutConvert { from: Layout, to: Layout }; layout becomes a TensorType attribute (pub layout: Layout) — shape unchanged, strides/permutation reinterpreted at lowering.

5.3 Deliverable

Layout-optimized Graph with (a) every node carrying a resolved Layout, (b) minimal LayoutConvert insertions, (c) contiguous-canonical outputs; tcc dump --graph --show-layout model prints per-node layout + convert count; numerics unchanged vs Phase 4 graph.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Convert count on the conv-stack fixture ≤ theoretical-minimum + 0; on the mixed fixture ≤ min+1 (documented).
  • Inverse-convert pairs: 0 survive folding (asserted).
  • Numerics: layout graph output == pre-layout output, rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6.
  • LayoutConvert inserted only on format-mismatch edges (verifier: no convert where from==to).
  • Pass idempotent: LayoutPass(LayoutPass(g)) == LayoutPass(g) (convert count stable).
  • Runtime ≤ 5 ms on a 200-node graph.

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
layout/conv_chain.txt conv→relu→conv (all NCHW pref) 0 converts (uniform format)
layout/nhwc_island.txt conv(NCHW)→conv(NHWC pref)→conv(NCHW) 2 converts, shared if fan-out
layout/matmul_canon.txt transpose→matmul 1 convert → RowMajor at matmul operand
layout/inverse_cancel.txt explicit NCHW→NHWC→NCHW 0 converts after fold
layout/const_fold.txt const→layout_convert→add convert folded into const, 0 runtime converts
Malformed layout/rank_mismatch.txt NHWC on rank-3 tensor LayoutError::RankIncompatible
Malformed layout/opaque_anchor.txt conv on Opaque input error: unresolvable anchor

Each fixture pairs with .expected (per-node layout + convert metric) and .npy oracle output.

5.6 Pitfalls

  • Greedy local insertion explodes converts: inserting per-edge without fan-out dedup yields N converts for N consumers → root cause: missing shared-convert pass. Debug: dump convert-byte metric, diff vs theoretical min.
  • Layout vs shape confusion: permuting Layout without updating strides at lowering corrupts indexing → keep shape logical, encode permutation only in LayoutConvert; assert shape invariant across convert.
  • Inverse pairs not canceling because intervening layout-agnostic op blocks adjacency → fold must look through pass-through ops (elementwise) when matching inverse converts.
  • Const-fold of convert changes dtype/byte layout silently → fold must materialize permuted TensorData and re-validate against oracle; bug surfaces as off-by-stride numerics.
  • Anchor oscillation: two conflicting conv preferences cause propagation to flip → fixed-point with deterministic tie-break (lowest NodeId) + iteration cap (graph-node-count); panic on non-convergence.
  • Dynamic dims (Dim::Dynamic) in spatial axes break NHWC channel-last assumptions → require static channel dim for format choice; else fall back to RowMajor.

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Propagation = O(nodes) topo passes × fixup; cap at 2 sweeps → ~O(2N). Cost model is O(candidates·args) per anchor, candidates ≤ 2.
  • Convert nodes are the output cost: each materializes a transpose kernel — minimizing count directly reduces runtime memory traffic (a convert ≈ full tensor copy).
  • Shared-convert dedup saves up to (fan-out−1)×bytes per boundary.
  • Const-fold removes converts from the runtime hot path entirely (compile-time permutation).

5.8 Edge Cases

  • Single-op graph: 0 converts, output canonicalized only if non-contiguous.
  • All-elementwise graph: fully layout-agnostic → 0 converts, inherits input layout.
  • Reshape that breaks contiguity → force materialize (insert canonicalize convert before reshape).
  • Branch where two paths reconverge with differing layouts at Add → insert convert on cheaper-bytes branch.
  • Const already in target layout → no convert, no fold needed.
  • Rank-1/scalar tensors: Layout::RowMajor(1), never NCHW/NHWC.

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Numerics drift post-layout oracle re-check in CI per fixture bisect: disable fold, then insert, isolate stage
Convert count regression metric assertion vs .expected dump propagation trace, inspect anchor choices
Non-convergent propagation iteration cap exceeded → panic w/ node cycle report conflicting anchors; require explicit layout hint
Unresolvable Opaque anchor infer.rs error reject graph at import; demand typed input
Lost convert at boundary verifier scan post-pass fail-closed: assert every mismatch edge has convert

5.10 Operational Notes

  • tcc dump --graph --show-layout / --metric converts exposes count + bytes; CI gates on convert-count budget per fixture.
  • Layout choice is deterministic (seeded tie-break) for reproducible builds.
  • GPU layouts (Shared/Thread scope, NCHWc blocked formats) are PhaseDeferred — Layout::Opaque reserved.
  • Pass slots after fusion (Phase 4), before TIR lowering (Phase 6): fused groups get a single resolved layout; convert nodes lower to dedicated transpose PrimFuncs.
  • Log level debug: per-anchor cost table; trace: full propagation fixed-point iterations.

18. Phase 6 — Tensor/loop IR (TIR) and lowering

Bridge phase: graph-level dataflow (tc-graph/tc-fuse) → imperative loop-nest IR (tc-ir). A FusionGroup becomes a TensorExpr (WHAT) plus a default naive nested-loop PrimFunc (HOW, unscheduled). No schedule applied here — Phase 7/8 transform the loop nest; this phase guarantees a correct baseline that tc-codegen can already compile + run vs the oracle.

6.1 Objectives

  • Land tc-ir in full: PrimFunc/Stmt/Expr/Buffer/IterVar, typed-id arena, IrBuilder, verify().
  • Lower a fused GraphOp group → TensorExpr (per-element index→value λ) → default PrimFunc (row-major nested For over output axes, single Store).
  • Reduce ops (Reduce(kind), MatMul, Conv2d) lower with an inner reduction loop (IterKind::Reduce) + init Store.
  • Verifier rejects: use-before-def (VarId/BufferId), out-of-range buffer index (static), dtype mismatch across Bin/Store/Load, malformed loop bounds.
  • Invariant: lowering is schedule-free; result is numerically identical to oracle within f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files (per §10 repo layout) under tc-ir/src/:

file contents
ids.rs VarId, BufferId, AxisId, IntrinId, IterKind, newtype u32 over Arena
expr.rs Expr enum, BinOp, Const, dtype_of(&Expr,&Ctx)->DType
stmt.rs Stmt enum, Block flattening helper
buffer.rs Buffer{id,shape:Vec<Expr>,dtype,scope}, Scope, byte/elem-stride calc
func.rs PrimFunc{params:Vec<Buffer>, body:Stmt}, name, buffer table
tensor_expr.rs TensorExpr{out_shape:Vec<Expr>, axes:Vec<IterVar>, reduce_axes:Vec<IterVar>, body:Box<dyn Fn(&[Expr])->Expr>-equiv via closure-id}
builder.rs IrBuilder: alloc_buffer, var, for_(var,min,extent,body), store, load, bin, block
verify.rs verify(&PrimFunc)->Result<(),IrError>
lower.rs lower_group(&FusionGroup,&Graph)->PrimFunc

TensorExpr representation (P3 §6.3 two-level IR, lower late; ADR-03 IR layering — avoid boxed Fn in serialized IR): store body as an Expr template parameterized by symbolic axis VarIds — axes are the output index vars, reduce_axes carry IterKind::Reduce. Compute lowering: compute(out_shape,axes,reduce_axes,body_expr)->PrimFunc emits a For per axis (min=0, extent=out_shape[i], IterKind::Data); with no reduce axes, body=Store{out,idx(axes),body_expr}; else Store{out,idx,init(kind)} then a reduce For doing Store{out,idx,combine(kind,Load out,body_expr)}; index linearization idx = Σ_k var_k·stride_k (row-major, contiguous default).

Per-GraphOp lowering rules (lower.rs):

GraphOp TensorExpr body reduce
Add/Mul/Relu `lhs[idx] (+ *) rhs[idx]/max(x,0)`
Reshape/Transpose re-index inputs (view), no new loop where contiguous none
MatMul [M,K]·[K,N] Σ_k A[m,k]*B[k,n] k∈Reduce
Reduce(Sum/Max) combine over reduced axes reduced dims
Conv2d Σ_{c,kh,kw} In[...]*W[...] c,kh,kw∈Reduce

Fused group: topo-order ops, materialize each producer's TensorExpr, then inline elementwise producers into the consumer's body (substitute Load(intermediate, idx) → producer body) — only the group's true outputs get a Buffer + Store; intermediates are not allocated. A reduce terminates inlining (its result is a buffer). Combine combine(kind): Sum→+, Max→max intrinsic, init Sum→0, Max→-inf.

6.3 Deliverable

tc-ir compiles. tcc dump tir <model> prints a PrimFunc for each fused group. lower_group produces a default PrimFunc for every group emitted by Phase 4/5; piped through Phase 0's hand-path codegen+runtime it executes and matches the oracle. verify() runs on every lowered func before codegen.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • All ops in the locked GraphOp set lower to a verifiable PrimFunc; verify() returns Ok for every corpus group, Err for every malformed fixture.
  • Numerical: lowered+compiled output == oracle within f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6 across the lowering corpus (≥20 groups incl. matmul, conv, fused elementwise+reduce).
  • Fused elementwise chain allocates 0 intermediate buffers (assert buffer count == #group-outputs + #group-inputs).
  • dump tir output is deterministic (stable id numbering, byte-identical across runs).

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

fixture input expected
tir/add_default.txt 2-input Add [4,8] 2 For + 1 Store, no Allocate
tir/matmul.txt [64,64]·[64,64] 3 For (k=Reduce), init+accum Store
tir/fused_add_relu.txt Add→Relu group single nest, inlined, 0 intermediates
tir/reduce_sum.txt Sum axis=1 [4,8] outer m-loop, inner reduce, init 0
tir/conv2d.txt 3×3 conv 4 data + 3 reduce loops
malformed tir/bad_usedef.txt Load of unallocated buffer IrError::UseBeforeDef
malformed tir/bad_dtype.txt F32+I32 Bin IrError::DTypeMismatch
malformed tir/bad_bounds.txt const index ≥ extent IrError::IndexOutOfBounds

Each .txt is the canonical dump tir pretty-print; golden-file compared.

6.6 Pitfalls

  • Boxed-closure TensorExpr → unserializable, non-deterministic dump, hostile to Phase 7 transforms. Root cause: storing dyn Fn. Fix: symbolic Expr template over axis VarIds. Debug: dump fails round-trip.
  • Inlining a reduce as if elementwise → wrong results (reduction collapses dims). Root cause: treating reduce output as a same-shape value. Fix: reduce is an inlining barrier (forces a buffer). Debug: shape mismatch in consumer index.
  • Transpose/Reshape as data movement → spurious loops + wrong strides. Root cause: not recognizing pure view ops. Fix: re-index via stride remap, no Store. Debug: extra buffer + perf cliff.
  • Reduction init missing / wrong identity (Max init 0 not -inf) → silently wrong on negative inputs. Debug: oracle diff only on negative-valued fixtures.
  • Row-major stride miscompute for rank>2 → off-by-stride loads, garbage. Root cause: hand-rolled linearization. Fix: single linearize(shape,idx) helper, unit-tested. Debug: correct for [N,M], wrong for [N,M,K].
  • Shared VarId across nested fused groups → use-before-def false negatives / aliasing. Fix: per-PrimFunc id namespace, reset in builder.

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • This phase emits the naive baseline; expect ≪10% of peak (no tiling/vectorize). That is intentional — Phases 7–9 close the gap; record baseline GFLOP/s for matmul/conv as the speedup denominator.
  • Lowering itself O(group size × index rank); must not be a compile-time bottleneck — target <1 ms/group for corpus.
  • Avoid allocating intermediates (fusion already eliminated them); a leaked intermediate buffer = both memory + a perf regression.
  • verify() is O(stmts); single pass, no quadratic def-scans (use a HashSet of live ids).

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Scalar (rank-0) tensor → 0 loops, single Store at index 0.
  • Dynamic dim (Dim::Dynamic) in shape → extent is a symbolic Expr(Var), not const; static bounds check skipped, deferred to VM (Phase 11).
  • Broadcasting input ([1,8] vs [4,8]) → producer index zeroes broadcast axis stride.
  • Empty reduce axis set on a Reduce op → degenerates to identity copy.
  • Single-element fused group (one op) → no inlining, direct compute.
  • Conv2d with stride/pad → index arithmetic with IfThenElse guard for padding (load 0 outside bounds).

6.9 Failure Analysis

failure detection response
use-before-def verify() live-set IrError::UseBeforeDef{buffer/var}, abort lowering
static index OOB verify() const-eval IrError::IndexOutOfBounds{idx,extent}
dtype mismatch verify() dtype_of IrError::DTypeMismatch{lhs,rhs}
unhandled GraphOp lower.rs match IrError::Unlowerable(op) — never panic
numerical drift vs oracle Phase 6 fixture run fail fixture, dump TIR + diff tensor

All errors are typed IrError; lowering never panics on malformed graph (panic = bug, caught by fuzz Phase 13).

6.10 Operational Notes

  • dump tir is the primary debug surface: pretty-printer with stable id labels (%v0, b0), loop indentation, [Reduce] axis tags.
  • Feature flag ir-trace logs each lowering decision (inline vs barrier) for diagnosing fusion/lowering mismatches.
  • Golden fixtures regenerated only via tcc dump tir --bless; CI diffs against committed goldens.
  • Downstream contract: Phase 7 consumes PrimFunc + builds Schedule over its IterVars; the default nest's axis order/ids are the schedule's starting point — keep them stable and documented.

19. Phase 7 — Schedule primitives I (loop structure)

Compute/schedule separation realized: TensorExpr (Phase 6) fixes WHAT; tc-schedule rewrites HOW. All six structural primitives are numerics-invariant loop-nest rewrites — they reorder/factor/relocate iteration, never alter the partial-sum semantics.

7.1 Objectives

  • Implement Schedule { func: PrimFunc, stages: Vec<Stage> } + Stage { axes: Vec<IterVar> } over a lowered PrimFunc; AxisId/StageId typed handles into a ScheduleState.
  • Structural primitives only: split, reorder, fuse, tile, compute_at, inline (perf primitives vectorize/unroll/parallel → Phase 8).
  • Invariant: every primitive is a semantics-preserving loop transform; reduce axes keep accumulation order or stay marked IterKind::Reduce (no result change for associative-exact / within-tolerance otherwise).
  • Deliverable: Schedule API transforms matmul/conv PrimFunc loop nests, validated by a numerics-preservation property test over random legal schedules.

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files (per section 10 layout) under tc-schedule/src/:

File Contents
lib.rs re-exports Schedule, Stage, AxisId, StageId
state.rs ScheduleState: axis table, parent/child loop relations, axis→stage map, IterDomain
axis.rs AxisId(u32), AxisRel (Split{outer,inner,factor}, Fuse{fused,a,b}), relation graph
prim/split.rs prim/reorder.rs prim/fuse.rs prim/tile.rs prim/compute_at.rs prim/inline.rs one primitive each
apply.rs materialize(&ScheduleState) -> PrimFunc: replays relations → emits Stmt::For nest

Data structures: IterDomain { min: Expr, extent: Expr } per axis; AxisRel DAG records derivation so materialize rebuilds the nest and rewrites every Expr::Var(VarId) of a consumed axis via substitution map.

Algorithms (signatures locked in spine; ADR-05 compute/schedule separation; P2 §6.2; paper P2 §5.2 Halide split/reorder/tile/compute_at/inline semantics):

  • split(ax, factor) -> (outer, inner): new axes, outer.extent = ceildiv(ext, factor), inner.extent = factor; index recovery ax = outer*factor + inner; guard inner < ext when factor ∤ ext.
  • fuse(a, b) -> AxisId: fused.extent = a.ext * b.ext; a = fused / b.ext, b = fused % b.ext; a is outer.
  • reorder(order): permute axis order vector within a stage; reject if order crosses a Reduce-before-Data dependency that changes results.
  • tile(ax, ay, fx, fy): split(ax,fx), split(ay,fy), reorder([axo, ayo, axi, ayi]).
  • compute_at(stage, ax): relocate producer stage's compute under consumer loop ax; recompute producer's needed sub-region (bounds inference over the consumer index at ax); insert Allocate at that scope.
  • inline(stage): delete producer's loop/store; substitute its compute Expr at each Load site in consumers; fail if producer has >1 store index pattern or a Reduce axis.

7.3 Deliverable

tc-schedule crate: Schedule::new(func), all six primitives, Schedule::materialize() -> PrimFunc. tcc dump scheduled <model> prints the transformed loop nest. Round-trips a matmul PrimFunc through tile(i,j,32,32) + reorder + compute_at and re-emits a valid PrimFunc.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • split(ext=1024, 32) → outer extent 32, inner 32; split(1000,32) → outer 32 + boundary guard, no OOB.
  • Property test: 10k random legal schedules on matmul [M,K]x[K,N], M,K,N ∈ {1..128} → output == naive reference within f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6.
  • materialize is idempotent on an empty schedule (no primitive ⇒ byte-identical PrimFunc).
  • tile+reorder+compute_at on conv2d produces a loop nest with ≤ original total iterations and identical result.
  • inline of an elementwise producer eliminates its Allocate+For; verified by Stmt count drop.

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
fixtures/sched/matmul_naive.tir → split_i32.scheduled 2-level i loop, guard if ¬32∣M
matmul_tile3232.scheduled nest order io,jo,ii,ji,k, golden text
conv2d_computeat.scheduled producer pad-buffer allocated under oc loop
ewise_inline.scheduled producer For/Allocate absent; Expr substituted
bad_reorder_reduce.json (malformed) error ReorderViolatesReduceDep
bad_inline_reduce.json (malformed) error CannotInlineReduceStage
bad_split_zero.json (factor=0) error SplitFactorMustBePositive

7.6 Pitfalls

  • Boundary-guard omission: non-dividing split without IfThenElse/min(extent) guard → OOB write past buffer. Root cause: assuming factor ∣ extent. Debug: compile with ASan, run extent=1000/factor=32.
  • Reorder past reduce: moving a Data axis inside a Reduce loop changes accumulation grouping → tolerance break for non-associative f32. Detect via axis IterKind dependency check before permuting.
  • compute_at bounds under-provision: producer region computed too small (off-by-stencil for conv) → reads stale/uninit. Root cause: bounds inference ignores halo. Debug: dump inferred IterDomain vs consumer access.
  • Stale VarId after fuse/split: consumer Exprs still reference the consumed axis var → dangling substitution. Fix: rewrite all references in one materialize pass; assert no orphan VarId.
  • inline of multi-store producer: substituting when producer writes via two index patterns yields wrong Load mapping. Guard: reject unless single affine store.
  • Double compute_at: relocating an already-relocated stage corrupts the relation DAG. Track stage scope; error on re-attach.

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • materialize is O(stages × axes); cache the AxisRel topo order, no per-primitive full rebuild.
  • Substitution map should be HashMap<VarId,Expr> applied in one Stmt walk, not recursive re-walks per axis.
  • compute_at shrinks intermediate buffer footprint → the actual perf payoff; quantify in Phase 8/9 (cache-resident tile).
  • Avoid Expr clone storms: store AxisRel as ids, build Exprs only at materialize.

7.8 Edge Cases

  • split with factor ≥ extent → outer extent 1, single guarded iteration.
  • split factor=1 → no-op outer; fuse of unit axis → identity extent.
  • reorder with identity permutation → unchanged nest.
  • fuse of two reduce axes → combined Reduce axis, accumulation order preserved.
  • compute_at at the outermost axis ≈ no movement (root scope); at innermost = full inline-like recompute.
  • inline of a constant-fold-collapsed producer (extent-1 loop) → trivial scalar substitution.

7.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
OOB on non-dividing split ASan / bounds assert in debug build emit min-guard automatically
Numerical drift on reorder property test tolerance fail reject illegal reorder, return ScheduleError
Orphan VarId post-fuse materialize validator abort transform, leave state unchanged (transactional)
compute_at halo miss conv golden mismatch widen inferred region by op stencil

Primitives are transactional: validate legality first, mutate ScheduleState only on success; on error return Err(ScheduleError) with state untouched.

7.10 Operational Notes

  • ScheduleError variants map 1:1 to malformed fixtures (§7.5); surfaced by tcc dump scheduled --explain.
  • tcc dump scheduled golden files regenerated via make update-goldens; diff-reviewed in CI.
  • Property-test seed logged on failure for deterministic replay (TC_SCHED_SEED).
  • Schedule legality checks gate every primitive — invalid schedules never reach codegen (Phase 9 assumes a verified Schedule).

20. Phase 8 — Schedule primitives II (performance: vectorize, unroll, parallel)

Crate: tc-schedule (primitives) + contract honored by tc-codegen (SimdBackend) + tc-runtime (thread pool). Depends on: Phase 7 loop transforms (split/reorder/fuse/tile/compute_at/inline). Invariant: schedule changes performance only — numerical result identical to the naive PrimFunc within oracle tolerance (f32 rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6).

8.1 Objectives

  • vectorize(ax) → map axis to SIMD lanes; legality: axis innermost + stride-1 (contiguous) in every Buffer it indexes.
  • unroll(ax) → full (extent ≤ unroll_max, default 64) or partial (factor) loop-body replication; reduces branch/IV overhead, exposes ILP.
  • parallel(ax) → map an outer axis to the runtime thread pool over tiles; data-parallel, no cross-iteration dependence.
  • Define codegen vectorization contract: what SimdBackend must emit/honor for a vectorized axis (lane width, tail/remainder, alignment, gather rejection).
  • Define interaction with Buffer.scope: Local buffers privatized per parallel iteration / per unroll copy; Global shared read-only or disjoint-write.
  • Deliverable: vectorized + parallel kernels, numerically verified, measured speedup vs naive PrimFunc.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files (per section 10 layout): tc-schedule/src/vectorize.rs, unroll.rs, parallel.rs, legality.rs; annotation carried on IterVar; consumed by tc-codegen/src/simd.rs + tc-runtime/src/threadpool.rs.

  • Extend IterKind annotation set (ADR-09 portable-SIMD strategy; P2 §6.2 compute/schedule separation; paper Required-Reading-P11 §5.2 Goto & van de Geijn GEMM tiling hierarchy — not new variants, a per-axis attribute on Stage): AxisAttr { Serial, Vectorized{lanes}, Unrolled{factor:Option<u32>}, Parallel }. Stage.axis_attr: HashMap<AxisId, AxisAttr>.
  • Schedule::vectorize(&mut self, ax): assert innermost (last in Stage.axes after reorder); call legality::check_contiguous(stage, ax) → all Load/Store indices affine in ax with coefficient 1 in the fastest-varying buffer dim; set Vectorized{lanes=target.vector_lanes(dtype)}.
  • Schedule::unroll(&mut self, ax, factor): full if factor==None && extent≤unroll_max; else partial → implicit split(ax, factor) then unroll inner. Sets Unrolled.
  • Schedule::parallel(&mut self, ax): assert ax is a Data IterVar with no carried dependence (loop-carried check: no read of a Storeed buffer at an index depending on prior ax value); set Parallel. Reject if a reduce axis (IterKind::Reduce) — reduction parallel needs atomic/tree-reduce (PhaseDeferred to Phase 12).
  • Lowering pass apply_attrs(schedule) -> PrimFunc: rewrites Stmt::For — Vectorized → vector intrinsic loop (codegen marker); Unrolled{full} → expand body N× with substituted IV const; Parallel → wrap body region with a parallel-for marker stmt (Call(IntrinId::ParallelFor, [extent, body])).
  • Codegen vectorization contract (tc-codegen honors): see 8.7 table; SimdBackend lowers a Vectorized axis of extent E, lanes L → E/L vector ops + scalar remainder for E mod L.

8.3 Deliverable

Schedule exposing vectorize/unroll/parallel; apply_attrs lowering producing annotated PrimFunc; tcc dump scheduled <model> shows attrs; tcc dump c shows vector/parallel emission; a benchmarked matmul + elementwise chain where the scheduled kernel beats naive.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Numerical match vs naive PrimFunc identical within rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6
Vectorized f32 elementwise (AVX2, L=8) speedup vs scalar ≥ 3.0×
parallel scaling on 8 cores, large elementwise ≥ 5× (≥62% efficiency)
unroll full on extent-4 reduce inner ≥ 1.2× vs rolled
Illegal vectorize (non-contiguous) rejected at schedule time, typed error
apply_attrs lowering deterministic; idempotent re-dump byte-identical

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
vec_contiguous.sched vectorize innermost stride-1 axis accepted; dump c emits L-wide vector loop + remainder
vec_strided.sched vectorize axis w/ stride≠1 Err(NotContiguous{axis}); no emission
vec_not_innermost.sched vectorize a non-last axis Err(NotInnermost)
unroll_full.sched unroll extent-4 axis body replicated 4×, no loop, IV folded
unroll_partial.sched unroll factor 8 on extent 1000 split→125×8, inner unrolled, tail handled
parallel_ok.sched parallel outer data axis thread-pool dispatch; result matches oracle
parallel_reduce.sched (malformed) parallel a reduce axis Err(ParallelReduceUnsupported)
parallel_dep.sched (malformed) parallel axis w/ loop-carried write Err(CarriedDependence)

8.6 Pitfalls

  • Vectorizing a reduce axis → silently wrong sum (lanes accumulate independently w/o horizontal reduce). Root cause: missing IterKind check. Debug: dump scheduled, confirm axis is Data; reject Reduce.
  • Contiguity assumed from shape, not strides → after transpose/layout the fastest dim isn't stride-1; vectorized loads stride-gather → wrong/slow. Debug: print buffer strides at the axis; legality must read actual Buffer.strides, not logical shape.
  • Unroll exploding code size → full-unrolling a 4096 extent → huge kernel, I-cache thrash, cc OOM. Root cause: no unroll_max guard. Debug: cap + warn; fall back to partial.
  • Parallel + Local buffer shared → races on a scratch buffer not privatized per thread. Root cause: scope not promoted. Debug: Local under a Parallel axis must be allocated inside the parallel region (per-iteration).
  • Tail remainder dropped → E mod L ≠ 0 and remainder loop omitted → last E mod L elements stale/garbage. Debug: compare element [E-1] vs oracle; assert remainder emitted.
  • Parallel over too-fine axis → thread spawn cost > work; slowdown. Root cause: parallel applied to tiny extent. Debug: cost-model gate (min grain ~10k elements/task).

8.7 Performance Concerns

Codegen vectorization contract (SimdBackend MUST honor):

Aspect Contract
Lane width L Target.vector_lanes(dtype): AVX2 f32=8, f64=4; SSE f32=4; scalar fallback L=1
Body emit E/L vector ops over contiguous loads/stores
Remainder scalar loop for E mod L; never dropped
Alignment assume unaligned loads (loadu); aligned only if Buffer base 32B-aligned
Reject non-affine / non-unit-stride index → bail to scalar (must not gather)
Reduce horizontal reduce intrinsic only if axis explicitly reduce-vectorized (Phase 12)
  • Parallel grain: tile so each task ≥ min_grain (default 1<<14 elems); thread pool sized to num_cpus, work-stealing.
  • Unroll trades I-cache for ILP; full-unroll only short axes (≤ unroll_max=64).
  • Target: vectorized+parallel GEMM ≥ 30% of OpenBLAS single-precision (documented in Phase 13).

8.8 Edge Cases

  • Extent < L → pure remainder (scalar), no vector op; still legal.
  • Extent exactly L → one vector op, zero remainder.
  • parallel on extent 1 → degenerate to serial (no dispatch).
  • vectorize then unroll same axis → reject (mutually exclusive on one axis).
  • parallel outer + vectorize inner → legal, common GEMM combo; verify both attrs coexist.
  • Dynamic-shape extent (Dim::Dynamic) → remainder mandatory; vector count computed at runtime by VM.
  • Bool/i8 dtype vectorize → lanes per Target; reject if no SIMD path → scalar.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Non-contiguous vectorize legality::check_contiguous Err(NotContiguous) pre-codegen
Carried dep on parallel axis dependence scan in parallel() Err(CarriedDependence)
Code-size blowup (unroll) extent > unroll_max downgrade to partial + warn
Wrong result post-vectorize oracle diff > tolerance in tcc check bisect: disable vectorize, re-run; flags lane/remainder bug
Parallel race (Local shared) ThreadSanitizer in CI fuzz privatize Local in region
Thread-pool oversubscribe runtime monitor clamp tasks to num_cpus

8.10 Operational Notes

  • tcc dump scheduled --show-attrs prints per-axis AxisAttr; tcc dump c shows emitted vector/parallel C.
  • Feature flag simd=avx2|sse|scalar; TC_THREADS env overrides pool size; TC_UNROLL_MAX overrides cap.
  • Determinism: parallel reductions disallowed here → bit-reproducible output regardless of thread count.
  • CI: run every fixture under scalar + avx2 + ThreadSanitizer; assert numerical parity across all three.
  • Bench harness emits GFLOP/s + speedup-vs-naive into bench/phase8.json for Phase 13 regression gating.

21. Phase 9 — CPU codegen (C backend + SIMD)

9.1 Objectives

  • Lower a scheduled PrimFunc (post tc-schedule) → emitted C source via CBackend and a portable-SIMD variant via SimdBackend; package as Kernel + Module.
  • Emit one C function per Kernel with a stable KernelSig (data ptrs, shapes, strides) so the runtime ABI is decoupled from kernel internals.
  • Compile emitted C through the system cc (clang/gcc) into a loadable shared object; cache by content hash.
  • Honor schedule annotations: vectorize→SIMD lanes, parallel→OpenMP/thread-pool pragma, unroll→#pragma unroll/manual.
  • Invariant (compute/schedule separation): codegen MUST preserve numerics — scalar CBackend and SimdBackend produce results equal within tolerance (f32 rtol 1e-5/atol 1e-6).
  • Beat the naive baseline (Phase 6 default loop nest, scalar, -O0) on the kernel corpus.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Modules in tc-codegen (crates/tc-codegen/src/):

File Owns
target.rs Target { arch, features }, feature gating (avx2,avx512,neon), lane width lookup
codegen.rs trait CodeGen { fn emit(&self, sched:&Schedule, target:&Target)->Kernel }; shared IR-walk
cbackend.rs struct CBackend; scalar C emitter (Stmt/Expr → C strings)
simd.rs struct SimdBackend; wraps CBackend, rewrites vectorized axis → SIMD intrinsics/vector types
csyntax.rs CWriter (indented buffer), C type map (DType→float/double/int32_t/...), identifier mangling
kernel.rs Kernel { name, source, signature }, KernelSig { params: Vec<ParamDesc> }
compile.rs cc invocation, flags, dlopen handle, content-hash cache
module.rs Module { kernels, graph, plan } assembly
  • KernelSig ABI (LOCKED layout; P8 §6.8 codegen emits stable kernel ABI; ADR-07 C-source default backend; INV-7 §7.8 Kernel↔KernelSig contract): void kfn(float* d0, const int64_t* shape0, const int64_t* strides0, ...) per buffer in PrimFunc.params; signature derived from Buffer{shape,dtype,scope}. ParamDesc{name,dtype,rank,is_output}.
  • Stmt → C: For → for(int64_t v=min; v<min+extent; ++v); Store → buf[idx]=val;; Block → {...}; IfThenElse → if(c){..}else{..}; Allocate{Global} → caller-provided ptr, {Local} → stack/malloc per size.
  • Expr → C: Load→buf[lin(index)]; Bin→infix w/ explicit parens; Call(IntrinId,..)→fmaxf/expf/sqrtf/fmaf; index linearization uses strides from KernelSig (strided view), not assumed-contiguous.
  • Vectorize: for axis tagged vectorize with extent E, lane width W=target.lanes(dtype): emit E/W SIMD steps over __m256/float32x4_t (or GCC __attribute__((vector_size)) portable fallback) + scalar tail when E%W≠0. Loads contiguous along vec axis → _mm256_loadu_ps; strided → gather fallback or de-vectorize.
  • Parallel: outer axis tagged parallel → #pragma omp parallel for (feature openmp) else partition into a tc-runtime thread-pool job descriptor (codegen emits per-tile fn + range).
  • Compile: cc -O3 -march=native -shared -fPIC -fno-math-errno (+-fopenmp); hash (source, target, flags) → cache .so under $TC_CACHE; dlopen+dlsym resolve name.

9.3 Deliverable

tc-codegen lowers any scheduled PrimFunc → compiled Kernel inside a Module; tcc dump c prints emitted C; tcc compile produces a runnable module whose kernels pass numerical check and outrun the naive baseline.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Numerical CBackend & SimdBackend vs oracle: f32 rtol ≤1e-5, atol ≤1e-6
Speedup matmul 256³ SIMD kernel ≥ 4× naive scalar -O0 baseline; ≥ 30% of system BLAS sgemm
Vectorize AVX2 f32 path utilizes 8 lanes; tail handled when extent%8≠0
Compile single kernel emit+cc ≤ 500 ms cold, ≤ 5 ms warm (cache hit)
Snapshot emitted C byte-stable for fixed (PrimFunc, schedule, target)
ABI every KernelSig param order = PrimFunc.params order

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
fixtures/codegen/add_elemwise.c.snap scalar C for add(a,b); matches snapshot byte-for-byte
fixtures/codegen/add_simd_avx2.c.snap AVX2 8-lane body + scalar tail
fixtures/codegen/matmul_tiled.c.snap tiled+vectorized C; inner _mm256_fmadd_ps
fixtures/codegen/strided_transpose.c.snap strided index linearization (no contiguous assumption)
fixtures/codegen/num/*.json input/output pairs; compiled kernel matches oracle within tol
malformed: unscheduled.json err MissingSchedule
malformed: vectorize_strided.json warn VecDegradeToScalar (non-unit stride on vec axis)
malformed: bad_target_avx512.json err UnsupportedFeature when host lacks flag

9.6 Pitfalls

  • Index linearization assuming contiguity → wrong results on strided/transposed views; root cause: ignoring KernelSig strides. Debug: diff emitted idx expr vs sum(i_k*stride_k); fixture strided_transpose.
  • SIMD tail dropped when extent%W≠0 → last lanes uninitialized/garbage; emit scalar remainder loop; debug: set extent=non-multiple, compare boundary elements.
  • Aliasing UB: in/out params marked restrict but executor reuses storage (MemoryPlan) → miscompile under -O3; root cause: false restrict. Fix: only emit restrict when MemoryPlan proves disjoint.
  • -ffast-math reassociation changes reduction order → exceeds tolerance / NaN handling differs; use -fno-math-errno only, never -ffast-math for conformance build.
  • -march=native non-portable cache: cached .so from host A run on host B → SIGILL; key cache on actual Target.features, not "native".
  • Integer overflow in index: i32 loop var × large stride overflows; emit int64_t induction vars always.
  • OpenMP false sharing on reduction accumulators across tiles → correct but slow; pad/privatize per-thread partials.

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • Default -O3 -march=native; rely on cc autovec only for scalar tail, drive main vectorization explicitly via SimdBackend.
  • Inner-loop unroll factor 4–8 to expose ILP; FMA via _mm256_fmadd_ps halves op count.
  • Keep accumulators in registers (SSA temps) across reduce loop; spill = perf cliff.
  • Parallel tile granularity ≥ ~16 KB work/thread to amortize pool dispatch; sub-tile → overhead dominates.
  • Warm .so cache eliminates cc latency in tcc bench/tune inner loop.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • Scalar kernel (no vectorizable axis) → SimdBackend falls back to CBackend output verbatim.
  • Extent < lane width → fully scalar, no SIMD prologue.
  • Zero-extent loop → emit no body (guard extent>0).
  • DType::Bool/I8 SIMD: narrow lanes (32×i8) or de-vectorize if unsupported.
  • Dynamic dim (Dim::Dynamic) → shape passed at runtime via KernelSig ptr; no compile-time unroll on dynamic extent.
  • Single-element tensor (rank-0) → scalar load/store, strides empty.

9.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
cc not found / nonzero exit capture stderr err CompileError{stderr}, dump offending C to tmp
dlsym symbol missing post-dlopen lookup err SymbolNotFound{name} (mangling mismatch)
SIGILL at kernel call target/host feature mismatch re-emit scalar, invalidate cache entry
numeric mismatch vs oracle check fixture bisect: disable vectorize → isolate SIMD vs lowering bug
segfault (OOB index) ASan build (-fsanitize=address) in CI report buffer+index from sanitizer trace

9.10 Operational Notes

  • tcc dump c --target x86_64,avx2 model.tg prints emitted C; tcc dump module lists kernels + sigs.
  • Env: TC_CACHE (so-cache dir), TC_CC (override compiler), TC_CFLAGS (extra flags); log cc cmdline at debug.
  • CI matrix: {scalar, avx2} × {clang, gcc}; ASan + snapshot diff gate; record GFLOP/s in bench.json for regression tracking.
  • GPU codegen is PhaseDeferred (project 248/180); Scope::Shared/Thread IterVars rejected here with GpuDeferred.

22. Phase 10 — Runtime: NDArray, allocator, memory planning, graph executor

Realize tc-runtime: zero-copy strided NDArray, ref-counted Storage + bump/pool Allocator, liveness-driven MemoryPlan reusing storage across non-overlapping live tensors, and a GraphExecutor that binds inputs, dispatches a Module's Kernels in topological order, returns outputs. End-to-end: load a compiled static-shape model, run real inputs, match the reference oracle within tolerance, demonstrate measured peak-memory reduction.

10.1 Objectives

  • NDArray: strided view (shape/strides/offset/dtype over shared Storage); zero-copy slice/reshape/transpose/view; contiguity classification (C/F/strided).
  • Storage: ref-counted byte buffer (ptr/bytes/device); clone bumps refcount, drop frees at zero; never aliased across Device.
  • Allocator trait: alloc(bytes)->Storage, free(Storage); two impls — BumpAllocator (arena, O(1), bulk reset) and PoolAllocator (size-class free-lists, reuse).
  • MemoryPlan: buffer-liveness analysis over executor topo order → slot assignment (interval-graph coloring) → Storage sharing for disjoint lifetimes.
  • GraphExecutor: topo-sort Module.graph, allocate intermediates per MemoryPlan, dispatch Kernels via KernelSig, bind inputs/outputs, validate vs oracle.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Modules under tc-runtime/src/:

File Owns Key items
ndarray.rs NDArray new, from_storage, slice(&[Range]), reshape(&[i64]), transpose(&[usize]), is_contiguous, as_ptr, numel, nbytes
storage.rs Storage Rc<StorageInner>; alloc_for(dtype,numel,&mut dyn Allocator); Drop → free
device.rs Device Cpu live; Gpu = unimplemented! stub (deferred)
alloc.rs Allocator,BumpAllocator,PoolAllocator aligned (align=64, AVX); size-class buckets pow2 256B..16MB
plan.rs MemoryPlan LiveInterval{tensor:NodeId,birth:usize,death:usize,bytes}; plan(graph,topo)->MemoryPlan; slot_of(NodeId)->SlotId
exec.rs GraphExecutor new(Module), set_input(name,NDArray), run(), get_output(idx)->NDArray
dispatch.rs op→kernel binding KernelSig marshalling: (*mut f32, shape[], strides[]); FFI call into compiled kernel
  • NDArray strides: row-major default strides[i]=prod(shape[i+1..]); slice adjusts offset += sum(start_k*stride_k), shrinks shape, keeps strides (no copy); reshape only zero-copy when contiguous, else error (caller inserts contiguous() copy).
  • Liveness (P7 §6.7 memory planning never aliases live tensors; ADR-10 liveness-based MemoryPlan; INV-5 §7.6 MemoryPlan storage disjointness): birth = topo index of producing node; death = max topo index of any consumer; inputs born at -1, outputs death = ∞ (never reused).
  • Planning algorithm: sort intervals by birth; sweep — on birth, assign smallest free slot ≥ bytes (best-fit / first-fit), on death return slot to free pool; slots back Storage regions; record node→(slot,byte_offset).
