  • Executor loop: for node in topo { bind args via plan slots; sig = kernel.signature; call kernel(out_ptr, in_ptrs, shapes, strides); }; constants pre-materialized; outputs copied/aliased out.

10.3 Deliverable

tc-runtime crate: NDArray/Storage/Allocator/MemoryPlan/GraphExecutor. tcc run --model M.tcg --input a.npy,b.npy --check oracle.npy loads the Phase 9 Module, executes, prints peak-memory bytes and pass/fail vs oracle. A 6-op MLP (matmul→add→relu→matmul→add→relu) runs end-to-end matching the reference within rtol 1e-5.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Correctness: MLP + conv-relu corpus output matches oracle, f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6, all pass.
  • Zero-copy: slice/reshape(contiguous)/transpose allocate 0 bytes (asserted via allocator counter); as_ptr aliases parent Storage.
  • Memory reduction: MemoryPlan peak ≤ 60% of naive (one buffer per intermediate) on the 6-op MLP; measured + logged.
  • Determinism: repeated run() byte-identical outputs; allocator reset leaves 0 leaked bytes (refcount audit == 0).
  • Dispatch overhead: per-op binding < 1µs (excludes kernel compute) on the MLP.

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Expected
fixtures/exec/mlp.tcg + mlp.in.npy output == mlp.oracle.npy within tol
fixtures/exec/conv_relu.tcg matches NumPy conv2d+relu oracle
fixtures/plan/diamond.tcg plan reuses 1 slot for the two parallel branch temps; peak = 3 buffers not 5
fixtures/ndarray/slice_view.json slice([1..3,..]) → expected shape/strides/offset, alloc_count==0
fixtures/ndarray/reshape_strided.json (malformed) reshape of non-contiguous → Err(NonContiguous)
fixtures/plan/output_alias.tcg (malformed) planner refusing to reuse an output slot → live to ∞

10.6 Pitfalls

  • Reusing a still-live buffer (death computed before all consumers found) → silent numerical corruption; root cause: incomplete consumer scan; debug: dump LiveInterval table, assert no overlap among same-slot tensors.
  • Aliased output written by in-place kernel → input clobbered; fix: planner marks input/output slots disjoint unless op declares in-place safe.
  • Non-contiguous reshape treated as zero-copy → garbage reads; classify contiguity first, error or copy.
  • Refcount cycle / leak via Rc<StorageInner> retained in NDArray cache → free never fires; debug: refcount audit at run() end must hit 0.
  • Alignment: passing unaligned as_ptr into a SIMD kernel → segfault/UB; allocator must 64-byte align every slot base.
  • Stride/shape int overflow on large tensors (i64 numel) → wrong offsets; checked-mul in numel/nbytes.

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Allocation off the hot path: MemoryPlan resolves all sizes at compile time; run() does zero malloc for static graphs (single arena from plan total).
  • Slot coloring minimizes peak; best-fit vs first-fit chosen by lower peak on corpus.
  • Dispatch is pointer-arithmetic + FFI call; avoid per-op Vec allocs (reuse arg scratch buffers).
  • Pool allocator size-classes amortize VM/dynamic-shape churn (Phase 11); bump for static one-shot.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-size tensor (numel==0): valid Storage of 0 bytes, kernel skipped.
  • Single-node graph / output == input (identity): alias, no copy.
  • Scalar (rank-0) NDArray: empty shape/strides, numel==1.
  • Broadcast inputs: strides with 0 stride dims; kernel honors, no expansion copy.
  • In-place op chain (relu over its own buffer): plan must allow same slot in==out when op-safe.

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
OOM in alloc alloc returns Err/null abort run(), report required vs available
Oracle mismatch post-run compare > tol dump per-op output, diff vs oracle stage-by-stage
Refcount leak end-of-run audit ≠ 0 log leaked StorageIds + producing node
Slot overlap debug-build interval assert panic with offending tensor pair
Kernel sig mismatch KernelSig arity/dtype check pre-call reject load of Module

10.10 Operational Notes

  • tcc run --dump-plan emits the slot table (tensor→slot, byte ranges, peak bytes); --no-plan runs naive for A/B peak comparison.
  • Feature flags: bump (default) vs pool allocator; --device cpu only (gpu stub errors).
  • Telemetry: per-run peak bytes, alloc count, dispatch µs/op logged to stderr at -v.
  • Phase 11 (tc-vm) reuses NDArray/Storage/Allocator/dispatch; only planning moves to runtime for dynamic shapes.

23. Phase 11 — The bytecode VM (dynamic shapes + control flow)

The GraphExecutor (Phase 10) is static-shape, branch-free, topological. Models with data-dependent control flow (If, dynamic loops), variable-shape inputs (Dim::Dynamic), and runtime-allocated tensors cannot be lowered to a fixed schedule. tc-vm adds a register-based bytecode Interpreter that dispatches the same compiled Kernels (from Module) but decides shapes, allocation, and branch targets at runtime. Compute/schedule separation is preserved: the VM never recompiles kernels, it only orchestrates their invocation.

11.1 Objectives

  • Define VmInstruction set: LoadConst, AllocTensor, InvokeKernel, If, Goto, Call, Ret (+ closure/object support) — register machine, not stack machine.
  • Interpreter: register file of NDArray/objects, call stack of frames, program counter; executes a VmFunc.
  • Dynamic shapes: ShapeRef resolves Dim::Dynamic symbols → concrete i64 at runtime; AllocTensor allocates via Allocator at dispatch time (no static MemoryPlan).
  • Control flow: If(cond_reg, then_label, else_label), Goto(label), Call(func, args, ret) with recursion + closures.
  • Device abstraction routing + async op dispatch onto the runtime thread pool with dependency tracking.
  • Deliverable: execute a control-flow + dynamic-shape model end-to-end, numerically matching the oracle.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files under tc-vm/src/:

File Contents
instr.rs VmInstruction, RegId(u32), Label(u32), ConstId, KernelId, FuncId, ShapeRef
func.rs VmFunc{ instrs, num_regs, params, consts }, label→pc table, VmModule{ funcs, kernels, consts }
value.rs VmObject enum: Tensor(NDArray), Shape(Vec<i64>), Tuple(Vec<VmObject>), Closure(Closure), Int(i64)
interp.rs Interpreter, Frame{ func, pc, regs: Vec<Option<VmObject>> }, fetch–decode–execute loop
shape.rs ShapeRef eval: Const(i64), Sym(SymId), BinOp over runtime symbol env; symbol table per frame
dispatch.rs async InvokeKernel → thread pool, TaskGraph of pending ops, await_reg barrier
lower.rs Graph IR (with control-flow ops) → VmFunc lowering; static subgraphs → fused InvokeKernel chains 
ShapeRef := Const i64 | Sym SymId | Bin op ShapeRef ShapeRef    (* op ∈ +,*,-,/, ceildiv *)
VmObject := Tensor NDArray | Shape (i64 list) | Tuple (VmObject list)
          | Closure { func: FuncId, captures: RegId list } | Int i64
  • Lowering (lower.rs; P10 §6.10 static graph→GraphExecutor, dynamic→VM; ADR-11 static-vs-dynamic split; INV-10 §7.11 VM runtime type/shape safety; paper R5 §5.3 TVM Relay VM Instruction set): graph regions without control flow → linear InvokeKernel sequences reusing Module.kernels; If/loop ops → basic blocks with If/Goto; recursive Calls preserved. Each InvokeKernel(KernelId, [in regs], out reg) binds NDArray views to the kernel's KernelSig.
  • Shape resolution: at AllocTensor(reg, shaperef), evaluate ShapeRef against the frame's symbol env (populated from input shapes on entry), then Allocator::alloc(bytes) → Storage → NDArray (contiguous strides).
  • Interpreter loop: match instrs[pc] dispatch; If reads bool/I32 scalar from cond_reg (host-read 1 element), sets pc=label; Call pushes Frame, binds arg regs → callee params; Ret(reg) pops frame, writes result into caller's ret reg.
  • Closures: Closure{func, captures} packages captured regs; invoked via Call resolving captures into the new frame's leading regs.
  • Async (dispatch.rs): InvokeKernel enqueues a task keyed by output RegId; reads of a not-ready reg block on its task (RAW dependency); thread pool = the runtime's (Phase 10) pool; Device::Cpu only (Gpu deferred → returns DeviceUnsupported).

11.3 Deliverable

tc-vm crate: full VmInstruction/VmFunc/Interpreter; lower.rs graph→VmFunc. tcc run --vm <model> --inputs ... and tcc dump vm <model> (disassembly). Executes dynamic-control-flow + dynamic-shape models, dispatching Module kernels, matching the oracle within f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • cargo test -p tc-vm green; tcc run --vm on the fixture corpus matches oracle within tolerance for all branch/shape combinations exercised.
  • Data-dependent If: both arms validated by forcing the predicate true/false from input.
  • Dynamic shape: same VmFunc runs on ≥3 distinct input shapes (e.g. seq_len ∈ {1, 17, 256}), each matching oracle; no recompilation (Module kernel set unchanged across shapes).
  • Recursion: a recursively-Called VmFunc (depth ≥ 32) terminates with correct result; stack overflow → clean VmError::StackOverflow at configurable limit (default 1024 frames).
  • Async path matches single-threaded path bit-for-bit; thread count ∈ {1, 4, 8} produces identical output.

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
vm_if_relu_vs_abs.txt scalar predicate + tensor branch-selected op, oracle-matched both arms
vm_dynamic_matmul.txt M,K,N dynamic dims correct for shapes {1×4×4, 17×33×8, 256×256×256}
vm_while_reduce.txt data-dependent trip count loop result == reference fold
vm_recursive_sum.txt depth N == iterative reference
vm_closure_apply.txt closure over captured tensor == direct application
vm_async_chain.txt long InvokeKernel chain identical across thread counts

Malformed: vm_bad_label.txt (Goto to undefined label → VmError::BadLabel at load); vm_reg_unbound.txt (read before write → VmError::UnboundReg); vm_shape_underflow.txt (ShapeRef resolves to negative dim → VmError::InvalidShape); vm_type_mismatch.txt (If on a tensor reg, not scalar → VmError::NotScalar); vm_arity.txt (Call arg count ≠ params → VmError::Arity).

11.6 Pitfalls

  • Reading If predicate forces a sync point: a host-side scalar read of cond_reg must await its producing task; forgetting drains a stale/zero value → wrong branch. Root cause: missing RAW barrier on control-flow reads. Debug: dump vm + per-instr task-state trace.
  • Dynamic alloc fragments the arena: per-AllocTensor runtime allocation (no MemoryPlan reuse) fragments and thrashes. Mitigate with a free-list Allocator + reg-liveness free at last use; debug with allocation high-water-mark counter.
  • Register liveness across branches: a reg written in only one If arm is unbound on the other path → UnboundReg. Root cause: lowering must dominate-define regs or insert phi-like merge writes; debug with a load-time def/use verifier.
  • Symbol-env leakage across Call: child frame inheriting parent's dynamic symbols silently uses wrong dims. Each Frame owns a fresh symbol table seeded only from passed shapes.
  • Async use-after-free: freeing a Storage whose tensor is still an input to an in-flight task → UB. Refcount Storage; defer free until dependent tasks complete.
  • Strided kernel inputs: VM-allocated tensors are contiguous, but a Reshape/slice produces strided views; passing to a contiguity-assuming Kernel → garbage. Insert a contiguity check + copy, or assert in KernelSig.

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Interpreter dispatch overhead: target < 200 ns/instr; amortized vs ms-scale kernel cost (overhead < 1% on real models). Use a match on a #[repr(u8)] opcode, not dynamic dispatch.
  • Per-op runtime allocation costs ~µs; reuse a free-list Allocator and reg-liveness-driven frees to keep alloc off the hot path.
  • Async dispatch latency: task enqueue/dequeue < 500 ns; small ops below a FLOP threshold run inline (no task) to avoid thread-pool overhead dominating.
  • Dynamic shape ⇒ no autotuned schedule ⇒ kernels run at default/conservative schedule; document the static-vs-VM throughput gap (expect VM 0.6–0.9× of GraphExecutor on shapes the static path can specialize).

11.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-extent dynamic dim (e.g. seq_len 0): AllocTensor of 0 bytes → valid empty NDArray; kernels skip; reduce over empty → identity.
  • Branch producing differently-shaped outputs: merge reg's TensorType must be the dynamic-shape union; downstream consumers handle either.
  • Unbalanced loop (trip count 0): loop body never runs; Ret carries the pre-loop value.
  • Closure capturing a since-freed reg: captures are refcounted at closure creation, pinning Storage.
  • Recursion to the frame limit: clean StackOverflow, no native stack blowup (heap-allocated frames).
  • Scalar predicate of F32/I32/Bool dtype: all coerce to bool (nonzero = true); other dtypes/non-scalar → NotScalar.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
BadLabel / undefined FuncId load-time verifier reject VmModule, no execution
UnboundReg run-time read of None reg abort with reg id + pc
InvalidShape (negative/overflow dim) ShapeRef eval check abort before alloc
Arity / NotScalar / TypeMismatch per-instr type guard abort with diagnostic
StackOverflow frame-count limit abort, report call chain
AllocFailure (OOM) Allocator::alloc returns err abort, report bytes + high-water
async task panic join handle catches propagate as VmError::KernelPanic, no silent corruption

11.10 Operational Notes

  • tcc dump vm emits human-readable disassembly (labels resolved, regs named) for debugging.
  • TC_VM_TRACE=1 logs per-instr pc/opcode/reg-states; TC_VM_THREADS=N overrides pool size (default = runtime pool).
  • VM is selected by tcc run --vm or auto when the imported graph contains control-flow / dynamic dims; static graphs default to GraphExecutor.
  • Determinism: with TC_VM_THREADS=1 execution is fully deterministic; multi-thread must match (no reduction-order nondeterminism — async affects scheduling, not numerics).
  • Limits configurable: frame cap, async inline-FLOP threshold, allocator arena size — surfaced via tcc flags + env, documented in the VM section of --help.

24. Phase 12 — Auto-tuning / schedule search

12.1 Objectives

  • Bounded search space over tc-schedule primitives per fused PrimFunc; enumerate/sample legal schedules.
  • Cost model: analytical roofline (FLOPs / bytes vs peak GFLOP/s + memory BW) + measured wall-time of compiled candidates.
  • Tuner: select best Schedule per fused kernel within a wall-clock/trial budget; cache winner keyed by kernel signature + Target.
  • Correctness gate: every candidate validated vs reference oracle (rtol 1e-5/atol 1e-6); illegal/incorrect schedules rejected, never measured.
  • Win target: tuned GFLOP/s ≥ default-schedule GFLOP/s on the kernel corpus; matmul ≥ 5× naive.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

New module tc-schedule/src/search/ — does NOT alter Schedule/Stage/primitives (locked).

File Contents
search/space.rs SearchSpace, ConfigKnob, ScheduleConfig, enumerate()/sample()
search/cost.rs CostModel trait, RooflineCost (analytical), MeasuredCost (timed)
search/tuner.rs Tuner, TuneOpts, tune(), TuneResult, early-stop
search/cache.rs ScheduleCache, on-disk JSON keyed by SchedKey 
pub struct ScheduleConfig { pub tile: Vec<i64>, pub reorder: Vec<AxisId>,
    pub vec_axis: Option<AxisId>, pub unroll: i64, pub parallel: Option<AxisId> }
pub struct SearchSpace { knobs: Vec<ConfigKnob> } // tile factors ∈ divisors∪pow2; vec∈inner axes; unroll∈{1,2,4,8}
impl SearchSpace {
    pub fn from_primfunc(f: &PrimFunc, t: &Target) -> Self;     // derive legal knob ranges from loop extents
    pub fn sample(&self, rng: &mut Rng, n: usize) -> Vec<ScheduleConfig>;
    pub fn apply(&self, f: &PrimFunc, c: &ScheduleConfig) -> Result<Schedule, IllegalSchedule>; }
pub trait CostModel { fn cost(&self, s: &Schedule, t: &Target) -> f64; } // lower = faster, seconds est.
pub struct RooflineCost { peak_gflops: f64, mem_bw: f64 } // cost = max(flops/peak, bytes/bw) + tile-reuse model
pub struct Tuner<'a> { space: SearchSpace, model: Box<dyn CostModel>, cache: ScheduleCache }
pub struct TuneOpts { pub budget: Duration, pub max_trials: usize, pub topk_measure: usize, pub seed: u64 }
pub fn tune(f: &PrimFunc, t: &Target, o: &TuneOpts) -> TuneResult; // {best: Schedule, gflops, trials, log}
  • Algorithm (P11 §6.11 bounded compile+search budgets; ADR-14 analytical+measured auto-tuner; paper P3 §5.2 Ansor sketch/measure loop): (1) build SearchSpace from loop extents; (2) sample N candidates; (3) score all via RooflineCost (cheap, no compile); (4) take top-K (topk_measure, e.g. 32); (5) for each: apply→CBackend/SimdBackend compile→numerics check vs oracle→time 3 runs (median, discard warmup); (6) keep min measured; (7) loop until budget/trials exhausted; (8) cache.
  • Search strategy: random sample + local hill-climb on top-K (mutate one knob); evolutionary optional. Analytical model prunes ≥90% before any compile.
  • SchedKey = hash(PrimFunc structure, input shapes, dtype, Target.arch+features); cache hit skips search.
  • CLI: tcc tune <model> --budget 60s --trials 512 --out sched.json; tcc compile --schedules sched.json consumes cache.

12.3 Deliverable

Auto-tuner: tcc tune produces a ScheduleCache (JSON) mapping each fused kernel → best ScheduleConfig + measured GFLOP/s; tcc compile applies cached schedules; demonstrable speedup over default on the corpus.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Tuned matmul (1024³, f32) ≥ 5× naive; ≥ 1.5× the Phase-8 default vectorized schedule; ≥ 30% of measured peak.
  • Tuning a kernel within --budget 60s finishes ≤ 60s wall, ≥ 50 candidates evaluated.
  • Every measured candidate passes oracle check; ≥1 deliberately-illegal config in corpus is rejected pre-measure (logged, not crashed).
  • Cache hit on identical SchedKey returns in <10ms (no re-search); tcc compile --schedules reproduces tuned GFLOP/s ±5%.
  • Roofline top-K overlaps true top-K (Spearman ≥ 0.5 on corpus) — model is predictive, not random.

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
tune/matmul_1024.json best config tiles {≥32}, vectorize inner; gflops ≥ 5× naive baseline recorded
tune/conv2d_nchw.json legal config, numerics pass, gflops > default
tune/elemwise_fused.json tuner picks vectorize+parallel; memory-bound → roofline cost ≈ bytes/bw
tune/cache_hit.json second tune call same key → trials: 0, source: cache
tune/illegal_tile7.bad tile factor 7 on extent 1024 (non-divisor + remainder) → IllegalSchedule, rejected
tune/wrong_numerics.bad injected mis-reorder altering result → oracle fail → candidate dropped, tuner continues

Each .json golden stores {config, gflops, oracle: pass}; .bad asserts rejection reason, no panic.

12.6 Pitfalls

  • Schedule changes result → root cause: bad reorder across reduce axis or wrong tile residue handling. Debug: diff oracle per candidate; never trust cost model alone for correctness.
  • Tuning noise → cold cache/CPU freq scaling/co-tenant load inflates variance; root cause: single-run timing. Fix: median of 3, warmup discard, pin thread affinity, report stddev.
  • Search collapse to one knob → root cause: dominant tile knob masks vectorize gains; hill-climb stuck in local min. Debug: log per-knob marginal; restart from random seeds.
  • Roofline lies for cache-blocked matmul → analytical model ignores L1/L2 reuse → ranks tiny tiles best. Fix: add tile-reuse term (working-set vs cache size); measure top-K not top-1.
  • Combinatorial blowup → product of all knobs >10⁶; root cause: unbounded tile×reorder×unroll. Fix: cap per-knob cardinality, sample not enumerate, budget on trials.
  • Cache key collision → two kernels same structure diff shapes share key → wrong schedule applied. Debug: include resolved shapes + dtype in SchedKey; assert extent match on load.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Compile dominates tuning wall-time (cc invocation ~100–500ms/candidate) → parallelize candidate compiles across threads; reuse process; cache object files by config hash.
  • Analytical pre-filter mandatory: scoring 512 configs <1ms total vs 512 compiles ~minutes → measure only top-K.
  • Timing harness: flush caches between trials for memory-bound kernels; minimum 3 runs, ≥10ms aggregate per measurement to beat timer resolution.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • Loop extent prime / not power-of-2 → tile factors restricted to divisors + remainder loop; tuner must keep legal set non-empty (identity schedule always present).
  • Tiny kernels (extent < SIMD width) → vectorize illegal → space degenerates; fall back to default, mark untuned.
  • Dynamic shape (Dim::Dynamic) → no static extent → tuner skipped, default schedule used; VM tunes at runtime deferred.
  • Single-iteration / scalar reduce → empty search space → return default, trials: 0, not error.
  • Budget exhausted before any measured candidate completes → return best-by-analytical-cost with warning.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Response
Candidate compile fails (cc error) nonzero exit drop candidate, log, continue
Candidate numerics fail oracle rtol/atol reject pre-measure, never cache
All candidates illegal empty legal set return default schedule, exit 0 + warn
Measured slower than default min vs baseline keep default, record "no improvement"
Cache corrupt/stale (key mismatch) shape assert on load ignore entry, re-tune

12.10 Operational Notes

  • tcc tune --budget --trials --topk --seed --jobs; deterministic given seed (logged for repro).
  • Cache at .tc-cache/schedules.json; portable across runs, NOT across Target (arch/features in key); CI ships a pre-tuned cache for the corpus to keep tcc compile fast + reproducible.
  • tcc tune --report emits per-kernel table: default vs tuned GFLOP/s, % peak, trials, wall-time; CI gates on no-regression vs committed baseline.
  • GPU/Ansor-style learned cost model = PhaseDeferred; this phase is analytical-roofline + measured only.

25. Phase 13 — Conformance, differential testing, fuzzing, and benchmarks

This is the project's "done" gate: numerical correctness vs a reference oracle, cross-execution-path agreement, crash/divergence-free fuzzing, and a published GFLOP/s performance report with regression gates.

13.1 Objectives

  • Numerical conformance: op + small-model corpus through full tc-graph → … → Module → tc-runtime pipeline, compared to a NumPy-style reference oracle within documented tolerance (f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6).
  • Differential testing: compiled-vs-oracle, GraphExecutor-vs-tc-vm agreement, scalar CBackend-vs-SimdBackend agreement, every schedule transform vs the naive PrimFunc (compute/schedule invariant).
  • Fuzzing: random legal Graph generator + random legal Schedule generator — must never panic/UB and must stay within tolerance.
  • Benchmarks: criterion harness + GFLOP/s vs naive baseline, % of theoretical peak, regression gates wired into CI.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Modules/files (all under existing crates + a new tc-conformance dev-only harness crate in the workspace, not a published member):

File Owns
tc-conformance/src/oracle.rs Oracle trait + RefTensor (f64-internal NDArray), reference impls of every GraphOp (Add/Mul/MatMul/Conv2d/Relu/Reshape/Transpose/Reduce)
tc-conformance/src/corpus.rs OpCase, ModelCase, corpus loader from tests/corpus/*.tgraph (textual graph) + golden *.npy-style *.ref blobs
tc-conformance/src/diff.rs compare(got: &NDArray, want: &RefTensor, tol: Tol) -> DiffReport; Tol{ rtol, atol, ulp }; ULP + relative + absolute + NaN/Inf policy
tc-conformance/src/gengraph.rs GraphGen — shape-typed random Graph builder (well-typed by construction)
tc-conformance/src/genschedule.rs ScheduleGen — random legal sequence of Schedule primitives for a given PrimFunc
tc-conformance/benches/kernels.rs criterion groups: matmul, conv2d, fused-elementwise, reduce; GFLOP/s computed from FLOP count / wall time
tc-cli tcc check (corpus run vs oracle) + tcc bench (report emit) wire to harness

Algorithms:

  • Oracle (P1 §6.1 the reference oracle is truth; ADR-13 external NumPy-style oracle; ADR-20 generator-based fuzzing): compute every op in f64 then cast to target dtype for comparison; deterministic, no SIMD, row-major. Single authority for "correct".
  • Differential pivots: for each ModelCase, run 4 paths — (a) oracle, (b) GraphExecutor over CBackend Module, (c) GraphExecutor over SimdBackend Module, (d) tc-vm Interpreter over same Module — assert pairwise within Tol.
  • Schedule invariant: pick PrimFunc, apply ScheduleGen sequence, codegen, run, compare to naive (unscheduled) Module output with tight tol (split/reorder/tile/fuse must be bit-exact for integer dtypes; f32 reductions allow reassociation slack).
  • GraphGen: maintain a typed value pool Vec<(NodeId, TensorType)>; each step picks an op whose arg-type constraints are satisfiable from the pool, infers result TensorType, appends; guarantees shape inference will succeed (no malformed graphs by construction). Seeded SmallRng.
  • ScheduleGen: tracks current axis set + extents; only proposes legal primitives (no vectorize on non-innermost-after-split unless lane-aligned; no parallel on a reduce axis; compute_at only at an enclosing axis of a true consumer). Each chosen primitive recorded for replay.
  • GFLOP/s: flops / median_secs / 1e9; peak via cores × SIMD_lanes × FMA(2) × clock.

13.3 Deliverable

cargo test -p tc-conformance green across all corpus buckets; tcc check tests/corpus/ exits 0 with a per-op/per-model pass table; fuzz target runs N iterations (CI budget) with zero crashes / zero tolerance divergences; tcc bench --report bench/report.md emits GFLOP/s + % peak + speedup-vs-naive table; criterion regression gate in CI.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Op corpus pass rate 100% of ops within f32 rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6 (f64 rtol 1e-12)
Model corpus pass rate 100% of small models (≤30 nodes) within tolerance
Path agreement oracle ≡ GraphExecutor ≡ VM ≡ CBackend ≡ SimdBackend, all pairs pass
Schedule invariant random legal schedule output == naive within tol, 1000 seeds
Fuzz 0 panics / 0 UB (ASan/Miri clean) / 0 divergences over ≥50k graph seeds + 10k schedule seeds (CI: 5k/2k)
matmul GFLOP/s ≥40% of single-core peak after tile+vectorize+unroll; ≥8× naive
fused elementwise ≥3× vs unfused (intermediate-materialization elimination)
Regression gate CI fails on >5% median regression vs stored baseline

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Expected output Malformed case
corpus/add_f32_1024.tgraph + .ref elementwise add matches oracle within tol add_shape_mismatch.tgraph → shape-infer error, not crash
corpus/matmul_128x128.tgraph + .ref GEMM within f32 rtol 1e-5 matmul_inner_mismatch.tgraph → typed rejection
corpus/conv2d_nchw_3x3.tgraph + .ref conv output matches; NHWC variant after layout pass matches conv_neg_pad.tgraph → diagnostic
corpus/relu_reduce_sum.tgraph + .ref fused reduce within tol; reduce reassociation noted —
corpus/mlp_2layer.tgraph + .ref full model end-to-end within tol mlp_dangling_node.tgraph → DCE removes / verifier flags
fuzz/seed_*.bin replayable graph+schedule seed corrupt seed → generator rejects, no panic

DiffReport prints first failing index, got/want, abs+rel error, ULP distance.

13.6 Pitfalls

  • Reduction reassociation: f32 sum order differs naive-vs-tiled/vectorized → false failure. Root cause: non-associative fp. Fix: tolerance accounts for O(sqrt(n))·eps error growth; debug by comparing in f64 oracle and inspecting per-element rel error histogram.
  • Oracle in same precision as compiled: hides compiler bugs (both wrong identically). Root cause: shared rounding. Fix: oracle computes in f64 then casts; never reuse compiled kernels in oracle path.
  • Generator only emits trivially-foldable graphs: const-fold collapses everything → coverage illusion. Fix: GraphGen forces ≥1 Input on every value chain; track "live input reach" per node.
  • Illegal schedule slips through as "fuzz crash": bug is in ScheduleGen legality check, not codegen. Disambiguate: replay seed under a Schedule::validate() assertion before codegen; if validate rejects, it's a generator bug.
  • Nondeterministic parallel reduction: thread-count-dependent partial sums → flaky CI. Fix: pin parallel fuzz runs to fixed thread count; record thread count in seed metadata.
  • NaN/Inf masking: assert (got-want).abs() < atol is true when both NaN-propagate differently. Fix: explicit NaN==NaN and signed-Inf equality policy in compare.

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Benchmark warmup + ≥100 samples to stabilize median; pin CPU governor / disable turbo for repeatable GFLOP/s.
  • Exclude cc compile time from kernel timing; measure compiled Kernel execution only (compile time tracked separately, target <2s per kernel).
  • Fuzz throughput: seeded SmallRng, no allocation in hot generate loop where avoidable; corpus run parallelized across cases (deterministic per-case).
  • Report both single-thread (codegen quality) and parallel-scheduled (scaling) numbers; report % theoretical peak and % of a reference BLAS sgemm.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-size dims / empty tensors → kernel must no-op, oracle agrees; degenerate Shape([0]).
  • Dynamic-shape model: only tc-vm path runs; oracle fed concrete runtime shape; GraphExecutor path skipped with recorded reason.
  • Single-element reduce, reduce over all axes, keepdims vs not.
  • Non-contiguous NDArray input (strided/transposed view) — zero-copy path must match a materialized-copy oracle.
  • Subnormal / very large f32 inputs near overflow; mixed-dtype graph (I32 indices + F32 data).
  • 1-iteration loops where unroll/vectorize factor exceeds extent (tail handling).

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery / action
Compiled ≠ oracle compare DiffReport bisect: check CBackend first (isolate SIMD), then per-pass (tcc dump after each pass)
GraphExecutor ≠ VM path-agreement assert dump VM instr trace + executor dispatch order; likely MemoryPlan aliasing bug
Schedule changes result invariant test, seed logged minimize primitive sequence (delta-debug) to smallest diverging schedule
Fuzz panic catch_unwind in harness persist seed to fuzz/crashes/, auto-add as regression fixture
Bench regression CI gate vs baseline.json flag commit, attach flamegraph, block merge until justified or fixed

Crash-corpus minimization: shrink failing graph (remove nodes while still diverging) before filing.

13.10 Operational Notes

  • CI matrix: cargo test -p tc-conformance, fuzz (bounded iters), criterion bench (compare-against-baseline), Miri on harness for UB, ASan on emitted-kernel runner.
  • Tolerances + thread count + corpus version pinned in tests/corpus/MANIFEST.toml; oracle is the single source of truth and is version-locked.
  • Baseline bench/baseline.json committed; regenerated only on intentional perf change with sign-off; gate = ±5% median.
  • tcc check is the human-facing one-shot gate (exit code = pass/fail); tcc bench publishes bench/report.md to artifacts.
  • Reproducibility: every fuzz failure reduces to a committed seed + replayable primitive list; no result accepted unless oracle-validated.

Threat Model

26.1 Scope, assets, attacker model

Asset class Why it matters Loss on compromise
Compile host (tcc compile/tune) runs untrusted graph through tc-pass/tc-fuse/tc-schedule/tc-codegen + invokes cc RCE, DoS, exfil of build env
Emitted C kernels + Module tc-codegen output, compiled by cc, dlopened by tc-runtime RCE on execute host, OOB memory
NDArray storage / Storage.ptr live tensor buffers in tc-runtime/tc-vm OOB read/write, info leak, corruption
Numerical result the contract: schedule changes preserve value within tolerance silent wrong answers (worst: undetected)
Build supply chain cc toolchain, crate deps, pinned IR trojaned kernels, reproducibility loss

Attacker capabilities (assume all): (a) supplies an arbitrary model file (ONNX-subset / textual graph); (b) supplies arbitrary input tensors at run; (c) may control schedule hints / tune config; (d) CANNOT modify tc-* source, the pinned cc, or deny.toml. Trust = code (trusted) vs data (graph + tensors + tune config, all UNTRUSTED).

26.2 Trust boundaries

   UNTRUSTED data                         TRUSTED code (tc-* + pinned cc)
   ───────────                            ──────────────────────────────
   model file ──┐
                ▼  [B1 import/verify]
            tc-graph Graph ─────────────► tc-pass / tc-fuse / tc-schedule  (in-proc, typed IR)
                                                     │
                                          [B2 codegen]│ emit C ONLY from typed TIR
                                                     ▼
                                            cc (sandboxed, pinned) ──► .so
                                                     │
                                          [B3 LOAD] dlopen Module  ◄── strongest boundary
                                                     ▼
   input tensors ─[B4 bind/shape-check]─► tc-runtime GraphExecutor / tc-vm Interpreter
                                                     ▼
                                            output (oracle-checked)
  • B1 import: only boundary where attacker bytes become IR; every node MUST be typed + verified before any pass runs.
  • B2 codegen: kernels emitted EXCLUSIVELY from Stmt/Expr/Buffer typed IR — never from raw user strings, names, or attributes. No user string reaches the C source except through an escaped/validated identifier whitelist.
  • B3 load (compile→load→execute): the highest-risk boundary. A .so produced on host A may run on host B. The loaded artifact is native code with full process privilege. Mitigations §26.3-T4.
  • B4 bind: runtime input shapes/dtypes re-checked against KernelSig before dispatch; dynamic dims resolved + bounds-validated in the VM.

26.3 Threat enumeration

Each: vector → mitigation → detection → telemetry → recovery.

T1 — Compile-time DoS (graph/fusion/search blowup)

  • Vector: huge graph (10⁶ nodes), pathological deep fusion chain, combinatorial schedule search, exponential shape-inference fixpoint, adversarial constant-fold (fold a 10 GB tensor).
  • Mitigation: hard caps — max_nodes (default 2²⁰), max_edges, max_fusion_group_size (default 256 ops), max_const_fold_bytes (default 64 MiB), tune_budget (wall-clock + trial count, default 5 min / 1000 trials), shape-inference iteration cap (default 10·|nodes|) with non-convergence = reject. Pass-manager global compile deadline.
  • Detection: cap exceeded → typed CompileError::ResourceCap{kind,limit,observed}, fail closed.
  • Telemetry: per-pass node count, peak RSS, fixpoint iterations, fusion group sizes, tune trials/elapsed.
  • Recovery: abort compile; partial artifacts discarded; caps configurable but never unbounded.

T2 — Shape / type confusion

  • Vector: model declares mismatched shapes/dtypes (e.g. MatMul [m,k]·[j,n], k≠j), negative/zero dims, rank mismatch, dtype that op doesn't support, Dim::Dynamic used where static required.
  • Mitigation: shape inference (Phase 2) propagates TensorType to fixpoint; the verifier (tc-pass) asserts every GraphNode.ty == Some post-inference; per-op typing rules reject illegal combos at IMPORT, before fusion/codegen.
  • Detection: VerifyError::Untyped(NodeId) / TypeError{op,expected,got} at B1.
  • Telemetry: count of rejected nodes by reason; histogram of inferred ranks.
  • Recovery: reject whole model; no IR with None type ever reaches tc-fuse. Invariant: no untyped node survives Phase 2.

T3 — OOB access in a generated kernel

  • Vector: bad strides/indices in emitted loop nest, off-by-one in tiled remainder, schedule split with non-dividing factor producing OOB tail, dynamic shape exceeding allocated buffer.
  • Mitigation: KernelSig is the contract (pointers + shapes + strides, all passed explicitly); debug builds emit bounds-checked codegen (each Load/Store index range-asserted against buffer extent); split/tile remainder handling proven against extent in tc-schedule; release builds rely on the schedule-correctness invariant + fuzz coverage.
  • Detection: debug bounds assert traps; AddressSanitizer in CI on emitted kernels; differential fuzzing (graph + schedule generators) must not OOB.
  • Telemetry: per-kernel assert hit counts (debug); ASan reports in CI.
  • Recovery: assert → abort kernel, surface RuntimeError::Bounds{kernel,index,extent}; CI gate blocks merge on any ASan finding.

T4 — codegen→cc→dlopen arbitrary-code-execution surface

  • Vector (the dominant RCE risk): emitted C compiled then loaded as native code. Injection if any user string reaches the C source unescaped; or a trojaned .so; or #include/pragma injection via op names/attrs.
  • Mitigation:
    • emit C ONLY from typed IR; identifiers from a generated whitelist (k0, buf3, …), never user-derived; numeric consts emitted via formatter, never string-spliced.
    • invoke a pinned cc (exact version + hash) with a fixed, minimal flag set; -I/-include/-D from user input forbidden; -nostdinc + an allowlisted include dir.
    • sandbox the cc invocation and the loaded module: seccomp-bpf profile (no exec, no network, restricted openat to the build tmpdir), separate uid, no env passthrough; emitted source written to an O_TMPFILE-style locked path.
    • artifact integrity: Module records the SHA-256 of each Kernel.source and the resulting .so; load verifies the hash chain; cross-host load requires matching Target.arch/features or refuses.
  • Detection: hash mismatch on load; seccomp violation killed + logged; static scan of emitted source for forbidden tokens (#include, system, __asm) before cc.
  • Telemetry: cc invocation args + version, sandbox denials, load hash-verify pass/fail, dlopen count.
  • Recovery: any mismatch/violation → refuse to load, quarantine artifact. Optional ahead-of-time mode separates the trusted compile host from the untrusted execute host across B3.

T5 — Numerical-correctness attack (schedule silently changes results)

  • Vector: a schedule (reorder of a non-associative reduce, unsafe vectorize, bad compute_at) that produces a value differing beyond tolerance; or a tuner that overfits to a single input.
  • Mitigation: compute/schedule separation invariant — every candidate schedule's output is checked against the reference oracle within documented tolerance (f32 rtol 1e-5, atol 1e-6; f64 tighter) before acceptance; the tuner rejects any schedule failing the oracle regardless of speed; reduction reordering only when the op is flagged associative-safe (or within fp tolerance).
  • Detection: oracle diff > tolerance → ScheduleError::NumericDrift{kernel,max_abs,max_rel}.
  • Telemetry: per-candidate max abs/rel error, fraction of schedules rejected for drift.
  • Recovery: drop the candidate; fall back to the validated naive PrimFunc. Invariant: a Module never ships an unvalidated schedule.

T6 — MemoryPlan aliasing bug

  • Vector: liveness analysis reuses storage for two tensors whose lifetimes actually overlap → silent corruption / data leak between ops.
  • Mitigation: MemoryPlan built from liveness over the executor's topological order; runtime liveness asserts (debug) verify no two simultaneously-live tensors share a Storage range; reuse only between provably non-overlapping intervals.
  • Detection: overlap assert; ASan/Miri on the planner; differential test (planned vs no-reuse) must match bit-exact.
  • Telemetry: reuse ratio, alias-assert hits, peak vs planned memory.
  • Recovery: assert → disable reuse for that buffer (fall back to fresh alloc), surface PlanError::Alias.

T7 — Integer overflow in shape / index math

  • Vector: shape product overflow (∏ dims > i64), stride computation overflow, split factor × extent overflow, byte-size numel·sizeof(dtype) overflow → undersized alloc + OOB.
  • Mitigation: checked arithmetic (checked_mul/checked_add) on ALL shape/stride/byte computations in tc-graph, tc-ir, tc-runtime; overflow = reject at the boundary, never wrap.
  • Detection: OverflowError{op,operands} at import / plan / alloc.
  • Telemetry: overflow rejection counts by site.
  • Recovery: reject model/allocation; no allocation proceeds on an overflowed size.

T8 — Supply-chain risk

  • Vector: compromised crate dependency, trojaned cc, drifting toolchain producing different/unsafe codegen.
  • Mitigation: deny.toml (cargo-deny: licenses, advisories, banned/duplicate deps); pinned MSRV 1.78; pinned cc version+hash; lockfile committed; reproducible-build target for Module artifacts (same IR + same Target ⇒ same kernel hashes).
  • Detection: cargo-deny CI gate; toolchain hash check at compile start; reproducibility diff in CI.
  • Telemetry: dep advisory hits, toolchain version logged in Module metadata.
  • Recovery: CI blocks on advisory/license/ban; mismatched toolchain refuses to compile.

T9 — Operator mistake (running an untuned / over-tuned schedule)

  • Vector: human ships a Module tuned for the wrong target, or an untuned naive schedule into a perf-critical path, or a schedule tuned past its validated tolerance regime.
  • Mitigation: the Module records its schedule provenance, Target, tune budget used, and the oracle tolerance it was validated at; tcc run/check warn on Target mismatch vs host; tcc check re-runs the oracle on the actual deployment inputs.
  • Detection: target/feature mismatch warning; tolerance metadata absent → check refuses to certify.
  • Telemetry: Module provenance (schedule hash, target, tolerance, GFLOP/s at tune time).
  • Recovery: re-tune for host target; fall back to naive PrimFunc if no validated schedule for the host.

T10 — Schedule-cache poisoning (tuned-config integrity, distinct from T8 supply-chain)

  • Vector: the on-disk ScheduleCache (.tc-cache/schedules.json, keyed by SchedKey) is attacker-writable or corrupted (shared CI cache, compromised artifact store, or a bit-flip) so tcc compile silently applies a malicious/stale ScheduleConfig — e.g. an illegal tile factor, or a schedule tuned for a different Target/shape than the one being compiled now. Distinct from T8 (which covers the cc/crate toolchain, not tuner-produced data).
  • Mitigation: every cache entry is re-validated on load — SchedKey must match (PrimFunc structure, resolved shapes, dtype, Target.arch+features) exactly (§12.2); the loaded ScheduleConfig is re-run through the same schedule legality validator (§19–20) and the per-kernel oracle check (§4.1) before it is trusted, identically to a freshly-measured candidate — a cache hit is never exempt from the correctness gate.
  • Detection: SchedKey mismatch → cache miss (safe, triggers re-tune or falls back to the analytical default schedule); legality-validator or oracle-check failure on a cache hit → CacheIntegrityError, entry evicted.
  • Telemetry: cache hit/miss counts, SchedKey mismatch rate, count of cache hits that failed post-load validation (should be 0 in a healthy cache).
  • Recovery: tcc tune --reset clears the cache; compilation always has a correct fallback (the analytical default schedule) so a poisoned cache degrades performance, never correctness.

26.4 Residual risks & assumptions

  • Trusted cc correctness assumed (a miscompiling pinned compiler is out of scope; mitigated by oracle check on output, which catches value drift but not all UB).
  • Release builds drop kernel bounds checks for performance → rely on schedule-correctness invariant + fuzz/ASan CI coverage; OOB in an un-fuzzed shape regime is a residual risk, bounded by T3 generators.
  • Side-channel / timing leakage from data-dependent control flow in the VM: out of scope (not a confidentiality system).
  • GPU surface (Device::Gpu, Scope::Shared/Thread) deferred — its codegen + driver attack surface is explicitly out of scope this phase.

26.5 Security invariants (asserted, CI-gated)

  1. No GraphNode with ty == None reaches tc-fuse (B1).
  2. Emitted C contains zero user-derived identifiers/strings (B2 static scan).
  3. Every loaded .so matches its recorded source+artifact hash and Target (B3).
  4. Every shipped schedule passed the oracle within recorded tolerance (T5).
  5. No two simultaneously-live tensors share Storage (T6).
  6. All shape/stride/byte arithmetic is checked; overflow ⇒ reject (T7).
  7. All resource caps (T1) are finite and enforced before the work they bound.

Failure Modes & Recovery

Taxonomy of failure classes across the compile/run pipeline (import → tc-graph → tc-pass → tc-fuse → layout → tc-ir → tc-schedule → tc-codegen → Module → tc-runtime/tc-vm). Each class: symptoms / root causes / detection / automatic recovery / manual recovery / operator actions / forensic artifacts / long-term mitigation. The numerics-breaking pass/schedule class (FM-3/FM-4) is the gravest: silent output drift with no crash. Governing methodology: per-IR dump + bisect-the-pass-pipeline (tcc dump graph|tir|scheduled|c|module --after <pass>), each stage re-checked vs the NumPy-style oracle.

27.1 Failure-class summary

ID Class Stage Crash? Numerics-affecting? Gravity
FM-1 Import / type error (rejected model) importer + tc-graph reject (clean) n/a low
FM-2 Shape-inference non-convergence tc-pass hang/abort n/a medium
FM-3 Pass breaks numerics tc-pass no YES (silent) critical
FM-4 Schedule changes results tc-schedule no YES (silent) critical
FM-5 Fusion changes dataflow tc-fuse no YES (silent) critical
FM-6 MemoryPlan aliasing (live overwrite) tc-runtime no/UB YES (silent/random) critical
FM-7 Codegen miscompile / cc failure tc-codegen cc-err or silent maybe high
FM-8 Kernel-ABI mismatch (KernelSig vs emitted) tc-codegen↔runtime UB/segfault YES high
FM-9 VM type/shape trap tc-vm trap (clean) n/a medium
FM-10 NaN/Inf propagation runtime/VM no YES (poison) high
FM-11 Auto-tuner picks slower/wrong schedule tc-tune no maybe medium/high

27.2 FM-1 — Import / type error (rejected model)

  • Symptoms: tcc compile exits nonzero pre-graph; "unsupported op X", "dtype Y not in {F32,F64,I32,I64,I8,Bool}", "rank mismatch", malformed textual graph / ONNX-subset proto.
  • Root causes: op outside registry; unsupported attr (dilation, group≠1); non-subset opset version; cyclic graph; missing input binding.
  • Detection: importer validates against op registry + TensorType rules before any pass runs; the verifier rejects untyped/dangling NodeId.
  • Automatic recovery: none — fail fast, no partial Graph emitted (no half-imported state).
  • Manual recovery: add op to registry + a lowering rule, or pre-rewrite model to subset; tcc dump graph on a hand-written textual equivalent to localize.
  • Operator actions: consult documented ONNX-subset support matrix; file unsupported-op ticket.
  • Forensic artifacts: importer error with (node_name, op, opset), offending line/byte offset, partial type table.
  • Long-term mitigation: version-pinned subset matrix in CI; importer fuzz corpus (FM in Phase 13); golden-reject fixtures so a once-rejected model stays a stable error.

27.3 FM-2 — Shape-inference non-convergence

  • Symptoms: shape-infer pass loops; iteration counter exceeds bound; Dim::Dynamic symbols never bound; OOM building shape constraints.
  • Root causes: contradictory constraints (broadcast of incompatible static dims); under-constrained dynamic dims with no runtime binding; a buggy op shape rule that widens the lattice every pass (non-monotone).
  • Detection: fixed-point loop has a hard iteration cap (= 4·|nodes|); monotonicity assertion — each iteration may only refine (Dynamic→Static), never re-widen; a non-monotone step aborts.
  • Automatic recovery: on cap-hit, retain last consistent partial typing; downgrade affected dims to Dim::Dynamic and defer to the VM (runtime shape resolution) instead of aborting compile.
  • Manual recovery: supply explicit input shapes; inspect the per-op shape rule via tcc dump graph --types --after shape_infer.
  • Operator actions: rerun with --static-shapes a:1x3x224x224; if dynamic intended, ensure the GraphExecutor path is disabled and VM path chosen.
  • Forensic artifacts: iteration log (dims-changed per round), the contradiction pair, the cap value.
  • Long-term mitigation: prove each op shape rule monotone over the dim lattice; property test "infer reaches fixed point ≤ cap" on random valid graphs.

27.4 FM-3 — A pass breaks numerics (GRAVEST)

The defining failure: a transform pass (const fold / CSE / DCE / algebraic simplify / layout) is semantically wrong; output drifts beyond tolerance with no crash.

  • Symptoms: tcc check reports max_abs_err/max_rel_err exceeding rtol 1e-5 / atol 1e-6 (f32); correct only with --passes none.
  • Root causes: non-associative f32 reassociation in algebraic simplify (e.g. (a+b)+c → a+(b+c) reordered reductions); const-fold using f64 host math vs f32 target rounding; DCE removing a node with a side effect on a shared buffer; layout transform with a wrong stride/permute; CSE merging two ops that differ in dtype/rounding.
  • Detection (PRIMARY METHOD): per-pass oracle gate. After every pass, tcc check --after <pass> compares the interpreted Graph IR against the reference oracle; the first pass whose output exceeds tolerance is the culprit. Automated bisect-the-pass-pipeline: binary-search the ordered pass list, toggling each, to localize to one pass.
  • Automatic recovery: PassManager runs in verified mode (CI/default-safe): any pass failing its post-condition oracle check is rolled back (its output IR discarded, pre-pass IR restored), pass disabled for that compile, warning emitted; compile proceeds with the unoptimized-but-correct IR.
  • Manual recovery: tcc dump graph --after <culprit> diff vs --before; reproduce on the minimal subgraph; fix the rewrite rule; add a fixture.
  • Operator actions: ship with --passes=safe-set (only oracle-clean passes) until fixed; never disable the post-pass gate in prod.
  • Forensic artifacts: before/after Graph IR dumps, the failing tensor + index of max error, the rewrite rule id, tolerance actual-vs-bound.
  • Long-term mitigation: every algebraic rule annotated fp-safe vs fp-reassoc; reassoc rules gated behind --fast-math, off by default; differential fuzzing (random graph → run with/without each pass → assert within tol).

27.5 FM-4 — A schedule changes results (illegal vectorize/reorder)

Compute/schedule separation invariant: a Schedule may change performance only, never the numerical result. Violation = scheduling bug.

  • Symptoms: scheduled Kernel output ≠ naive PrimFunc output; error appears only at certain tile/vector factors.
  • Root causes: reorder moving a reduce axis outside its consumer (illegal loop interchange across a IterKind::Reduce carried dependence); vectorize on an axis carrying a true dependence or non-unit stride; split factor not dividing extent without a guard (tail not handled); parallel over an axis with a cross-iteration accumulation (data race); compute_at/inline violating producer-consumer liveness.
  • Detection: schedule legality validator before codegen — dependence test per primitive: vectorize requires lane-independent + unit-stride access; reorder requires the permutation preserve all carried dependences; parallel requires no write-write/read-write conflict across the partition. Plus the per-kernel oracle: scheduled-vs-naive within tol.
  • Automatic recovery: illegal primitive rejected at schedule-apply time (legality check fails → primitive not applied, falls back to prior loop nest); on a post-codegen oracle miss the tuner discards that schedule point and reverts to the default naive schedule (guaranteed correct).
  • Manual recovery: tcc dump scheduled --after <primitive> + tcc dump tir; compare loop nest dependence; re-derive legal factors (divisor of extent).
  • Operator actions: --schedule=naive to bypass all primitives; report the offending (primitive, AxisId, factor).
  • Forensic artifacts: the Stage/axis list, applied primitive trace, the dependence-test verdict, tail-loop presence flag.
  • Long-term mitigation: never expose an unguarded split (always emit a tail/predicated remainder); legality test is mandatory and unconditional; fuzz the schedule generator (random legal primitive sequences must stay within tol — Phase 13).

27.6 FM-5 — Fusion changes dataflow

  • Symptoms: fused-kernel output ≠ unfused; wrong only when a node has multiple consumers.
  • Root causes: fusing a node whose intermediate is also externally consumed (fusion drops the materialized output); fusing across a reduce that two consumers share; dominator analysis grouping a non-dominated node (incorrect dominator-tree edge); fusing ops with incompatible layouts without an inserted convert.
  • Detection: post-fusion verifier — every FusionGroup must (a) have a single live external output OR re-expose all externally-consumed intermediates; (b) respect the dominator constraint (fused producer dominated by group root); plus per-group oracle vs the unfused subgraph.
  • Automatic recovery: verifier rejects an illegal group → split it back into singleton groups (no fusion = correct, slower); re-run lowering on the split groups.
  • Manual recovery: tcc dump graph --after fuse to view group boundaries; inspect consumer counts; fix the fusion rule (elementwise vs reduce/inject eligibility).
  • Operator actions: --no-fuse for a correct baseline; compare GFLOP/s loss.
  • Forensic artifacts: group membership, dominator tree edges, external-consumer set per node.
  • Long-term mitigation: fusion rule explicitly bars fusing a multi-consumer intermediate unless re-exposed; property test "fuse then lower ≡ unfused lower" over random elementwise+reduce chains.

27.7 FM-6 — MemoryPlan aliasing (live tensors overwrite each other)

  • Symptoms: nondeterministic wrong outputs, run-to-run variance, errors that vanish under a debugger/ASAN; failure scales with reuse aggressiveness.
  • Root causes: liveness analysis under-computes a tensor's live range (last-use computed before its true last read); two overlapping-lifetime tensors assigned the same Storage; in-place op aliasing input==output where the op reads after write; missing edge in the executor topo order.
  • Detection: MemoryPlan interval-overlap assertion — for any two tensors sharing storage, their [def, last_use] intervals must be disjoint; an ASAN/Miri run over the GraphExecutor in CI; a "paint each buffer with a poison pattern on free" debug mode that surfaces stale reads.
  • Automatic recovery: on assertion failure, disable reuse (every tensor gets a fresh Storage) — correct, higher memory; emit warning with the conflicting pair.
  • Manual recovery: tcc dump module --memory-plan shows the storage-sharing map + live intervals; recompute liveness over the executor's actual topo order.
  • Operator actions: --no-mem-reuse as the safe fallback; check peak-memory regression.
  • Forensic artifacts: the liveness intervals, storage-assignment table, the overlapping (NodeId, NodeId) + shared Storage id.
  • Long-term mitigation: liveness over the exact dispatch order used by GraphExecutor; in-place ops require an explicit "may-alias" op attribute checked by the planner; randomized poison-fill in debug builds; differential run reuse-on vs reuse-off in conformance.

27.8 FM-7 — Codegen miscompile / cc failure

  • Symptoms: cc nonzero exit (syntax error in emitted C); or compiles but kernel result wrong; or undefined-behavior segfault in the kernel.
  • Root causes: CBackend emitting invalid C (bad index expr, type mismatch); SimdBackend intrinsic for an unavailable Target.feature; integer overflow in computed index; off-by-one in emitted loop bound; missing restrict/aliasing assumption violated.
  • Detection: capture cc stderr; compile emitted C with -Wall -Werror; UBSan-instrumented kernel build in CI; per-kernel oracle after compile.
  • Automatic recovery: SIMD feature unsupported → fall back to CBackend scalar path; cc failure → retry with conservative flags (no -ffast-math, no -march); persistent failure aborts that kernel and marks the Module incomplete.
  • Manual recovery: tcc dump c --kernel <name> to read the emitted source; minimize the loop nest; reproduce by compiling the dumped .c manually.
  • Operator actions: force scalar via --backend=c; pin --target arch=x86_64,features= (empty) to disable SIMD.
  • Forensic artifacts: emitted .c source, cc command line + stderr, the KernelSig, target features. Example emitted scalar add kernel ABI (OUTPUT artifact — signature only):
Param Type Role
out float* restrict output buffer
a, b const float* restrict input buffers
n int64_t element count

Stable KernelSig ABI: void add_k0(float* restrict out, const float* restrict a, const float* restrict b, int64_t n);

  • Long-term mitigation: emitted-C golden snapshots per kernel; UBSan+ASan kernel CI lane; SIMD path always shadowed by a scalar fallback proven equal in conformance.

27.9 FM-8 — Kernel-ABI mismatch (KernelSig vs emitted signature)

  • Symptoms: segfault/garbage on first call; arg count/order/type disagreement between runtime call site and emitted C.
  • Root causes: KernelSig (pointers + shapes + strides) drifting from the emitted function signature (extra stride arg, swapped shape/stride order, i32 vs i64 extent); ABI change in emitter not reflected in the dispatch path; packed-vs-unpacked args.
  • Detection: the Kernel carries a structured KernelSig; the GraphExecutor/Interpreter dispatch is generated from the same KernelSig (single source of truth) — a build-time assertion that emitter and caller share it; a per-kernel "ABI canary" call with sentinel args in CI.
  • Automatic recovery: none at runtime (UB) — prevented at build time; mismatch is a hard compile error, not a fallback.
  • Manual recovery: diff tcc dump c signature vs the KernelSig struct; align the emitter template.
  • Operator actions: rebuild Module after any emitter ABI change; never mix Modules across emitter versions.
  • Forensic artifacts: the KernelSig (arg list, dtypes, ptr/shape/stride layout), the emitted C prototype, the runtime call-site arg vector.
  • Long-term mitigation: generate caller + callee from one KernelSig definition; embed an ABI/emitter version in the Module header, refuse to load on mismatch.

27.10 FM-9 — VM type/shape trap (tc-vm)

  • Symptoms: Interpreter halts with a typed trap; AllocTensor/InvokeKernel dynamic-shape resolution fails.
  • Root causes: runtime shape inconsistent with kernel's static assumptions; If predicate not Bool/scalar; Call arg register holds wrong dtype; InvokeKernel register count ≠ KernelSig arity; uninitialized register read.
  • Detection: per-instruction guards — register dtype/shape checked before InvokeKernel; ShapeRef resolved + validated against the kernel's dynamic-dim constraints; control-flow predicate type-checked.
  • Automatic recovery: clean trap (no UB) — VM aborts the call frame, unwinds the stack, returns a typed error; partial outputs not committed.
  • Manual recovery: inspect VmFunc instruction trace + register file snapshot at trap PC.
  • Operator actions: bind concrete dynamic dims; verify the model was compiled for the VM (dynamic) path, not the static GraphExecutor path.
  • Forensic artifacts: trap PC, instruction, register file dump (NDArray shapes/dtypes), call stack of VmFunc frames.
  • Long-term mitigation: VM verifier pass over VmInstruction stream pre-execution (register def-use, arity, type lattice); fuzz dynamic-shape inputs against the static-shape oracle.

27.11 FM-10 — NaN/Inf propagation

  • Symptoms: output all/partly NaN/Inf; oracle check fails as not-finite; often originates in one upstream op (div, log, exp overflow).
  • Root causes: genuine input pathology; uninitialized buffer read (links to FM-6); overflow in f32 reduce; const fold producing Inf folded into the graph.
  • Detection: runtime finite-check probes (debug/--check-finite) after each kernel; the oracle comparator treats NaN/Inf as a hard mismatch (no tolerance applies); first-not-finite kernel pinpointed.
  • Automatic recovery: none numerically (NaN is data) — but --check-finite localizes by halting at the first non-finite producer; reuse-aliasing as a cause is ruled out by the FM-6 poison mode.
  • Manual recovery: bisect kernels with finite-probe enabled; dump the offending kernel's inputs.
  • Operator actions: validate input ranges; enable --check-finite in suspect runs.
  • Forensic artifacts: the first non-finite tensor + index, producing kernel name, its input min/max.
  • Long-term mitigation: finite-check lane in conformance; numerically-stable op formulations (log-sum-exp) documented per op; distinguish "garbage NaN" (uninit/aliasing bug) from "data NaN" (genuine) via the poison-fill signature.

27.12 FM-11 — Auto-tuner selects a slower-or-wrong schedule

  • Symptoms: tuned Module slower than --schedule=naive, or (worse) wrong; tune run picks a schedule that fails the per-kernel oracle.
  • Root causes: cost model mis-ranks (analytical model diverges from measured); noisy measurement (thermal/contention) misleads search; the search proposes an illegal schedule (caught by FM-4 legality) but the tuner's fallback path is wrong; overfitting to one input shape.
  • Detection: tuner gates every candidate through the schedule legality validator AND the per-kernel oracle before scoring — a candidate failing either is scored −∞ (never selected); a final guard requires the chosen schedule's measured GFLOP/s ≥ the naive baseline, else keep naive.
  • Automatic recovery: no correct+faster candidate found → emit the default naive schedule (correct, baseline perf); selection never regresses below baseline by construction.
  • Manual recovery: tcc tune --dump-trials to inspect candidate scores; re-tune with more measurement repeats / fixed CPU affinity.
  • Operator actions: pin tuning host (no contention); set measurement repeats ≥ 5, take median; cache tuned schedules per (kernel, shape, target).
  • Forensic artifacts: trial log (schedule, predicted vs measured GFLOP/s, legality verdict, oracle verdict), chosen schedule, baseline.
  • Long-term mitigation: measured cost always overrides analytical for the final pick; correctness gate is unconditional; record measurement variance and reject if CV > threshold.

27.13 Cross-cutting debugging methodology (binding)

  1. Per-IR dump at every boundary: tcc dump graph|tir|scheduled|c|module [--after <pass|primitive>]. Every stage is independently oracle-checkable.
  2. Bisect the pass pipeline: binary-search the ordered PassManager list (toggle passes) to localize a numerics break (FM-3) to a single pass; analogously bisect schedule primitives for FM-4.
  3. Layered oracle gates: Graph-IR-interpret vs oracle (catches FM-3/5); scheduled-vs-naive per kernel (FM-4); reuse-on vs reuse-off (FM-6); SIMD vs scalar (FM-7); VM vs static (FM-9); finite-check (FM-10).
  4. Safe fallbacks form a correctness ladder (each strictly correct, decreasing perf): tuned → default schedule (--schedule=naive) → no fusion (--no-fuse) → no mem-reuse (--no-mem-reuse) → scalar backend (--backend=c) → no passes (--passes=none). Descend the ladder until the bug disappears to localize the class.
  5. Invariant restated: passes and schedules change performance, never numerics — any violation is a bug gated automatically in verified mode and rolled back, never shipped silently.

Operational Readiness

Framing: tcc is a compiler + runtime crate set + CLI, not a long-running service. "Operational readiness" = reproducible builds, deterministic numerics, observable compile/run stages, and runbooks for the three failure classes (wrong output, slow kernel, OOM). No daemon, no listening socket, no uptime SLO — instead: tolerance SLO, compile-time budget SLO, no-crash SLO.

28.1 Philosophy: dumps + diagnostics, not logging

  • The toolchain returns structured diagnostics + IR dumps; it does not emit ambient logs to stderr during normal operation. A compiler that logs is noise; a compiler that dumps the IR at each stage is debuggable.
  • tcc surfaces state through three channels, all deterministic and diffable:
    1. Per-stage IR dumps (tcc dump <stage>): graph | tir | scheduled | c | module.
    2. Per-pass timing log (machine-readable JSON/CSV): pass name → wall-ns, nodes-in/out.
    3. Oracle-diff report (tcc check): per-tensor max-abs / max-rel error vs the NumPy-style reference oracle.
  • RUST_LOG-style tracing exists only behind --verbose (a tc-cli flag wiring a tracing subscriber); off by default. Library crates (tc-graph … tc-vm) never write to stdout/stderr — they return Result<_, Diagnostic>.
  • All dumps are byte-stable for a fixed input + version + seed → enables golden-file diffing in CI and git diff of IR across commits.

28.2 Metrics exposed

Emitted by tcc compile/run/tune --metrics out.json. One row = one fact; all quantified.

Metric Source crate Definition Target
compile_ms_total tc-cli import → Module wall time < 2 s for a 50-op graph (debug cc -O2)
pass_ms[name] tc-pass per-pass wall-ns (shape/fold/CSE/DCE/simplify/layout) each < 200 ms @ 50 ops
fusion_count tc-fuse # FusionGroups produced reported; > 1 expected
fusion_ratio tc-fuse fused ops / total ops ≥ 0.6 on the elementwise corpus
materialization_saved tc-fuse intermediate tensors eliminated by fusion (bytes) reported; ≥ 30% of naive
kernels_emitted tc-codegen # Kernel in Module == fusion_count
peak_runtime_bytes tc-runtime max live Storage during GraphExecutor run reported
memplan_reuse_ratio tc-runtime 1 − (planned bytes / sum-of-tensor bytes) ≥ 0.4 on linear chains
kernel_gflops[name] tc-runtime measured FLOPs / kernel wall-time matmul ≥ 30% of a naive triple-loop baseline ×10; SIMD path ≥ 4× scalar
dispatch_overhead_ns tc-runtime per-op executor overhead (alloc+bind+call) excl. kernel < 500 ns/op
vm_dispatch_ns tc-vm per-VmInstruction interpreter overhead < 200 ns/instr
tuner_trials tc-schedule candidate schedules measured bounded by --trials (default 256)
tuner_best_gflops tc-schedule best measured schedule perf ≥ analytical-only baseline
oracle_max_relerr tc-cli worst per-element rel error vs oracle ≤ rtol 1e-5 (f32)

GFLOP/s + roofline context: report measured GFLOP/s alongside the machine's scalar peak and (where known) achievable arithmetic intensity, so a "slow kernel" is judged against roofline, not in a vacuum.

28.3 Structured tracing via IR dumps

Pipeline observability = the ability to dump every intermediate representation:

tcc dump graph     model.txt   # tc-graph Graph IR (post-import, pre-pass)
tcc dump graph     --after=fuse # IR after a named pass/stage
tcc dump tir       model.txt   # tc-ir PrimFunc per fused group (pre-schedule)
tcc dump scheduled model.txt   # PrimFunc after Schedule primitives applied
tcc dump c         model.txt   # emitted C kernel source (CBackend/SimdBackend)
tcc dump module    model.txt   # Module: KernelSigs + MemoryPlan + graph summary
  • --after=<pass|stage> snapshots IR at any pipeline point → enables pass-by-pass bisection (§28.6).
  • Each dump carries a header: tool version, target, seed, input hash → reproducibility provenance.
  • Timing log (--metrics) and oracle-diff (tcc check --report) are the other two structured streams. The oracle-diff report lists, per graph output: shape, dtype, max-abs-err, max-rel-err, first-failing-index, pass/fail vs tolerance.

28.4 Experiment-harness "dashboard"

No live dashboard (no service). Instead a batch experiment harness (tcc bench --corpus … --csv runs.csv) feeding offline plots:

  • Compile-time breakdown: stacked bar of pass_ms[*] + lower + codegen + cc invoke, per model.
  • Kernel performance: GFLOP/s per Kernel vs scalar baseline and vs roofline ceiling; SIMD-vs-scalar speedup.
  • Memory: peak_runtime_bytes and memplan_reuse_ratio per model; allocator high-water timeline.
  • Tuning curve: best GFLOP/s vs tuner_trials (convergence), per fused kernel.
  • Plots are regenerated from CSV by a checked-in script; the harness output is the artifact, not a server.

28.5 SLO-style targets

SLO Target Measured by Enforcement
Numerical correctness f32 rtol ≤ 1e-5, atol ≤ 1e-6; f64 rtol ≤ 1e-12 vs oracle tcc check CI gate; release-blocking
Schedule invariance any Schedule change Δresult ≤ tolerance (perf-only) differential test (Phase 13) CI gate
Compile-time budget < 2 s @ 50 ops; pass pipeline < 1 s --metrics CI perf regression alert > 20%
No-crash fuzzed graph + schedule generators never panic/UB Phase 13 fuzzing CI gate
Perf floor matmul/conv kernel ≥ documented GFLOP/s floor; no regression > 10% tcc bench CI perf gate

Tolerance is the correctness oracle: a "passing" build must satisfy the numerical SLO on the full op + model corpus.

28.6 Incident response + runbooks

RB-1 — "Output is wrong" (oracle-diff fails).

  1. tcc check --report → identify which graph output + first-failing index.
  2. Bisect the pass pipeline: rerun with --disable-pass=<name> one at a time; the pass whose removal restores correctness is the culprit (most often simplify/layout/fuse).
  3. At the failing stage, tcc dump <stage> --after=<pass> and diff IR vs the previous stage; confirm the transform preserves semantics.
  4. For a fused kernel, dump tir + scheduled + c; re-run the same compute with an identity schedule (no primitives) — if that passes, the bug is in a Schedule primitive, not compute (compute/schedule separation localizes it).
  5. Compare the single Kernel output to the oracle on the fused subgraph alone.

RB-2 — "Kernel is slow" (GFLOP/s below floor).

  1. tcc dump scheduled <kernel> → inspect loop nest: is the hot axis vectorized? is an outer axis parallel? is tiling sized to L1/L2?
  2. tcc dump c <kernel> → confirm SimdBackend emitted SIMD intrinsics on the vectorized axis (not scalar fallback); check inner loop is unrolled and stride-1.
  3. Roofline check: compute arithmetic intensity; if memory-bound, fusion (reduce materialization) is the lever, not vectorize.
  4. Re-tune: tcc tune --trials N <kernel> and compare tuner_best_gflops; inspect the chosen Schedule.

RB-3 — "OOM at runtime."

  1. tcc dump module → inspect the MemoryPlan: which tensors share Storage, total planned bytes, memplan_reuse_ratio.
  2. Compare peak_runtime_bytes to expected; a low reuse ratio means liveness analysis failed to coalesce — check for a long-lived tensor pinned across the whole graph (a missing DCE, or an output kept live).
  3. For the VM path, inspect dynamic AllocTensor sites; dynamic shapes defeat static planning → expect higher peak.
  4. Fallback: cap allocator arena; confirm Allocator::free is reached (no leaked Storage refcount).

RB-4 — "Compile too slow." Inspect pass_ms[*]; a runaway pass is usually shape-inference not reaching fixed point or CSE hashing blowup. Bisect with --disable-pass.

28.7 Deployment: crates, artifact, caches

  • Crates: the nine tc-* crates publish under semver; tc-graph/tc-ir are the stable public IR surface — IR enum changes (GraphOp, Stmt, Expr) are breaking (major bump). tcc binary versioned with the workspace.
  • Module artifact format: Module { kernels, graph, plan } serialized with a versioned header { magic, format_version, tool_version, target }. Kernels store emitted C source + KernelSig (so a Module is reproducible/recompilable). Loader rejects mismatched format_version; refuses to run a Module whose Target ≠ host arch/features unless --force.
  • Schedule / tuning cache lifecycle: tuner results keyed by (kernel structure hash, Target, tool_version) → JSON cache. On compile, a cache hit reuses the best Schedule (skips search); a miss triggers tuning or falls back to the analytical default schedule. Cache is invalidated on tool-version or Target change; tcc tune --reset clears it. Cache is advisory: a stale/missing cache never affects correctness, only compile time + perf.
  • Reproducibility: fixed seed + version + Target ⇒ byte-identical dumps and identical Module → golden-file CI; cross-platform numerics validated to tolerance, not bit-equality (FMA/SIMD reassociation differences are within rtol).

Compatibility & Versioning

Public surfaces, versioning policy, and the contracts a downstream consumer may rely on. "Stable" = covered by semver below; everything else is explicitly internal and may change in any release.

Public surface area (the only things under semver)

Surface Owner Stability tier Breaking change requires
tcc CLI (subcommands, flags, exit codes) tc-cli Stable major bump + deprecation cycle
Textual graph format (.tcg) tc-graph Stable format-version bump
ONNX-subset importer (opset matrix) tc-graph Stable, additive opset re-pin = minor
Module artifact format (.tcm) tc-codegen Stable, versioned module-version bump
KernelSig ABI (C calling convention) tc-codegen Stable abi-version bump
Dump formats (graph/tir/scheduled/c/module) tc-cli Stable for --machine; human dumps best-effort machine schema bump
Numerical-tolerance contract tc-runtime / conformance Stable, documented event published change note
Rust crate APIs (tc-* pub items) all crates Per-crate semver major bump
Internal IR node layout, arena ids, pass ordering tc-pass/tc-ir Unstable none

Crate semver (the nine tc-* crates)

  • Independent versions; workspace-wide compat enforced by a tc-version const re-exported from every crate. All tc-* in one build must share the same MAJOR.MINOR; mismatched patch is allowed.
  • pub API breakage (renamed/removed type, changed method signature, changed enum variant set on a #[non_exhaustive]-absent enum) → MAJOR.
  • Core IR enums (GraphOp, Stmt, Expr, Device, DType, VmInstruction) are #[non_exhaustive] so adding a variant (new op, new instruction) is MINOR, not MAJOR.
  • New pub fn, new schedule primitive on Schedule, new Target feature string → MINOR.
  • Pre-1.0: 0.MINOR.PATCH, MINOR carries breaking changes; the #[non_exhaustive] + version-const rules still hold.

Supported-op matrix (additive growth)

  • A versioned table OPS.md lists each GraphOp (and importer-mapped ONNX op) × {dtypes, rank constraints, attributes, fusion-eligible, codegen path}.
  • Additive guarantee: ops are only added or have constraints widened within a major line; never narrowed or removed. Removing/narrowing an op = MAJOR + entry moved to a Removed appendix.
  • An op marked Experimental in OPS.md is exempt from additive stability until promoted to Stable; tcc warns when an experimental op is used.

Module artifact format (.tcm)

Module is self-describing and refuses to load on an incompatible runtime.

TcmHeader {
  magic:        "TCM\0",
  module_version: u16,        // bumped on layout/semantics change
  compiler_version: SemVer,   // tc-version that emitted it
  abi_version:  u16,          // KernelSig ABI generation
  target:       Target,       // arch + feature list (e.g. x86_64 + [avx2])
  schedule_digest: u64,       // hash of applied schedule per kernel (provenance)
  tolerance:    ToleranceSpec,// rtol/atol used at conformance time
  oracle_pin:   u32,          // reference-oracle corpus revision
}

Load policy (checked by tc-runtime before binding kernels):

Field mismatch Action
magic wrong reject: not a Module
module_version > runtime max reject: "module too new"
module_version < runtime min-supported reject: "module too old, recompile"
compiler_version MAJOR ≠ runtime reject
abi_version ≠ runtime reject: KernelSig ABI mismatch
target features ⊄ host CPU features reject: missing CPU feature (e.g. AVX2)
tolerance differs from request warn; honor module-recorded value
  • Forward compat is not promised: an older runtime never loads a newer module_version. Backward compat (newer runtime loads older Module within the same MAJOR) is promised down to a published min-supported floor.

KernelSig ABI

  • The C calling convention for every emitted Kernel is fixed per abi_version: void kernel(void** buffers, const int64_t* shapes, const int64_t* strides, void* env).
  • Buffer order, the (ptr, shape-span, stride-span) packing, and DType→C-type mapping (F32→float, I64→int64_t, Bool→uint8_t) are ABI-frozen.
  • Any change to argument order/packing or the dtype map = abi_version bump → all older .tcm rejected.
  • Adding the env opaque pointer's contents (thread-pool handle, error sink) is allowed without a bump as long as the pointer slot already exists.

Dump-format stability

  • tcc dump <stage> --machine emits a stable, schema-versioned JSON per IR stage (graph, tir, scheduled, c, module); each carries a schema_version. Consumed by tests/tooling → covered by semver.
  • Human-readable dumps (no --machine) are best-effort, unstable — for eyeballing, not parsing.
  • The c dump is the exact source handed to cc; its text is unstable (formatting, temp names) but its numerical behavior under the tolerance contract is stable.

ONNX-subset import

  • Tracks one pinned opset per release (recorded in OPS.md and stamped into Module.oracle_pin's sibling field). Default pin documented; re-pinning to a newer opset = MINOR (additive) provided no previously-imported model changes semantics.
  • Unsupported op in an input model → hard error naming the op + opset, never a silent skip or approximation.

Numerical-tolerance contract (public guarantee)

  • Default tolerances are part of the public API: f32 rtol=1e-5, atol=1e-6; f64 rtol=1e-12, atol=1e-14. Every conformance pass and tcc check asserts compiled output is within these of the NumPy-style reference oracle.
  • Invariant: a schedule change must never move output by more than the tolerance (compute/schedule separation) — only performance changes. A schedule that violates this is a bug, not an allowed variation.
  • Tightening or loosening a default tolerance is a documented event: published in the changelog with rationale, affects ToleranceSpec in new Modules, and is treated as a MINOR (loosen) or potentially-breaking (tighten, since previously-passing modules may regress) change.

Float-reassociation & bit-exactness policy

  • Determinism guarantee: for a fixed {Module, target, input}, output is bit-identical run-to-run (no nondeterministic reduction ordering; parallel reductions use a fixed tree/tiling order independent of thread count).
  • Reassociation is permitted by the compiler (e.g. tiled/SIMD reduction reorders sums) and so the result is not bit-identical to the naive scalar oracle — only within-tolerance. This is the contract: tolerance-equal, not bit-equal, across schedule/target.
  • A codegen change that alters bit-exact output (new SIMD reduction tree, FMA contraction toggle, different tiling default) is versioned and communicated: it bumps the schedule_digest provenance and, if it could shift results near the tolerance boundary, is called out in release notes as a "numerical-behavior change."
  • --strict-fp flag (and a Target feature) disables reassociation/FMA-contraction for users needing oracle-bit-reproducibility, at a performance cost.

Differential parity guarantee

  • Every Stable op + every shipped model in the corpus is differentially tested vs the pinned reference oracle (oracle_pin revision) on each release; CI fails on any out-of-tolerance divergence.
  • The oracle revision is frozen per release; bumping it is a documented event since it can change the reference values a Module is validated against.
  • Fuzz-generated graphs and schedules must (a) not crash and (b) stay within tolerance — these are parity invariants, not just smoke tests.

MSRV policy

  • MSRV = Rust 1.78 (edition 2021). Raising MSRV is a MINOR bump, announced one release ahead, never in a patch.
  • The optional LLVM-codegen feature may carry a higher toolchain requirement; the default C-source + portable-SIMD path holds the stated MSRV.

Explicit non-guarantees

  • Arena NodeId/BufferId/VarId numbering, pass execution order, internal PrimFunc structure, MemoryPlan reuse decisions, and emitted-C variable names — all unstable, may change any release.
  • GPU/Device::Gpu, Scope::Shared, and IterKind::Thread are deferred; no compatibility is promised for these placeholder surfaces until the GPU phase ships.
  • Performance numbers (GFLOP/s, % of peak) are targets, not contracts; tuner-selected schedules may differ across releases as the cost model improves (always within the tolerance contract).

Testing & Verification Strategy
